# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file. The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

## [3.0.0](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/compare/v2.2.0...v3.0.0) (2026-10-04)

SKF 3.0.0 is safe to use in a skills folder shared with other skills: it moves, updates, renames and deletes only the skills it generated. Update Skill now reads a remote source at the exact commit the skill tracks and writes each update into a new version folder, and every provenance entry is labeled by the tool that read it and checked against the line it cites. Read Breaking changes before you upgrade: SKF now requires Python 3.11, the Setup result envelope no longer lists the `write_failure` status, `npm install bmad-module-skill-forge@rc` and `@alpha` stop resolving once 3.0.0 is published, and some runs now stop with a message that says what to run. Those are runs on a skill still in the old flat layout (Create Skill, Quick Skill, Stack Skill, and Update Skill with `--detect-only` or `--dry-run`), Drop Skill and Rename Skill on a skill folder that also holds files SKF did not write, Drop Skill on a never-exported skill without `--mode purge`, Update Skill of a remote skill when it cannot read the commit it tracks, Update Skill with `--allow-workspace-drift` when a gap needs a line or a signature from the pinned commit (as every new or changed export does) or a recount of the public API there (as a removed export does), a second Update Skill of a skill whose run lock another update holds (`halted-for-concurrent-run`), and Update Skill with `--from-test-report`, `--dry-run` included, when a gap rated Critical or High, or with no severity it recognizes, names no source line and no file to read for an export it has no provenance for.

### Breaking changes

- **all workflows, skf-forger:** Every workflow now runs BMad core's customization resolver through `uv run`, with `--project-root`, instead of a bare `python3`. Customization overrides in `_bmad/custom/<skill>.toml` and `_bmad/custom/<skill>.user.toml` therefore start to apply where the resolver used to fail silently (a bare `python3` missing or older than 3.11), so a setting such as `forbid_purge_in_headless` now takes effect. When the resolver still cannot run, for example in a project without the BMAD Method installer's customization script, the run prints `[activation/warn] customization_resolver_unavailable: <reason>`, uses the bundled `customize.toml` alone, and reports `customization_resolver_unavailable: <reason>` among the run's warnings: in the result envelope and result file, or in Campaign's decision log. Ferris (`skf-forger`) runs the same resolver with `--key agent`, so `_bmad/custom/skf-forger.toml` and `_bmad/custom/skf-forger.user.toml` can give him `activation_steps_prepend`, `persistent_facts` and `activation_steps_append` (his roster values stay as shipped); when it cannot run he opens his greeting with the same warning line. ([#650](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/650), [#652](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/652); issue [#595](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/595))

  **Migration:** Review `_bmad/custom/<skill>.toml` and `_bmad/custom/<skill>.user.toml`, since their settings now apply, and install `uv` if runs warn `customization_resolver_unavailable`.

- **packaging:** The npm dist-tags `rc` and `alpha`, which still point at `1.0.0-rc.3` and `0.10.1-alpha.0`, are removed once 3.0.0 is published, so `npm install bmad-module-skill-forge@rc`, `npm install bmad-module-skill-forge@alpha` and `npx bmad-module-skill-forge@rc` stop resolving instead of installing a build two majors older than `latest`. From now on `latest` is the only permanent dist-tag: `alpha`, `beta` and `rc` exist only while a prerelease line is open, and the stable release that closes the line removes its tag. ([#636](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/636); issue [#568](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/568))

  **Migration:** Install `bmad-module-skill-forge@latest`, or an exact version such as `bmad-module-skill-forge@3.0.0`, instead of `bmad-module-skill-forge@rc` or `bmad-module-skill-forge@alpha`, and change any script or pipeline that names `@rc` or `@alpha` the same way.

- **requirements:** SKF now requires Python 3.11 or newer; Python 3.10 is no longer supported. What SKF needs did not change: in v2.2.0 the helpers that read package manifests (used by Quick Skill, Brief Skill, Analyze Source and Stack Skill) already needed 3.11, and Quick Skill runs one of them with a bare `python3`, so its export extractor already crashed on 3.10. The documented minimum now says so. ([#522](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/522))

  **Migration:** Run `python3 --version`. If it prints 3.10 or older, install Python 3.11 or newer and make it the `python3` on your `PATH`; nothing else needs to change.

- **skf-analyze-source:** Analyze Source writes every `skill-brief.yaml`, on the `[auto]` path and in the interactive step alike, through `skf-write-skill-brief.py`, and outside the documentation-URL branch checks it with `skf-validate-brief-schema.py`, the check Brief Skill runs when it reads a brief, before any brief reaches the forge data folder. A brief either one rejects now stops the run with exit `4` (`write-failed`) and an error envelope, where `AN[auto]` used to write it, report success and leave `BS[auto]` to halt one stage later (a whole-language caveat holding an apostrophe was enough). `brief_paths` therefore lists only briefs that passed these checks. Exit `4` with `write-failed` at the end of a run now means that the shared emitter refused the result payload twice: a result file that cannot be written leaves `result_path` null and a `result_file_write_failed` warning, and the run finishes with exit `0`. The auto path also reads the target's whole file list from one file, so shape detection sees every grammar file and compiler folder. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issue [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592))

  **Migration:** Re-run `@Ferris AN[auto] <target>` (or the `forge-auto` pipeline) on a target whose run stops with `write-failed`: the envelope's `error.reason` names the brief field the writer or the schema rejected. A pipeline that treated exit `4` as a missing result file now checks the envelope's `warnings` for `result_file_write_failed` before it reads `analyze-source-result-latest.json`.

- **skf-analyze-source:** Analyze Source drops two customization settings that could not do what they offered. `brief_schema_path` pointed the analysis at a copy of the brief contract, but the brief writer and the schema gate check every brief against the bundled schema, so a house-style copy either changed nothing or produced briefs the gate rejects. `unit_detection_heuristics_path` pointed it at a copy of the unit detection rules, but the size limits, the generated-file rules and the unit names come from the shared helpers, so a copy changed only some judgments. Both settings are now ignored when an override sets them. `analysis_report_template_path` now ships its real default, `templates/analysis-report-template.md`, and the comments of `customize.toml` say when the activation hooks run and that the `on_complete` command receives `--result-path` with the latest result file and never fails the run. ([#637](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/637); issue [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596))

  **Migration:** Remove `brief_schema_path` and `unit_detection_heuristics_path` from `_bmad/custom/skf-analyze-source.toml` and `_bmad/custom/skf-analyze-source.user.toml`. Rules your copy of the heuristics added for the analysis to keep in mind go into `persistent_facts` instead.

- **skf-analyze-source:** When no unit is confirmed (every unit rejected, or every boundary dropped in a headless run), Analyze Source now ends like any other run: it writes `analyze-source-result-<YYYYMMDD-HHmmss>.json`, replaces `analyze-source-result-latest.json` with that record, which lists no brief, prints `SKF_ANALYZE_RESULT_JSON` with `brief_paths: []` and `unit_counts.confirmed: 0` (which the forger's AN circuit breaker reads as no skillable unit), and runs the `on_complete` hook and the health check. Before, it stopped with no envelope, ran no hook and left an earlier run's `analyze-source-result-latest.json` in place. The `on_complete` hook now also runs when an `[auto]` run ends with a coexistence `redirect` or `skipped` status, which before wrote its record without calling the hook. Every envelope now comes from the shared emitter and follows the new schema `skf-analyze-result-envelope.v1.json`: each one gains `result_path` (the per-run result file, null on a halt), a halt's gains `error` with the step it stopped at (`phase`) and its message, a redirect or a skip carries `report_path`, `brief_paths` and `unit_counts` too, and `headless_decisions` lists the `[auto]` merge-or-split decision and the choice of the documented language when a run made them. Every HARD HALT of a headless run prints its envelope, from activation on. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issues [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585), [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))

  **Migration:** A hook or pipeline that reads `analyze-source-result-latest.json` (the `on_complete` hook's `--result-path`) as a run that wrote briefs checks the record first: its `status` (`redirect` and `skipped` write no brief) and whether its `outputs` hold an entry of type `brief`.

- **skf-audit-skill:** Audit Skill no longer offers degraded mode, which had no baseline to compare against and could not finish: the `degraded` input, the degraded-mode gate and its `init.degraded-mode` entry in the envelope's `headless_decisions` are gone, and so is the report's Mode row (`confidence_mode`). A skill with no provenance map, such as a Quick Skill, now stops with the new halt reason `no-baseline` (exit `3`) in every mode, and names Test Skill and Create Skill; before, a headless run stopped there as `user-cancelled` (exit `6`). A docs-only skill is now audited by its documents: the audit compares each document's hash with the one recorded at compile time, grades each changed document HIGH (the new `--doc-drift` input of `skf-severity-classify.py`), so the score is `SIGNIFICANT` and `next_workflow` is `update-skill`, or `CLEAN` when nothing changed. A docs-only skill that records no document hash stops with `no-baseline`, and one none of whose documents could be compared (offline, for example) stops with the halt reason `source-unreadable` (exit `3`); when only some could not be compared, the envelope's `warnings` carry `doc_fetch_failed` or `doc_not_hashed` with their counts. A stack built from code is now audited, from the project root it was scanned from and library by library, where it stopped at `source-dir-missing`. Audit Skill now checks `uv` and its shared helpers before the first question and stops with `helper-missing` when one is missing, so it no longer falls back to diffs, grades or hashes made by hand; a skill-inventory helper that is missing or crashes is now `helper-missing` instead of `skill-not-found`, and a missing script and asset hash helper skips that check with a note instead of halting. The `severity_rules_path` setting is removed: `skf-severity-classify.py` grades every finding by its own rule table, so the setting changed grades only in the by-hand fallback, which is gone. `drift_report_template_path` now names its default, `assets/drift-report-template.md`, and the comments of `customize.toml` say to keep a template copy outside the skill folder, named from `{project-root}`, what the copy must keep, and that `on_complete` is a shell command run from the project root once a finished audit's result file is written. ([#640](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/640); issues [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594), [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596), [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599), [#600](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/600), [#589](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/589))

  **Migration:** Drop `degraded` from audit-skill invocations and `severity_rules_path` from `_bmad/custom/skf-audit-skill.toml` and `skf-audit-skill.user.toml`. For a skill without a provenance map, run Test Skill (`@Ferris TS`) to check it against its source, or re-create it with Create Skill (`@Ferris CS`) to make it auditable. A pipeline that matched `user-cancelled` or an `init.degraded-mode` decision for such a skill matches `no-baseline` instead. Run audits where `uv` is installed.

- **skf-audit-skill, skf-forger:** Audit Skill no longer checks out SKF's shared clone to audit a newer upstream ref: the `[C]` choice of its upstream-drift gate reads that ref into a private tree of the run's own and leaves the clone as it was, with no lock held across steps. The dirty-worktree sub-gate goes with the checkout, and so do its inputs `dirty_worktree_choice` and `force`. Since nothing on disk changes any more, `[C]` is now the headless default when `upstream_drift_choice` is unset (it was `[S]`, stay on the baseline). The upstream check is one script call that compares commits, so a clone made with `--depth 1 --branch <tag>` no longer reads as moved on every audit. The result contract and the `SKF_AUDIT_RESULT_JSON` envelope gain `upstream_moved` and `upstream_ref`, and `next_workflow` is now `update-skill` also when upstream moved, whatever the drift score, so a `maintain` pipeline reaches update-skill after an upstream release: the forger's gate no longer skips US after a `CLEAN` audit whose `next_workflow` is `update-skill`, and it passes `upstream_ref` to update-skill as `--target-ref`. Every envelope, each halt's included, now comes from the shared emitter and is checked against the new `skf-audit-result-envelope.v1.json`; it adds `run_id`, `result_path`, `headless_decisions` (the manifest-vs-link, degraded-mode and upstream-drift gates' choices), `warnings` and, on a halt, `error`, and three halt reasons with exit `3`: `helper-missing`, `provenance-invalid` and `source-unreadable` (a source file the re-index could read neither by script nor by eye). The upstream-drift gate's headless decision is recorded once its choice has run, as `S` when `[C]` could not read the private tree and the audit stayed on the baseline. ([#633](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/633); issues [#588](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/588), [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))

  **Migration:** Drop `dirty_worktree_choice` and `force` from audit-skill invocations. To keep a headless audit on the baseline ref, pass `upstream_drift_choice=S`. A pipeline that read `next_workflow` as "CRITICAL or HIGH drift found" reads `drift_score` for that instead, and `upstream_moved` and `upstream_ref` for an upstream release.

- **skf-brief-skill:** Headless Brief Skill no longer stalls or guesses at the scope boundaries when you pass no `include`: each scope type takes a stated default, and the result envelope's `warnings` names it (`warn: headless boundary default <type>: <what was chosen>`). `full-library` includes the modules the analysis found, `public-api` the files that define the names the package's entry points export (traced by the recipe runner, with full-library boundaries when it cannot trace), `component-library` the full-library boundaries plus the registry and demo patterns `skf-detect-registry.py` finds and every design system variant, and `specific-modules` the modules your `intent` or `scope_hint` names. An `exclude` passed without `include` is added to the default's exclusions. When `specific-modules` came only from the module-count rule (six or more modules, none named), the brief now gets full-library boundaries, and `scope.rationale` records the switch. A `scope_type=reference-app` argument without `include` halts with `input-missing` (exit `2`) before the repository is read, and so do a `reference-app` type the recommendation picked and a `scope_type=specific-modules` argument that names no module, at scope definition. The headless arguments are now checked, and a `preset` merged, before anything uses them, so a preset's `intent` reaches the description. An interactive `[R]` Revise Scope pass on a ratified brief now keeps its `scope.amendments`, `scope.registry_path`, `scope.ui_variants` and `scope.demo_patterns` instead of dropping them, and the component-library questions show the registry candidates `skf-detect-registry.py` scores and the demo patterns it counts. Brief Skill drops the `brief_schema_path` setting: a value set in `_bmad/custom/skf-brief-skill.toml` is ignored. Interactive runs wait once fewer at the end of each of the first three steps (the summary and the proposed description share one message, the analysis no longer pauses before scope definition, and scope definition goes straight to the brief review, which keeps `[X]` to cancel), and `BS[auto]` no longer describes an approve, edit or reject menu that a pipeline, which always runs it headless, never showed. ([#638](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/638); issues [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594), [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596), [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599), [#600](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/600))

  **Migration:** Pass `include` (comma-separated globs) to a headless run whose scope type is `reference-app`, or that passes `scope_type=specific-modules` without naming the modules in `intent` or `scope_hint`. To keep a scope narrower than full-library on a library with many modules, pass `scope_type` and `include`. Remove `brief_schema_path` from `_bmad/custom/skf-brief-skill.toml` and `skf-brief-skill.user.toml`: nothing replaces it, because the brief writer's schema decides what a brief holds.

- **skf-brief-skill:** A ratified brief is written to `forge-data/<name>/skill-brief.yaml`, and the overwrite question is now skipped only when that is the file the run ratified. When the brief came from another path (such as `briefs/skill-brief.yaml`) or was renamed at the confirmation step, and another brief already sits at that path, an interactive run now asks before replacing it, and a headless `from_brief` run stops with exit code `5` (`overwrite-cancelled`) unless `force` is passed, as the invocation contract's rule that existing briefs are kept unless `force` says. Before, the existing brief was replaced without a question, while the docs promised that the ratified file was rewritten in place. ([#659](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/659))

  **Migration:** To let a headless `from_brief` run replace a brief that already sits at `forge-data/<name>/skill-brief.yaml`, pass `force`; to keep that brief, ratify it from its own path or give the ratified brief another name at the confirmation step.

- **skf-campaign:** A headless campaign no longer stops at the first Tier A skill whose dependency failed. When every unmet dependency of a Tier A skill failed or was skipped, headless now skips that skill (`[S]kip`), logs why, and goes on with the independent skills, the Tier B batch and the report. It halts only while a dependency is still pending or active, now with exit code `13` and error code `dependency-blocked`, which the interactive `[H]alt` at a blocked skill uses too; before, that halt had no exit code. In execution order every dependency runs first, so only a `--from` resume or a hand-edited state leaves one pending. Exit code `7` (`dependency-deadlock`) stays for a deadlock, which Strategy's checks keep a normal run from reaching too. The Tier B batch applies no dependency rule, as before. `campaign-deps.py --check` reports each unmet dependency's status (`unmet_status`) and the headless choice (`default_action`). ([#635](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/635); issue [#586](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/586))

  **Migration:** A job that expected a headless campaign to halt at a skill whose dependency failed now sees it finish, with that skill `skipped` in state and in the decision log; branch on exit code `13` (`dependency-blocked`) for the halt that remains.

- **skf-campaign:** Campaign Setup no longer asks whether to send anonymized quality findings to the shared improvement queue, and `health_findings_queue` is no longer a required field of `_campaign-state.yaml`. The field stays optional and is ignored, in the state and in `campaign-brief.yaml`, so older campaigns still validate and resume. The end-of-run health check decides where findings go, as it does for every workflow: it asks before it submits a finding, and a headless run queues every finding locally. The completion message no longer shows a findings-routing setting: it shows how many skills completed and failed, and how long the run took. The Skill Loop also no longer tells the model to write campaign-level `workarounds_applied` and `quality_score`, keys the state schema rejects (a run that wrote them stopped with exit `3` at the next stage); both stay recorded per skill. ([#631](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/631); issue [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599))

  **Migration:** Nothing to change in an existing campaign. To send findings to the shared queue, answer the health check's question at the end of an interactive run. A script or brief that sets `health_findings_queue` can drop it, since Campaign ignores it.

- **skf-campaign:** The campaign's `on_complete` hook now runs after the campaign's final state write, so a hook that archives or commits the campaign workspace captures the finished campaign (`current_stage` 10); before, it ran right after the report, before that write. When the report could not be generated (the degraded finish, `halt_reason` `report-failure`), the hook is now called without `--report-path`, since there is no report to read; before, it always got `--report-path=<path>`, even when no report was written there. ([#641](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/641); issue [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585))

  **Migration:** Treat `--report-path` as optional in an `on_complete` hook: when it is absent, the report failed and the campaign decision log says why. A hook that reads `_campaign-state.yaml` now sees the finished campaign, with `current_stage` 10.

- **skf-campaign:** Campaign now enforces its quality gate instead of only printing it. Each Tier A skill runs brief, compile and test with `--headless`: brief-skill gets the repository, the pin (as `target_version`), the skill name and the target's language and scope hints from `campaign-quality-gate.py resolve`, and test-skill gets `--threshold` from `quality_gate_soft_target` (after the directive's `## Quality Overrides`, resolved again right before the test) with `pipeline_alias` `campaign`. The skill loop no longer runs analyze-source for a Tier A skill: brief-skill scopes the named skill itself, as the forger's `forge` pipeline does, and analyze-source's briefs, named after the repository, were overwritten or never read. A skill completes only on the test verdict `PASS`: `FAIL`, `INCONCLUSIVE` and `pass-with-drift` now fail it, although test-skill reports `status` `success` for them. At Export, the new `campaign-quality-gate.py` classifies every completed skill as `pass` (at or above `quality_gate_soft_target`), `fallback` (at or above `quality_gate_soft_fallback`) or `fail`, and only `pass` and `fallback` skills are exported, headless included: the Export table shows each verdict and skill path (now recorded for Tier A skills too) and lists the skills it leaves out. The capstone composes only those skills, each by the skill folder its build wrote. The campaign report gains an Export Gate section, and the headless `SKF_CAMPAIGN_RESULT_JSON` envelope gains `export_verdicts` and `skills_excluded`, so a caller sees which completed skills were not exported. A Tier B skill is classified by the skill-check score quick-skill records, so one with no score is not exported. The directive's `## Skip List` now skips the skills it names in the skill loop and the Tier B batch, with formats for both sections in the directive spec. A `quality_gate` in a campaign brief now wins over the customize.toml values, as documented (Setup settles it with `campaign-quality-gate.py check --brief-file`), and `quality_gate_hard` accepts only `zero-critical-high` (Setup stops on another value with exit `2` `invalid-input`). The Tier B batch writes a line-to-skill map, `_batch-map.json`, joins quick-skill's results to skills by batch number, and batches skills an interrupted batch left `active`; a language or scope hint that holds a space is left off its batch line and logged, since quick-skill reads a hint as one word. The capstone gives `skf-create-stack-skill` its `skills`, `stack_name` and `architecture_doc_path` inputs headless and records the `quality_score` it reports. The Tier A kickoff message no longer shows the campaign-wide gate or a pipeline list: the skill loop states both per skill. ([#635](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/635), [#655](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/655); issues [#586](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/586), [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596))

  **Migration:** Set `quality_gate_soft_target` and `quality_gate_soft_fallback` to the bar you want enforced (in customize.toml, the campaign brief, or a directive `## Quality Overrides` line such as `- soft_target: 85`): a completed skill below the fallback is now listed at Export and not exported. test-skill already fails a Tier A skill below its threshold unless it scores at least 80, so a fallback below 80 changes only Tier B skills (or a target below 80), and a Tier B skill needs `npx skill-check` for a score. Replace any other `quality_gate_hard` value with `zero-critical-high`, and remove `## Skip List` entries you meant only as notes. A headless caller that read `skills_completed` as the exported count reads `skills_excluded` too.

- **skf-campaign:** `campaign-validate-state.py` now refuses a campaign state whose `started_at`, `last_updated` or `completed_at` (the campaign's, its capstone's or a skill's) is not an ISO-8601 date and time with a UTC offset, such as `2026-05-01T00:00:00Z` or `2026-05-01T00:00:00+00:00`. `campaign-state.py` stamps every one of them from the clock in UTC, so a campaign started on 3.0.0 always passes. A state written by hand before 3.0.0 with a timestamp that has no offset now stops every stage that loads it with exit code `3` (`invalid-state`), and `campaign resume` stops with exit code `9` (`corrupt-state`) when its `.bak` holds such a timestamp too. ([#641](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/641); issue [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587))

  **Migration:** Before you resume a campaign started before 3.0.0, rewrite each `started_at`, `last_updated` and `completed_at` in `_campaign-state.yaml` and `_campaign-state.yaml.bak` as ISO-8601 with `Z` or `+00:00` (for example `2026-05-01T09:30:00Z`), then run `campaign resume`.

- **skf-campaign:** Campaign validates every target before the first compile, whatever its source: a `--manifest` file, a `--brief` campaign-brief.yaml (the new `campaign-parse-manifest.py --brief`), or the list given at the Setup question. Setup now stops with exit `2` `invalid-input` on a `repo_url` that is no GitHub repository (another host, or a path inside a repository such as `/tree/main`), on a name that is no skill name (lower-case letters, digits and hyphens, at most 64 characters, as brief-skill requires), on a Tier A `pin` that is no X.Y.Z version (brief-skill builds a Tier A skill from a version only, as its `target_version`), on two targets with one name, and on a brief target whose `pin` is not a string. A brief or a pasted list used to pass unchecked, a duplicate name silently replaced the first, and a bad name or pin failed its skill part-way through the run. A `repo_url` given as an SSH URL or `owner/repo` is written to the campaign brief as `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>`, the shape every later stage reads. A target with an empty name takes its repository's name. Strategy now rejects a Tier A skill that depends on a Tier B skill (exit `4` `circular-deps`, its `tier_inversions` named) before any skill is built: Tier B skills are built after the skill loop, so that skill used to stop at its dependency gate. An interactive Setup asks about each such dependency, and each `depends_on` name that is no target, from the script's output, whose entries name a `skill` and its `depends_on`. ([#635](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/635); issue [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594))

  **Migration:** Give every target a unique skill name (lower-case letters, digits and hyphens) and a GitHub repository URL, SSH URL or `owner/repo` as its `repo_url`. Write a Tier A pin as a full version (`1.10.0` or `v1.10.0`, not `1.10` or a branch name) and quote it in a campaign brief (`pin: "1.10.0"`), and make each dependency of a Tier A skill Tier A too, or drop the dependency.

- **skf-campaign:** Campaign now records the verdict Verify Stack returns. `campaign.verification.overall_verdict` in `_campaign-state.yaml` takes `FEASIBLE`, `CONDITIONALLY_FEASIBLE` or `NOT_FEASIBLE`, and the values `Verified`, `Plausible`, `Risky` and `Blocked` are removed: they are per-pair verdicts, so a real overall verdict failed state validation, and `campaign.capstone.verified` could never be `true`. It is now `true` when the verdict is `FEASIBLE`. The verification stage now saves the timestamped report Verify Stack wrote (the envelope's `report_path`, `feasibility-report-<slug>-<timestamp>.md`) in `campaign.verification.report_path`, instead of the `-latest` copy that every later Verify Stack run rewrites. The refinement stage hands Refine Architecture that report, where it used to search `feasibility-report-*-latest.md` on resume (and could pick a stale report or miss a customized output folder). When that path is empty, it passes `--vs-report-path none` so Refine Architecture refines without a report. ([#631](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/631); issue [#590](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/590))

  **Migration:** If `_campaign-state.yaml` holds `Verified`, `Plausible`, `Risky` or `Blocked` in `campaign.verification.overall_verdict`, set it to the `overallVerdict` of the report at `campaign.verification.report_path`, or to `null`, then run `campaign resume`. A script that reads the field compares it with `FEASIBLE`, `CONDITIONALLY_FEASIBLE` and `NOT_FEASIBLE`.

- **skf-create-skill:** A headless run that meets an authoritative AI documentation file outside the brief's scope (`llms.txt`, `AGENTS.md` and the like) now records it in the brief's `scope.amendments` with `action: "deferred-headless"` instead of `action: "skipped"`, and leaves the file out of that run without declining it: the next interactive run asks about it. It also records one auto-decision per file, with the gate `authoritative-file:<path>`, in the evidence report's Auto-Decisions table and the result's `headless_decisions`. A file the brief already defers is not recorded again. Every decision on such a file (promote, skip or defer) now reaches the brief through `skf-write-skill-brief.py amend`, which keeps the previous brief as `skill-brief.yaml.bak`, refuses an amendment that breaks the brief schema, and adds a promoted file to `scope.include` only when that list is not empty. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issue [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))

  **Migration:** A tool that reads `scope.amendments` and treats `action: "skipped"` as the only headless outcome also reads `action: "deferred-headless"`, which means no person has decided the file yet. Existing briefs need no change: a `skipped` amendment an earlier headless run wrote already counts as deferred, and the next interactive run asks about its file.

- **skf-create-skill:** `--batch` now checks every brief with the brief schema validator before the first one compiles, refuses a brief whose `name` an earlier brief of the batch already has (both would compile into one skill folder), shows the plan (the briefs to compile, and each refused brief with the validator's first message), and records a refused brief as failed instead of halting the batch on it later. A HARD HALT now ends only its own brief: the batch records it with its `halt_reason` and exit code and compiles the next brief, where it used to stop the run, with the brief still checkpointed so every later `--batch` run halted on it again. The paths after `--batch` name the briefs (a `skill-brief.yaml`, or a folder whose briefs are compiled), `forge_data_folder` when none is given. When the batch ends it writes `create-skill-batch-<timestamp>.json` and `create-skill-batch-latest.json` under `<skills_output_folder>/_batch/` (each brief's `status`, `exit_code`, `skill_package` and `error_code`), runs the health check once, and exits with the highest exit code among its briefs, `0` when every brief finished. The shared `skf-quick-batch.py` keeps each set of briefs' batch in a folder of its own, `_bmad-output/.skf-run/skf-create-skill-batch-<hash>/`, so a batch an ended session left goes on with the brief it was working on when it is run again with the same briefs, a batch of other briefs started from another terminal never touches it, and `batch-state.yaml` in the forger sidecar, with its `batch_active`, `current_index`, `completed` and `refused` fields, is no longer written. Run with no brief path, skill name or `--batch`, Create Skill now loads the only brief in `forge_data_folder` and names it in its opening banner, asks which one to compile when there are several, and in a headless run stops with exit code `2` (`brief-missing`) naming them, where it used to stop with "No skill brief found" whatever the folder held. The recipe runner now records each function's `params` and `return_type`, and compile writes the whole provenance map through the new `skf-extraction-inventory.py provenance` command (an entry for every export the extraction inventory holds, matched by ast-grep or read by eye, then the documentation and file entries through `provenance --add-entries`), so no entry is retyped and a signature is read by eye only where the runner could not parse it. The doc-rot step now also proposes a changelog, release or pull request line that names one of the skill's exports, such as a Keep a Changelog `### Removed` bullet with no correction keyword, ranks keyword hits and lines under a change section (a heading that is a change name as a whole, such as `### Removed`; GitHub's generated `## What's Changed` is not one) ahead of plain mentions when it cuts the list, and cites each `## CORRECTION` block in a provenance form the skill's citation rule allows (the temporal QMD collection, or the affected export's source line) instead of a bare file name. A team can now drop the bundled `persistent_facts` entry that loads every `project-context.md` by adding it with a leading `!` in `_bmad/custom/skf-create-skill.toml`. ([#638](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/638); issues [#582](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/582), [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585), [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594), [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596), [#600](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/600), [#605](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/605))

  **Migration:** A pipeline that read `batch-state.yaml` (`batch_active`, `current_index`, `completed`, `refused`) reads `create-skill-batch-latest.json` under `<skills_output_folder>/_batch/` instead, each brief's outcome in its `results[]`. One that relied on a batch stopping at its first failing brief reads each brief's `exit_code` and `error_code` there, or the per-brief `fail` events, since the batch now compiles every valid brief and exits with the highest failing code. A headless run that named no brief passes the brief's path or skill name, or `--batch`, when `forge_data_folder` holds more than one brief.

- **skf-create-skill:** A `docs-only` brief whose documentation could not be fetched at all (every `doc_urls` page failed, or the environment has no web fetch tool) now stops before compiling with `halt_reason` `docs-unreachable` and exit code `3`, naming each URL that failed; it used to compile and publish an empty skill. Nothing is staged or written. A source brief whose extraction found no export and whose every `doc_urls` fetch failed now gets the zero-export warning at that point, as a source brief without `doc_urls` already did. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issue [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))

  **Migration:** Fix what kept the documentation out of reach (the network, the web fetch tool, or the `doc_urls` in the brief), then re-run Create Skill. A pipeline that treated an empty docs-only skill as done now reads the halt's `SKF_CREATE_SKILL_RESULT_JSON` line or its exit code `3`.

- **skf-create-skill:** A HARD HALT after step 7 has promoted the skill package now reports `status: "failed"`, with the promoted files in `skill_package` and `outputs`, where the old halt contract reported `partial`. `partial` now means only a finished source brief whose extraction found no export (`summary.warning: "zero-exports"`). The run's auto-decisions are no longer written to the sidecar's `auto-decisions.jsonl`: each one is recorded in the brief's run folder as its gate decides, and reaches the result as `headless_decisions`. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issue [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))

  **Migration:** A pipeline that read `status: "partial"` as "the package is on disk but the run stopped" now reads `status: "failed"` and checks `skill_package` and `outputs`, which are set once step 7 has promoted the package. A tool that read `auto-decisions.jsonl` reads `headless_decisions` instead, from the `SKF_CREATE_SKILL_RESULT_JSON` line or from `create-skill-result-latest.json` in the version's forge folder.

- **skf-create-skill, skf-quick-skill, skf-create-stack-skill:** Create Skill, Quick Skill and Stack Skill now stop with `flat-layout`, and write nothing, instead of writing a new version into an SKF skill that still uses the old flat layout (`SKILL.md` directly in `<skills folder>/<name>/`, with no version folder). Before, they wrote the new version beside the flat files and finished without error, which left a skill SKF could no longer migrate or rename. In a pipeline this is the new `state-conflict` exit (Quick Skill `9`, Stack Skill `5`), and Create Skill's headless `SKF_CREATE_SKILL_RESULT_JSON` line (on stderr) reports `status` `failed` with `summary.halt_reason` `flat-layout`. ([#509](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/509); issue [#501](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/501))

  **Migration:** Run `@Ferris TS <name>` (Test Skill) once, or `US`, `AS` or `EX` on the skill, to move it into the versioned layout, then re-run Create Skill, Quick Skill or Stack Skill. Skills already in the versioned layout are not affected. If `skills_output_folder` or `forge_data_folder` is set to `_bmad-output`, the flat files may instead be staging files an earlier release left there: delete them as the troubleshooting guide describes, then re-run.

- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill drops the `manifest_patterns_path`, `provenance_map_schema_path` and `compose_mode_rules_path` settings: the manifest scanner, the provenance verifier and the co-mention helper own those rules, so a house-style copy changed nothing the run did, or could break a map later workflows read. A `_bmad/custom/skf-create-stack-skill.toml` that sets them is now ignored. `stack_skill_template_path` no longer covers the `metadata.json` structure: it moved to `assets/metadata-contract.md`, which no override replaces, because its `generated_by` and `tool_versions.skf` fields, and `skill_type` with `forge_tier` or `confidence_tier`, are the markers every later SKF run checks before it writes. The override still covers the SKILL.md, context snippet, catalog and reference-file structures. The `on_complete` comment now names the step that runs the hook and says a halted run never runs it. ([#640](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/640); issue [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596))

  **Migration:** Remove `manifest_patterns_path`, `provenance_map_schema_path` and `compose_mode_rules_path` from `_bmad/custom/skf-create-stack-skill.toml` and `_bmad/custom/skf-create-stack-skill.user.toml`. If your copy of the stack template changed its `metadata.json` section, drop that section from the copy: the run now writes the fields of `assets/metadata-contract.md` whatever the override holds.

- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill's `SKF_STACK_RESULT_JSON` line now has a schema, `skf-stack-result-envelope.v1.json`, and the shared emitter builds and checks every line, a halt's included, so no step types one. A shared helper the run needs that resolves to no path now stops it with exit `3` and the new halt reason `helper-missing`, with an envelope, where some of these halts printed no envelope or exit code and others reported `resolution-failure`; a missing `skf-comention-pairs.py` halts too instead of being worked out by hand. A Verify Stack report with a verdict token outside the schema's set now stops the run with exit `2` and the new halt reason `unknown-verdict-token`, and cancelling at the no-manifests prompt exits `6` with `user-cancelled`. Every envelope gains `run_id`, `result_path`, `headless_decisions` and `warnings`, and a halt's gains `error` with the step and check it stopped at (`phase`) and its message. The run records each decision it takes without asking the moment its gate decides: the compose-mode suggestion and the scan scope in the first step, the scope gate, whose record names every library the default left out of the stack and why, and the review gate. The emitter names the per-run result file `create-stack-skill-result-<YYYYMMDD-HHmmss>.json` (UTC, `-2` and so on for a run that ends in the same second as another) in the version's forge folder and writes its `create-stack-skill-result-latest.json` copy beside it; the copy at the stack group root stays. The run keeps its state in a run folder under `_bmad-output/.skf-run/`, deleted when the run finishes or is cancelled and kept after any other halt: the import counts, extraction bundle and checked export records the later steps read from disk instead of from memory, and the compiled draft. The review gate shows the stats and the draft's path instead of the whole compiled SKILL.md (`[P]` shows it, and a headless run never does), feedback edits the draft, and the approved draft is staged as it stands. The scope is confirmed at one gate instead of two prompts. ([#640](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/640); issues [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593), [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587), [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599), [#600](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/600))

  **Migration:** A pipeline that branched on `resolution-failure` for a missing helper branches on `helper-missing` (exit `3` either way): reinstall SKF, or install `uv`, so the shared helpers resolve. One that globbed `create-stack-skill-result-*-*-*.json` for the old `-{pid}-{rand}` suffix reads `result_path` from the envelope or `create-stack-skill-result-latest.json` instead. A pipeline that validates envelopes against its own copy of a schema takes the installed `skf-stack-result-envelope.v1.json`. To review the whole draft before approving it, answer `[P]` at the review gate or open the path it shows.

- **skf-drop-skill:** Drop Skill no longer reads the `default_mode` setting: the drop mode now comes only from the `mode` argument (`--mode deprecate` or `--mode purge`) or, in an interactive run, from the question Drop Skill asks. A `--headless` run with no `mode` argument now stops with exit `2` (`input-missing`) even when an override sets `default_mode`, and the result record's `headless_provenance.mode_source` no longer takes the value `customize.toml.workflow.default_mode`. Any non-empty `forbid_purge_in_headless` now blocks a headless purge (exit `6`, `headless-purge-forbidden`), where before only the exact string `"true"` did: a TOML `true`, `"yes"`, and even `"false"` or `false`, now turn the guard on, and only an empty value leaves it off. The guard checks the `mode` argument alone. The halt that asks for a mode, and the purge guard's halt, no longer point at `customize.toml`, which every update overwrites: the guard's message says to re-run with `mode=deprecate`, or to empty `forbid_purge_in_headless` in the team or personal override that sets it. A failing `on_complete` hook is now shown as a line of its own after the result, instead of being dropped. ([#639](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/639), [#658](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/658); issues [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596), [#595](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/595))

  **Migration:** Remove `default_mode` from `_bmad/custom/skf-drop-skill.toml` and `_bmad/custom/skf-drop-skill.user.toml`, and pass `--mode deprecate` or `--mode purge` on every headless drop. To keep the headless-purge guard off, remove `forbid_purge_in_headless` from those overrides or set it to `""`; to turn it on, set it to any other value, written quoted, such as `"true"`: an override file that fails to parse (an unquoted `True`, `yes` or `on` included), the personal `.user.toml` as much as the team file, makes the run ignore both overrides with a `customization_resolver_unavailable` warning, which leaves the guard off.

- **skf-drop-skill:** Drop Skill deletes a draft skill (one SKF made that was never exported, so the export manifest has no entry for it) only when the run asks for a purge. A draft cannot be deprecated, so `--mode deprecate` now stops with exit `2` (`input-invalid`), and a `--headless` run with no `--mode` stops with exit `2` (`input-missing`). Before, Drop Skill switched each of these runs to a purge and deleted the skill, in a headless run even with `forbid_purge_in_headless` set. ([#499](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/499), [#639](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/639); issue [#497](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/497))

  **Migration:** To delete a draft skill, run `@Ferris DS <name> --mode purge`; a `--headless` run also needs `forbid_purge_in_headless` unset. To keep it, change nothing.

- **skf-drop-skill:** A headless Drop Skill run on a skill in the export manifest now needs the `version` argument: without it the run stops with exit `2` and `input-missing` before it changes anything, where before it had no rule for the missing answer and could stall or guess. A specific version for a draft skill (one with no manifest entry) now stops with exit `2` and `input-invalid` in every mode; before, the drop widened to the whole skill and, headless, deleted every version. `version=all` drops the whole skill and any other value drops that one version, and a `skill_name` or `version` given at invocation now answers its question in an interactive run too, which no longer asks again. The count behind the active-version guard, the version count in the confirmation summary and the version `active` points at after a purge now come from `skf-skill-inventory.py resolve` instead of a count made by hand. ([#629](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/629); issues [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594), [#597](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/597))

  **Migration:** In every headless run on an exported skill, pass `version=all` to drop the whole skill or the version to drop, for example `version=0.5.0`. For a draft skill, pass `version=all` or leave `version` out.

- **skf-drop-skill:** When Drop Skill cannot write the export manifest, it now stops with exit `4` and `manifest-write-failed` in every mode, before it deletes or rebuilds anything, and writes no result file. Before, it went on to report "Drop operation complete.", recorded `status: "success"` in `drop-skill-result-latest.json` and exited `0`, and a headless run printed a success envelope after the error one. A missing `skf-manifest-ops.py` or `skf-rebuild-managed-sections.py` now stops the run with `write-failed` (`error.phase` `on-activation:helpers`) before its first question, where it used to stop later with `manifest-write-failed` (step 2) or `context-rebuild-failed` (step 1); a missing `skf-emit-result-envelope.py`, which Drop Skill did not use before, stops it the same way. Drop Skill now keeps its envelope payloads in a run folder under `_bmad-output/.skf-run/`, deleted when the run ends without an error and kept after a HALT, so a run that cannot create it stops with exit `4` and `write-failed` (`error.phase` `on-activation:run-folder`) where it used to go on. The `SKF_DROP_SKILL_RESULT_JSON` envelope and the result files now come from the shared emitter, checked against the new `skf-drop-skill-result-envelope.v1.json` schema: the per-run file name carries the UTC time read from the clock, the envelope adds `result_path`, `error` and, when the run recorded one, `warnings`, the result file adds `run_id`, `warnings` and the `headless_decisions` a headless run took (the drop mode and the confirmation), and a headless `--dry-run` of a purge lists the folders it would delete in `would_delete`, next to `forge_left_in_place`. `on_complete` is skipped when the result record could not be written (`result_path` is `null`, and `warnings` says why). ([#629](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/629); issues [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585), [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593), [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594))

  **Migration:** Treat exit `4` with `manifest-write-failed` as a failed drop: nothing changed, so fix the cause (permissions, a full disk, or a manifest another process changed) and run the drop again. Keep `_bmad-output/` writable, and match `write-failed` where you matched `context-rebuild-failed` or `manifest-write-failed` for a missing script; when `write-failed` names a missing script, re-install SKF. A pipeline that reads the envelope keeps every field it read before; to preview a purge, read `would_delete` from a `--dry-run --headless` run.

- **skf-drop-skill, skf-rename-skill:** Drop Skill no longer purges a whole skill, and Rename Skill no longer renames one, when the skill's folder in `skills_output_folder` or in `forge_data_folder` also holds files SKF did not write, such as a note you added there. Both stop with exit `5` (`not-skf-output`), list those entries and change nothing. Before, Drop Skill deleted the whole folder, your files included, and Rename Skill moved it. ([#499](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/499), [#509](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/509); issues [#497](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/497), [#500](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/500))

  **Migration:** Move the files SKF did not write out of `<skills folder>/<name>/` or `<forge data folder>/<name>/`, then re-run. To keep them where they are, Drop Skill can still, for an exported skill, purge a single version SKF generated or mark the skill deprecated with `--mode deprecate`. A version folder with no `metadata.json` can be one an interrupted run left behind: delete it yourself. If `skills_output_folder` or `forge_data_folder` is `_bmad-output`, the entries may be staging files an earlier release left there: delete them as the troubleshooting guide describes.

- **skf-export-skill:** Export Skill with `--dry-run` no longer writes a per-run `export-skill-result-{YYYYMMDD-HHmmss}.json` or overwrites `export-skill-result-latest.json`, and no longer runs the `on_complete` hook, so the stable result file keeps the last real export. Its `SKF_EXPORT_RESULT_JSON` envelope now reports `manifest_path: null`, which every envelope now does unless the export manifest write completed, and the dry run shows the manifest change it previews. A real export now writes each skill's `context-snippet.md` last, after its context-file gate, the context files and the manifest, so a cancelled export, or one that halts before the snippet writes, leaves the snippet as it was and a carried gotchas line keeps its one `[CARRIED]` cycle. Export also reads the test report of the version it exports through the shared helper: a test run that stopped before its verdict no longer hides the last finished report, and an `inconclusive` or `pass-with-drift` verdict now warns too. ([#629](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/629); issues [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585), [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587), [#583](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/583))

  **Migration:** Read a dry run's outcome from its `SKF_EXPORT_RESULT_JSON` line (`status: "dry-run"`), not from `export-skill-result-latest.json`, and run the export without `--dry-run` when a pipeline needs the result files or the `on_complete` hook; treat `manifest_path: null` as "the manifest was not written".

- **skf-export-skill, skf-drop-skill, skf-rename-skill:** Export Skill, Drop Skill and Rename Skill now build and write the SKF managed section of `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` or `.cursorrules` through one shared helper and one IDE mapping, so the three write the same section for the same skills. Drop Skill and Rename Skill no longer read the `unknown_ide_default_context_file` and `unknown_ide_default_skill_root` settings, and Export Skill no longer reads `managed_section_format_path`: an IDE the shared mapping does not list always resolves to `AGENTS.md` with `.agents/skills/`. Export Skill now records every exported skill in `.export-manifest.json` also when `passive_context` is `false`, measures the 300-token snippet ceiling with the token counter (and stops with exit `4`, `context-rebuild-failed`, when the counter is missing), and stops with exit `3` (`resolution-failure`) on an export manifest that does not parse or a `--context-file` value other than `CLAUDE.md`, `.cursorrules` or `AGENTS.md`. Export Skill now also finds a managed section left in `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` or `.cursorrules` when no configured IDE uses that file, and asks whether to clear, keep or rewrite it (a headless run keeps it and prints a warning). Drop Skill and Rename Skill now keep the rows of skills the manifest does not know (skills installed from elsewhere) as Export Skill does, where they used to delete them. Drop Skill takes its purge verdict from the inventory helper and Rename Skill its ownership verdict, and both delete folders only through the helper's checked delete. ([#625](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/625); issue [#591](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/591))

  **Migration:** Delete `unknown_ide_default_context_file` and `unknown_ide_default_skill_root` from `_bmad/custom/skf-drop-skill.toml` and `_bmad/custom/skf-rename-skill.toml`, and `managed_section_format_path` from `_bmad/custom/skf-export-skill.toml`, and from the `.user.toml` files beside them. Unknown IDEs now resolve to `AGENTS.md` with `.agents/skills/`; to map another IDE, open an issue to add it to the shared mapping, since an update replaces a local copy. To remove the row of a skill installed from elsewhere, run Export Skill and choose Drop at its orphan-row prompt.

- **skf-forger:** Forge pipelines (`forge-auto`, `forge`, `forge-quick`, `maintain` and chains such as `QS TS EX`) now export a skill only when Test Skill settles on `PASS`, the one verdict whose `next_workflow` is `export-skill`. A test that ends `INCONCLUSIVE` or `pass-with-drift` now stops the pipeline before `EX` with the verdict as the halt reason, and so does every `FAIL`, including a `FAIL` that a post-score cap forced while the score still cleared the threshold, which used to go on to `EX`. Ferris no longer compares the test score with a threshold of its own: a `TS[min:N]` bracket reaches Test Skill as `--threshold=N` (`forge-auto` passes `--threshold=90`), and a score between 80% and a higher threshold that Test Skill settles as a fallback `PASS` goes on to export. The new `pipeline-gate.py` script takes these decisions, and those of the `AN`, `AS` and `VS` circuit breakers, from the result envelope the step just printed, never from an earlier run's `-latest.json` file; a step that prints no envelope stops the pipeline. ([#625](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/625); issue [#586](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/586))

  **Migration:** When a pipeline stops at `TS`, open the test report it names. For `FAIL`, run `@Ferris US <name> --from-test-report`, then `@Ferris TS[<name>] EX`. For `INCONCLUSIVE`, add evidence (install skill-check, or move up a tier with `@Ferris SF`) and test again. For `pass-with-drift`, test again against the pinned commit without `--allow-workspace-drift`. Export a skill the test did not pass only by running `@Ferris EX <name>` yourself after reviewing it.

- **skf-forger:** Ferris now hands the whole pipeline invocation to `parse-pipeline.py` and stops before any workflow runs when it does not parse: an unknown code such as the `TX` in `BS CS TX EX`, which used to be dropped so that the chain ran `BS CS EX` and exported an untested skill, an alias without the argument its first workflow needs (`forge-quick` alone, or `forge <repo-url-or-path>` with no skill name), an argument an alias does not take, a flag other than `--pin`, `--headless` or `-H`, or a malformed threshold bracket such as `TS[min:80%]`, which used to pass on as a target. Interactively Ferris names the problem and asks for the corrected invocation; a headless run halts and writes a `failed` pipeline result to `pipeline-result-latest.json`, with the reason in `summary.halt_reason`, as it now also does for the removed `onboard` alias and when the parser prints no JSON. The documented alias forms parse with their arguments, which the parser returned as unknown codes before: `forge-auto <repo-or-doc-url>` (with `--pin <version>`), `forge <repo-url-or-path> <skill-name>`, `forge-quick <package-or-url>` and `maintain <skill>`. Bracket keywords match in any case (`TS[MIN:80]`, `AN[AUTO]`), and a `min:N` on a code other than `AN` and `TS`, such as `EX[min:80]` or `CS[min:80]`, is ignored with a warning: only `AN` (a unit count) and `TS` (a test threshold) take one. ([#625](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/625); issue [#586](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/586))

  **Migration:** Fix the invocation the halt names and run it again: correct the code, give the alias its argument (`forge-quick cognee`), put a target in brackets (`CS[cocoindex]`), write a threshold as `TS[min:80]`, or drop the extra argument, flag or `min:N`.

- **skf-forger:** A pipeline now stops after `VS` only when Verify Stack covered none of the architecture's technologies (`coverage_percentage` 0), with `zero-coverage` as the halt reason, since it then verified nothing a later step can use. The pipeline contract used to stop a chain such as `VS RA` when every integration was blocked. Now a `NOT_FEASIBLE` verdict with coverage, such as one a single `Blocked` integration gives, goes on to `RA`, which takes each Blocked integration as a critical issue to redesign. ([#625](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/625); issue [#586](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/586))

  **Migration:** When a `VS RA` chain stops at `VS` with `zero-coverage`, create skills for the technologies the architecture names (for example with `@Ferris forge-quick <package>`), then run `@Ferris VS RA` again. To stop before `RA` on any `NOT_FEASIBLE` verdict, run `@Ferris VS` on its own and run `@Ferris RA` after reading the feasibility report.

- **skf-quick-skill:** Quick Skill's `on_complete` hook is now called with `--result-path=<skill package>/quick-skill-result-latest.json`, like the other per-skill workflows, instead of `--skill-package=<skill package>`. It runs only when the run wrote that file: when the result contract or its `-latest` copy was not written, the run skips the hook and says so. ([#650](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/650); issue [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596))

  **Migration:** Change an `on_complete` command in `_bmad/custom/skf-quick-skill.toml` or `_bmad/custom/skf-quick-skill.user.toml` to take `--result-path` instead of `--skill-package`. The skill package is the folder that holds that file (the parent of `--result-path`), and the file's `outputs` list its `SKILL.md`, `context-snippet.md` and `metadata.json` paths.

- **skf-quick-skill:** The package resolver now asks every registry of the npm, PyPI, crates.io chain for a package name, not only until one resolves it. The first registry in that order that gives a GitHub repository is the pick, and a name another registry also answers for gets the new status `ambiguous`, with those registries in `name_found_in` and the other candidates in `also_found_in`: an interactive run shows every candidate and asks which project you mean, and a headless run keeps the pick and records an `also_found_in` warning in its result envelope. A registry that could not be read (an error other than a timeout, such as a blocked or failing npm) counts as an answer too. Before, the run compiled whichever repository the first registry to resolve the name gave, with no word of the others, so a bare `numpy` compiled an unrelated npm project in silence. The resolver also reads repository names with dots and `/tree/<ref>/<folder>` URLs, so `next`, `three` and `socket.io` resolve to `vercel/next.js`, `mrdoob/three.js` and `socketio/socket.io` instead of an unrelated PyPI project or a failed lookup, and it reads PyPI's `project_urls` labels in any letter case, so `numpy` and `pandas` resolve to their PyPI projects with a Python language hint (`language=python` on a batch line) or a `==` pin; without one, npm's `numpy`, `pandas` or `requests` stays the pick, and the gate or the warning names PyPI's. Every target now goes through the resolver's new `parse-target` step: `requests==2.31.0` pins a version looked up on PyPI only, npm, PyPI and crates.io page URLs are accepted, an npm dist-tag such as `next@canary` pins no version instead of halting on a missing tag, a URL on another host or a local path gets a message pointing to `/skf-brief-skill` instead of a failed lookup, a JavaScript, TypeScript, Python or Rust language hint looks the name up in that language's registry only, and a monorepo package's folder (npm's `repository.directory`, or the folder of a `/tree/` URL) becomes the default scope. A skill is now named after its package (`@babel/core` gives `babel-core`), else its `/tree/` folder or its repository, in lower case with hyphens, so `https://github.com/vercel/next.js` gives `next-js` instead of a `next.js` skill whose name fails validation, and two packages of one repository no longer write one skill folder. A pinned version's tag is now checked through `skf-github-probe.py`, which falls back to `git ls-remote` when `gh` is missing or logged out and also finds a monorepo package's tags (`astro@4.16.0`), so a tag is reported missing only when the tag listing lacks it, with the tags nearest the version, and a GitHub access failure is reported as one. ([#621](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/621), [#627](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/627); issues [#582](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/582), [#588](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/588))

  **Migration:** Pass the GitHub URL of the project you mean (for example `https://github.com/vercel/next.js`), or its npm, PyPI or crates.io page URL, instead of a package name that more than one registry answers for; in a batch file, a `language=` modifier on the line also picks the registry. A skill Quick Skill wrote before under a name with capitals, dots or underscores, or under its repository's name for a package named otherwise (`query` for `@tanstack/react-query`), gets the new name on its next run: rename the old skill to that name with `@Ferris RS` first so the run updates it instead of writing a second skill.

- **skf-quick-skill:** `--batch` no longer ends at the first target that fails. A HARD HALT now ends only its own target, which the batch records as failed, and the batch runs every remaining target, writes its summary and runs the health check once, after the summary, instead of after each target. It then exits with the highest exit code among its failed targets (`0` when none failed), where it used to exit with the first failed target's code. `--fail-fast` keeps the old stop: the batch ends at the first failed target, writes its summary and exits with that target's code. The new `skf-quick-batch.py` helper parses the batch file and keeps the batch's place and every target's outcome on disk in the run folder as each target ends, so a long batch survives a context compaction, and it computes the counts, the status and the exit code. A batch file that cannot be read, or a missing `skf-quick-batch.py`, now stops the run before its first target with exit code `2` (`input-invalid`), and a batch run folder that cannot be written stops it with exit code `4` (`write-failure`). A line with a word after its target that is not a `language=` or `scope=` modifier, a trailing `# comment` included, is taken whole as its target, which then fails on its own. ([#627](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/627); issues [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585), [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587))

  **Migration:** A pipeline that relied on a batch stopping at its first failed target passes `--fail-fast`. One that read the batch's exit code as the first failed target's code reads each target's own `exit_code` and `error_code` in the `results[]` of `quick-skill-batch-latest.json`, or in the per-target `fail` events. In a batch file, put a comment on a line of its own that starts with `#`, and write only `language=` and `scope=` modifiers after a target.

- **skf-quick-skill:** `--language-hint` and `--scope-hint` no longer combine with `--batch`. A batch run given either flag now stops before its first target with exit code `2` (`error.code` `input-invalid`, `phase` `on-activation`) and writes no batch summary; before, the run did not read the two flags, so the batch went on without them and could exit `0`. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issue [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594))

  **Migration:** Drop `--language-hint` and `--scope-hint` from `--batch` runs and move the hint onto each batch line that needs it, as `language=<lang>` or `scope=<path>`.

- **skf-quick-skill:** `--description` and `--exports` no longer combine with `--batch`. A batch run given either flag now stops before its first target with exit code `2` (`error.code` `input-invalid`, `phase` `on-activation`) and writes no batch summary; before, it wrote the same description or export list into every skill in the batch. `--skip-snippet` and `--no-active-pointer` still apply to every target. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#609](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/609))

  **Migration:** Drop `--description` and `--exports` from `--batch` runs and let each target's extraction supply them. To give a target its own description or export list, run it on its own: `@Ferris QS <target> --description "..."` or `--exports "name1,name2"`.

- **skf-quick-skill:** A missing GitHub fetch helper (`skf-github-fetch.py`), skills-module helper (`skf-skills-module.py`), public-API extractor (`skf-extract-public-api.py`), atomic writer (`skf-atomic-write.py`), output validator (`skf-validate-output.py`) or frontmatter validator (`skf-validate-frontmatter.py`, used when skill-check is unavailable) now stops Quick Skill with exit `3` `resolution-failure`, naming the helper in the message and in the envelope's `error.details.cause`, instead of fetching files with `gh api` by hand, classifying the repository from its README alone, parsing exports in the prompt, copying `metadata.json` with `cp`, or skipping the output or frontmatter checks. The output validator now runs through `uv`. ([#645](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/645); issue [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599))

  **Migration:** Reinstall SKF (`npx bmad-module-skill-forge install`) or install `uv` so the shipped helpers resolve. A batch that relied on the fallback branches on exit `3`.

- **skf-quick-skill:** When the version Quick Skill writes already holds a build another SKF workflow made (a Create Skill or Stack Skill build), the overwrite question now names that build and its confidence tier, and a yes replaces the whole package, so none of the old build's files (such as `references/` or `extraction-rules.yaml`) stays beside the quick skill; the completion summary names the build's forge data folder, which is left in place. A headless run, or a `--batch` target, over such a version no longer overwrites it: it stops with exit code `5` (`overwrite-cancelled`), with `error.details.existing_generator` naming the generator, and records the `write-and-validate.overwrite` decision with the default `N`. A quick skill over a quick skill is still overwritten without asking in headless mode. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))

  **Migration:** To replace another workflow's build with a quick skill, run Quick Skill interactively and answer `Y` at the overwrite question, or remove that version folder yourself first; to keep the build, give the quick skill another version (`@Ferris QS <package>@<version>`) or leave the target out of the batch.

- **skf-quick-skill:** Quick Skill drops the `registry_resolution_path` setting: `skf-resolve-package.py` asks npm, PyPI and crates.io itself, so a house-style registry chain was never tried, and a `_bmad/custom/skf-quick-skill.toml` that sets it is now ignored. Its ecosystem check no longer searches the web for an official agentskills.io skill, because agentskills.io has no registry API and a search hit cannot show that a skill is official: the run goes from resolving the target straight to extraction, and the `ecosystem-check` step, its gate and exit code `8` stay documented for when a registry API exists. A missing or failing package resolver or language detector now stops the run with exit `3` (`resolution-failure`) instead of being worked out by hand, and a host without `python3` or `uv` stops at activation with exit `3` and a line naming the missing tool, with no result envelope, since the emitter needs `uv` too. `customize.toml` now names the real defaults of `skill_template_path` and `batch_output_path`, the headings a template copy must keep, and how `on_complete` is called. ([#637](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/637); issues [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596), [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599), [#600](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/600))

  **Migration:** Remove `registry_resolution_path` from `_bmad/custom/skf-quick-skill.toml` and `_bmad/custom/skf-quick-skill.user.toml`. To build a package that only an internal registry holds, pass its GitHub URL as the target. If a run now stops with exit `3` `resolution-failure` at activation or on a missing helper, install `uv` and Python 3.11 or later, or reinstall SKF so the shared helpers resolve.

- **skf-refine-architecture:** A missing `skf-enumerate-stack-skills.py`, `skf-validate-feasibility-report.py` or `skf-comention-pairs.py` now stops Refine Architecture with exit `3` `resolution-failure` (phase `init:inventory`, `init:feasibility-validator` or `gap-analysis:comention`) instead of falling back to a hand walk of the skills folder, a run without the [VS] report or co-mention facts found by hand. The skill listing and the mentions helper stop the run the same way when they exit non-zero (a skills folder that does not exist, say), naming their first error line. `--vs-report-path none` still refines without a report and needs no feasibility helper. The refinement rules are now checked by a script, `skf-check-preservation.py rules`, with the same tables and tier rules and the same exit `3` at `init:rules`: a VS Report Integration row raises the Issue Severity tier its Raises cell names (in bold, as the bundled rows do, or as a plain word), or names none and says No issue or None. Issue detection now joins the [VS] verdicts to the skill inventory and the document scope through `skf-check-preservation.py verdicts`, which reads the report again on every run, so a run stops with exit `8` `recovery-failed` whenever [VS] rewrote the report during the run or the refinement rules no longer pass the `rules` check, not only after a context loss. The mentions helper now reports the term that named each skill and which skills cover each technology the document names, so the scope confirmation and the `Not verified (no skill)` row read them instead of matching names by hand. ([#645](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/645); issues [#598](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/598), [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599), [#600](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/600))

  **Migration:** Reinstall SKF (`npx bmad-module-skill-forge install`) or install `uv` so the shared helpers resolve. A pipeline that relied on the fallback branches on exit `3` instead; one that wants no [VS] report passes `--vs-report-path none`.

- **skf-refine-architecture:** When Refine Architecture cannot write a result file, `refine-architecture-result-<YYYYMMDD-HHmmss>.json` or `refine-architecture-result-latest.json` in the output folder, the run no longer stops with exit `4` (`write-failed`, status `error`). It finishes with exit `0` and status `success`, and its envelope's `warnings` list `result_file_write_failed` (`result_path` is null when the per-run file is the one that failed), so `refine-architecture-result-latest.json` can still hold an earlier run's record. Exit `4` with `write-failed` at the result contract now means only that the shared emitter refused the result payload twice. Two other exits change too. An architecture document that is not UTF-8 text, which used to be refined, now halts at the start with exit `2` (`input-invalid`, phase `init:previous-pass`). A halt in the report step now sets `refined_path` to the refined document the review already promoted, instead of `null`, and its exit `8` (`recovery-failed`) now means a record of the draft's build or promotion is missing, no longer a refined document without its `## Refinement Summary`. ([#633](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/633), [#641](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/641); issues [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585), [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))

  **Migration:** A pipeline that treated exit `4` as a missing result file now checks that the envelope's `warnings` hold no `result_file_write_failed` entry and that the `run_id` in `refine-architecture-result-latest.json` matches the envelope's, before it reads that file. Re-save an architecture document that is not UTF-8 as UTF-8 text before you run Refine Architecture. A wrapper that expected `refined_path: null` on every halt reads the promoted document's path on a halt in the report step.

- **skf-refine-architecture:** `refinement_rules_path` now swaps only the house-style rules: the gap, issue and improvement types, the issue severity and improvement value tiers, and the tier each Verify Stack verdict (`Blocked`, `Risky`, `Plausible`, `Verified`) raises. Every step now classifies with the tables that file defines, so the types and tiers a team's copy adds reach the findings, the Refinement Summary and the final report instead of being overridden by the bundled lists. How the steps store their findings for recovery moved to a fixed file, `references/finding-storage.md`, so a copy can no longer drop it, and the refined document's headings, callouts and RA markers stay fixed whatever the copy says.
