Refine Architecture now stops before any analysis with exit `3` (`resolution-failure`, phase `init:rules`) when the rules file cannot be read, lacks one of its six tables (`Gap Classification`, `Issue Classification`, `Issue Severity`, `VS Report Integration`, `Improvement Classification` and `Improvement Value`), or names a tier the Refinement Summary cannot count: each severity and value tier starts with a letter and holds only the letters A to Z, digits and spaces, no two tiers share a name (so one set of names cannot serve both severity and value), no tier is named `gap`, `issue`, `improvement`, `unverified` or `skill`, and each `VS Report Integration` row raises a severity tier or no issue. The walkthrough of each refinement moved from the menu after approval to the review, where feedback can still change the draft: the review menu now offers `[R] Review each refinement`, `[C]` and `[X]`, and the final report no longer shows the `[R] Review changes in detail` and `[X]` menu, finishing the run with exit `0` on its own. A finding you drop at a review you then approve is recorded in `.ra-dismissed-<project>.json` in the output folder, and a later run on the refined document lists it as previously dismissed instead of inserting it again; an improvement stays dropped only for the capability it named, so the other improvements of the same skill still come in. The headless envelope's `warnings` gain `out_of_scope_skills`, which counts and names the skills the run's scope left out. ([#641](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/641); issues [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596), [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599), [#600](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/600), [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587), [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))

  **Migration:** If your team overrides `refinement_rules_path`, start the copy again from the bundled `references/refinement-rules.md` and re-apply your changes to its six tables, renaming any tier that breaks the rules above; a Finding Storage or Preservation section in the old copy is no longer read. A wrapper that answered the final menu with `[X]` sends nothing there now, since the run finishes on its own; to walk through the refinements, answer `[R]` at the review instead.

- **skf-refine-architecture, skf-verify-stack:** Refine Architecture now finds the report Verify Stack writes. Before it asks for its inputs, it looks up `feasibility-report-<slug>-latest.md` in the forge data folder with `skf-validate-feasibility-report.py --locate` and offers that report as the default (press Enter to use it), and a headless run without `--vs-report-path` uses it and logs that it did. Before, it looked for `feasibility-report-<project_name>.md`, a name Verify Stack never writes, so it went on without the report and never raised a `Blocked` or `Risky` pair as an issue. A report path you give, with `--vs-report-path` or at the prompt, is read through the same script, and `--architecture-doc` and `--vs-report-path` now skip their questions in interactive runs too. `--vs-report-path none` refines without a report and skips the search for one. Refine Architecture now stops with exit `2` and `input-invalid` when the report it would use has a `schemaVersion` other than `1.0`, is missing a required section, has no verdict table or two of them, uses a verdict token other than `Verified`, `Plausible`, `Risky` or `Blocked` or an `overallVerdict` other than `FEASIBLE`, `CONDITIONALLY_FEASIBLE` or `NOT_FEASIBLE` (tokens are case-sensitive), or cannot be read. Every `Plausible` pair in the document's scope is now a Minor issue, whatever its rationale says. The document scope now comes from a word-boundary match of each skill's name and of the repository and folder it was built from, the scope confirmation shows the word that named each skill and lists a skill named only through a common word (a repository called `core`, say) as ambiguous, and a `--scope-skills` name that is not a skill is now named instead of being ignored. The report check Verify Stack runs before it presents a report (`skf-validate-feasibility-report.py <report>`) now also fails on a missing or doubled verdict table and on an unknown verdict token, so Verify Stack stops with `schema-violation` on such a report. ([#623](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/623); issues [#590](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/590), [#598](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/598))

  **Migration:** Re-run Verify Stack (`@Ferris VS`) to write a current report, then run Refine Architecture again. To use a different report, pass it with `--vs-report-path <path>`; to refine without one, pass `--vs-report-path none`.

- **skf-rename-skill:** Rename Skill's lock against two renames of one skill at once now works, so exit `5` `halted-for-concurrent-run` fires while another rename of the same skill holds a fresh lock. The lock file, `.skf-rename-<name>.lock` in the forge data folder, now names the run that holds it and when that run took it, through the shared run-lock helper. Before, it held the process id of a shell that was gone by the next step, so every later run read the lock as left behind, cleared it and went on, even while the first rename was still copying. A lock goes stale 60 minutes after it was taken or renewed, so a crashed run blocks the next rename of that skill until then, and the next run replaces a stale lock with a `run-lock-replaced` warning. A rename renews its lock before it copies anything and stops with `halted-for-concurrent-run` when its lock went stale while it waited at a prompt. Every stop after the lock releases it, a release never removes another run's lock, and `--dry-run` takes no lock. Each run keeps a run folder under `_bmad-output/.skf-run/`, deleted when the rename or the dry run finishes and kept after a halt or when the emitter could not print the run's envelope, and stops with `write-failed` (exit `4`) when it cannot create that folder or when the run-lock or envelope helper is missing. The result files `rename-skill-result-<timestamp>.json` and `rename-skill-result-latest.json` now carry the auto-decisions in a top-level `headless_decisions` list, no longer in `summary.headless_decisions`, and the per-run file name is the UTC time the shared emitter stamps. `versions_renamed`, in the envelope and in the result files' `summary`, now lists only the versions whose package the rename moved: a version folder without a package is still copied but no longer listed, and the report counts the versions and the rewritten files from what the rename did instead of repeating the version count. `manifest_rekeyed` is now true only when the rename re-keyed the skill's export manifest entry, and false when the manifest has entries but none for the skill. When the new name collides because an earlier rename to it stopped after re-keying the export manifest, the run now stops with `name-collision` in interactive runs too, instead of asking for another name, and names the old folders that rename left and the commands that finish it. ([#629](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/629); issues [#588](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/588), [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587), [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))

  **Migration:** When Rename Skill stops with `halted-for-concurrent-run` and no other rename of that skill is running, delete the lock file the message names (`<forge data folder>/.skf-rename-<name>.lock`), or wait until the time the message gives, when the lock goes stale; then run the rename again. A pipeline that read `summary.headless_decisions` from a rename result file reads the top-level `headless_decisions` instead. A pipeline that read `versions_renamed` as every version folder the rename copied lists the renamed skill folder instead, and reads `manifest_rekeyed` as whether the manifest entry moved. After a `name-collision` that names leftover folders, delete them with the checked delete the message gives and run Export Skill, instead of renaming the old skill to another name.

- **skf-setup:** Setup now parses `_bmad/skf/config.yaml` and `preferences.yaml` with `skf-preflight.py`, the helper Ferris uses, instead of reading them by eye, and checks for `uv` before it does. A config that does not parse, or a `sidecar_path` that is missing or still holds a placeholder, stops setup at `on-activation:config-malformed` with the helper's message, which names the file and the fix, as Ferris does: setup used to ignore `sidecar_path` and go on. A missing helper stops it at the new `error.phase` `on-activation:helper-missing`. Because the helper runs through `uv`, `headless_mode: true` in `preferences.yaml` takes effect only once `uv` is found: a run that stops at `on-activation:uv-missing` (or `on-activation:config-missing`) prints a `blocked` `SKF_SETUP_RESULT_JSON` envelope only under `--headless`, `--quiet` or a Ferris pipeline, and the bare reason otherwise, where a preferences-only headless run used to get the envelope for a missing `uv`. ([#641](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/641); issue [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592))

  **Migration:** If setup now stops at `on-activation:config-malformed` naming `sidecar_path`, set `sidecar_path: _bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar` in `_bmad/skf/config.yaml` and run it again. A pipeline that sets only `headless_mode: true` in `preferences.yaml` and needs a `blocked` envelope from an `on-activation:uv-missing` halt passes `--headless` or `--quiet` as well.

- **skf-setup:** Setup no longer creates `preferences.yaml`: the SKF installer already writes it, and setup reads it when it is there and uses the defaults when it is not. Setup's write step loses its `step 2:init-prefs` halt, so a `blocked` `SKF_SETUP_RESULT_JSON` envelope never carries that `error.phase` again, and `files_written` no longer lists `preferences.yaml`: the value is gone from the envelope schema. ([#641](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/641); issue [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596))

  **Migration:** To create a missing `preferences.yaml`, re-run the installer (`npx bmad-module-skill-forge install`): it writes the file under `_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/`, so a project whose `sidecar_path` points elsewhere copies it from there into its own sidecar folder, and until it does every workflow uses the defaults. In a pipeline, drop any check for `error.phase` `step 2:init-prefs` (write failures are `step 2:write-tools` and `step 2:forge-data-dir`) and stop reading `preferences.yaml` in `files_written` as a sign of a first run: read `previous_tier`, which is null on a first run.

- **skf-setup:** A `--require-tier` value that is not exactly `Quick`, `Forge`, `Forge+` or `Deep` no longer switches the tier gate off. Setup passes it to `skf-detect-tools.py`, which rejects it before probing anything, and the run halts at `step 1:detect-tools` with a reason that names the valid tiers, with a did-you-mean hint (`Deep` for `deep`). Before, a wrong-case tier such as `--require-tier=deep` was dropped, and the run reported `success` even on a Quick-tier machine. The space form, `--require-tier Deep`, now works as well. An `--orphan-action` other than `keep` or `remove` halts before setup does anything, at `on-activation:orphan-action-invalid`. Setup now keeps its helpers' output in a scratch run folder under `_bmad-output/.skf-run/`, so a run that cannot create that folder halts at `step 1:run-folder`, where it used to succeed. Under `--headless` or `--quiet`, and in a forger pipeline, each of these halts ends the run `blocked`, with `error.phase` naming the halt; an interactive run halts with the same reason and no envelope. The envelope's `tier_override_invalid` warning now carries the did-you-mean hint the report shows: `tier_override_invalid: deep` becomes `tier_override_invalid: deep (did you mean Deep?)`. `--quiet` is now an alias of `--headless`, and a forger pipeline runs setup the same way: the envelope-only run is unchanged, and an orphan default taken under `--quiet` is still recorded as `quiet-default`. ([#625](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/625); issues [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594), [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))

  **Migration:** Pass `--require-tier` with the exact tier name (`Quick`, `Forge`, `Forge+` or `Deep`) and `--orphan-action` with `keep` or `remove`; a pipeline that got `success` for a mistyped tier now gets `blocked` until the value is fixed. Keep `_bmad-output/` writable, and treat `error.phase` `step 1:run-folder` like any other blocked phase. Match the `tier_override_invalid:` warning by its prefix, not by its whole text.

- **skf-setup:** A run that misses `--require-tier` now fails fast. It no longer builds a ccc index that is due: the envelope's `ccc_index.status` reads `"skipped"` where it read `"created"`, `files_written` no longer lists `ccc_index` (a fresh index is still kept, as `"fresh"`), and the FORGE STATUS banner says the index waits for a run that meets the required tier. It no longer runs the QMD and CCC registry hygiene: it removes no orphaned QMD collection, even under `--orphan-action=remove`, and prunes no stale entry from `forge-tier.yaml`'s registries, so its `warnings` hold no `orphan_auto_resolution`, `orphan_removed: <name>`, `orphan_remove_failed: <name>` or `ccc_registry_stale_removed: <path>` entry. It still prepares the ccc settings, writes `forge-tier.yaml` with the detected tier and ends `tier_failure` as before. ([#646](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/646); issue [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599))

  **Migration:** A pipeline that relied on a run that misses `--require-tier` to build the ccc index, remove orphaned QMD collections or prune stale registry entries installs the missing tools (or lowers `--require-tier`) and runs `/skf-setup` again: a run that meets the required tier builds the index and runs the hygiene.

- **skf-setup:** The `SKF_SETUP_RESULT_JSON` envelope no longer lists `write_failure` as a `status`. No run ever produced it: a failed write of `forge-tier.yaml`, `preferences.yaml` or the forge data folder has always ended with `status: "blocked"`. The schema now matches, and `status` is `blocked` exactly when `error` is not null. ([#509](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/509), [#641](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/641); issue [#502](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/502))

  **Migration:** If a pipeline tests for `write_failure`, test for `blocked` and read `error.phase` instead: `step 2:write-tools` and `step 2:forge-data-dir` name the write failures. Do not read the other fields of a `blocked` envelope: they hold placeholder values.

- **skf-setup, installer:** SKF now holds the tools it runs to the minimum versions in `src/shared/tool-requirements.yaml`, which the installer copies to `_bmad/skf/shared/`. v3.0.0 sets four: `Node.js` 22, `Python` 3.11, `git` 2.15 and `ast-grep` 0.45.3. uv, gh, ccc, qmd, tessl and skill-check have none yet, so they never warn. Setup no longer counts a tier tool below its minimum: an ast-grep older than 0.45.3 is reported unavailable (`below_minimum: true` in the detector output, `false` under `tools` in `SKF_SETUP_RESULT_JSON`), so the tier can drop (ast-grep 0.42 alone now gives Quick, not Forge), a re-run lists the tool in `tools_removed`, and `--require-tier` can fail where it passed. The envelope's `warnings` gain one `tool_below_minimum: <tool> <version> (minimum <minimum>)` entry per tool below its minimum; setup also checks git and uv, which are no tier tools, so below their minimum they only warn. No envelope field is added or removed. FORGE STATUS shows an upgrade line for each such tool at every tier instead of calling it no longer detected, and shows ccc's version when uv installed it. `install`, `update` and `status` now print each tool with the version found, `ok` or `upgrade to >= <minimum>` (or missing, optional, or `installed, version unknown`), and the command or page to upgrade or install it. The update notice now also prints after `install` and `update`, and `status` returns to the prompt in a terminal instead of waiting. ([#631](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/631); issue [#577](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/577))

  **Migration:** Upgrade each tool the report names, then run `/skf-setup` again: `npm install -g @ast-grep/cli@latest` for ast-grep, the latest release from https://git-scm.com/downloads for git, `uv python install` for Python and the current LTS release from https://nodejs.org for Node.js. A pipeline that checks the envelope's `warnings` one by one should accept the `tool_below_minimum:` prefix.

- **skf-test-skill:** Test Skill's hard gate now works, and it blocks on any Critical or High gap. The coverage, coherence and external validation steps record every gap they find in a gap ledger, `test-findings-<run_id>.json` in the skill's version folder, each with the severity and category the Gap Severity table gives it, and the gate reads that ledger: a run with a Critical or High gap stops before scoring with `hard-gate-blocked` (exit 2, verdict `FAIL`). Before, the gate looked for gap lines no step wrote, so it never blocked. The severities change: a missing export is now Medium (it lowers Export Coverage and the threshold decides, where it was Critical), a wrong signature and a fabricated one (a documented export that the source does not export and the file the skill cites for it does not define) are Critical, and in a stack skill a broken reference is Critical, a `scripts/` or `assets/` file the skill names that is not there included, an inaccurate reference High and an incomplete integration pattern Medium. In an individual skill, a missing description, usage or API section, an unbalanced code fence and a usage example that awaits a sync function or skips the `await` of an async one are High, so they now block too, while a documented function no usage example or reference file names is Medium. A blocked run no longer stops without a record: it writes the Gap Report, writes a `FAIL` record with `halt_reason` `hard-gate-blocked` to `skf-test-skill-result-<run_id>.json` and `skf-test-skill-result-latest.json` (where an earlier run's verdict used to stay), runs `on_complete` and the health check, then exits 2. Each Gap Report entry now names its category as `Coverage (missing-export)` and so on, and the next step of a FAIL names Update Skill with `--from-test-report`. The result files gain `summary.gapCounts` (the gaps by severity) and `summary.hardGate` (`passed` or `blocked`), and the test report's frontmatter a `hardGate` key. ([#627](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/627); issues [#583](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/583), [#541](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/541), [#545](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/545))

  **Migration:** Re-run Test Skill on the skills you export. A run that now exits 2 with `hard-gate-blocked` lists its Critical and High gaps first in the Gap Report: repair them with `@Ferris US <skill> --from-test-report`, or by hand (add the missing section, close the code fence, fix the example's `await`), then test again. A pipeline that read `skf-test-skill-result-latest.json` after a hard-gate stop now reads that run's `FAIL` record, with `status` `error`.

- **skf-test-skill:** A headless `skf-test-skill` run given no skill name now halts with `halt_reason` `input-missing` (exit code 1 in its envelope) instead of waiting for an answer. Every halt now prints its `SKF_TEST_RESULT_JSON` envelope, the ones that printed none before included: `runtime-missing` (no `python3` or `uv`), `helper-missing`, `helper-failed`, `write-failed`, `input-invalid` (a `--tier` value that names no tier) and `indeterminate-surface` (zero exports or an empty stack surface). The `atomic-writer-missing` halt reason is gone: the shared emitter writes the result files, and a missing SKF helper halts `helper-missing`. The per-run result file now takes its name from the clock, `skf-test-skill-result-<YYYYMMDD-HHmmss>.json`, instead of `skf-test-skill-result-<run_id>.json`, and the `on_complete` hook's `--result-path` names that file. An interactive run given a name that does not resolve lists the skills there are to test, closest first, instead of pointing at create-skill. ([#637](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/637); issues [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594), [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))

  **Migration:** Pass the skill name to every headless run (`/skf-test-skill <name> --headless`). Branch on `helper-missing` where a pipeline branched on `atomic-writer-missing`, and read `skf-test-skill-result-latest.json` or the envelope's `result_path` instead of building the per-run file name from the run id.

- **skf-test-skill:** Test Skill now holds a real run lock. Before it writes the test report, it takes `.test-skill.lock` in the skill's version folder in the forge data through the shared run-lock helper, and a second run against the same skill version halts with `another-run-active` (exit 1) while the first run's lock is fresh. Before, the lock was an `flock` that no step could hold across tool calls, so two runs could overwrite each other's `skf-test-skill-result-latest.json` and `another-run-active` never fired. A run renews its lock before it writes the result files, and every halt after the lock releases it, as do both ways a run ends, with and without `--no-health-check`. A lock left by a run that crashed now blocks the next run until it goes stale, 60 minutes after it was taken or last renewed. The run id in `test-report-<skill>-<run_id>.md`, `skf-test-skill-result-<run_id>.json` and `runId` is now the UTC time and eight random hex digits, without a process id. The lock, the version lookup and the workspace drift check all run through `uv`, so a run now checks for `uv` and `python3` first and halts when either is missing, where it used to finish as a capped FAIL. ([#624](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/624); issues [#542](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/542), [#588](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/588))

  **Migration:** When a run halts with `another-run-active` and no other Test Skill run is active for that skill, delete the lock file the message names, or re-run after the time the message gives, when the lock goes stale. A pipeline that parses a Test Skill run id should read only its leading UTC time. Install `uv` and `python3` before you run Test Skill.

- **skf-test-skill:** Test Skill drops the `scoring_rules_path` and `output_formats_path` settings: its scripts own the scoring rules, and the Gap Severity table and the ledger record format decide what blocks export, so a house-style copy either changed nothing or could change the hard gate. A `_bmad/custom/skf-test-skill.toml` that sets them is now ignored. A threshold above 80 (`default_threshold`, `--threshold` or a pipeline's default) no longer turns a FAIL that a cap forced into a PASS: the threshold fallback, which accepts a run at the 80% floor and writes an evidence report, now takes only a run that no cap failed. ([#635](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/635); issue [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596))

  **Migration:** Remove `scoring_rules_path` and `output_formats_path` from `_bmad/custom/skf-test-skill.toml` and `_bmad/custom/skf-test-skill.user.toml`; set `default_threshold` to change the pass threshold. A run a cap failed passes only once its cause is fixed: restore the tooling that `toolingStatus` names, or give a docs-only skill an external validation score by installing skill-check.

- **skf-test-skill:** The test report's `analysisConfidence` no longer takes `degraded`: it names the source access only (`full`, `provenance-map`, `metadata-only`, `remote-only` or `docs-only`), and tooling health moves to `toolingStatus` (`ok`, or `frontmatter-validator-timeout` when the frontmatter validator timed out), so a later source-access write can no longer erase it. The tooling cap now fails a run for any `toolingStatus` other than `ok`. Before, it fired only on `degraded` or on a status naming a missing helper, so a run whose frontmatter validator timed out could pass at threshold 80; it now fails. `compute-score.py` refuses `analysisConfidence: "degraded"` instead of ignoring it. ([#635](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/635); issue [#613](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/613))

  **Migration:** Read tooling health from the test report's `toolingStatus` rather than `analysisConfidence: degraded`, and have a script that calls `compute-score.py` pass the tooling problem as `toolingStatus`. Re-test a skill whose last run timed out in the frontmatter validator once the validator runs again.

- **skf-update-skill:** Update Skill now records each update in one place, at the top level of `provenance-map.json`, replacing what the previous update wrote there: `last_update`, `update_type`, `files_changed`, `exports_affected`, `confidence_tier` and `manual_sections_preserved`. A repair from a test report records `update_type` `gap-driven` (a normal update records `incremental`, a rebuild without a provenance map `full`). `metadata.json` no longer carries `last_update` or `update_type`: an update records itself there only in `generation_date`, and removes either key that an earlier SKF version left. Before, a repair from a test report had no `update_type` value to record and could put `last_update` in either file, so the two files could give different dates. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#548](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/548))

  **Migration:** Read `last_update` and `update_type` from the top level of `provenance-map.json`, not from `metadata.json`.

- **skf-update-skill:** Update Skill with `--from-test-report --allow-workspace-drift` no longer writes anything read from a source checkout that is not at the skill's pinned commit: no provenance line, no signature, parameter list, return type or node kind, and no count of the public API. Before, it recorded them under the pinned commit, so citations, signatures and stats described the wrong code. When any gap needs something from the source, as every new or changed export does and as a rescope (a gap that takes an export out of the skill's scope) does, the whole run now stops before it changes the skill, with status `halted-for-workspace-drift` (headless `error.phase` `re-extract:workspace-drift`); the message lists those gaps with what each one needs, the pinned commit and the `git checkout` command. A rescope's scope amendment reaches the skill brief only when the repair writes, so the halt leaves the brief as it was. When no gap needs the source (provenance line fixes, structural fixes and metadata patches), the run repairs the gaps as before: a line check that would have moved a line records `unknown` instead, a node kind it would have looked up in the source stays as it is with a warning, the stats keep the public API counts `metadata.json` records, and `warnings[]` gets `workspace_drift_overridden`. ([#551](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/551), [#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618), [#624](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/624), [#633](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/633); issues [#530](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/530), [#557](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/557))

  **Migration:** When the halt appears, check out the pinned commit it names (`git -C <source> checkout <commit>`) and re-run `--from-test-report` without `--allow-workspace-drift`. For a skill built at Forge tier or above from a remote repository, you can instead run a normal Update Skill (without `--from-test-report`), which records the commit it reads as the new pin, and then run Test Skill again; if that update warns `workspace-clone-not-updated`, first run the two `git` commands its report gives to move SKF's clone to the new commit. For a skill built from a local folder, only the checkout works: a normal update keeps its pin.

- **skf-update-skill:** Every `SKF_UPDATE_RESULT_JSON` line Update Skill prints, at a stop as well as at the end of a run, now comes from the shared emitter and follows `skf-update-result-envelope.v1.json`: a stop's line carries the `skf_update` wrapper with `skf_update.status` (a `halted-for-*` code or `blocked`) and `skf_update.error` (`phase`, `path`, `reason`), where before a stop printed a top-level `status` and `error` with no wrapper and fewer fields. Each gate records its headless decision in the run folder as it decides, so a stop reports the decisions taken before it. In a headless run, a document or code path found outside the skill brief's scope is no longer recorded as `skipped`: the brief records it as `deferred-headless`, and the `headless_decisions[]` entry of the gates `detect-changes.promoted-doc-prompt` and `detect-changes.scope-expansion` has `taken_action` `deferred-headless` in place of `S`, one entry per path, so the next interactive run asks about it. A `skipped` amendment an earlier headless run wrote (reason `headless: no user to prompt`) now counts as deferred too. The gate `init.degraded-rebuild`, which `--allow-degraded` records, is now in the schema. A run that finds no change now writes `update-skill-result-latest.json` and its per-run record in the current version's forge folder and runs `on_complete`; `--detect-only` and `--dry-run` write no result file and never run `on_complete`, whether or not they find a change. An `on_complete` failure no longer appears in `warnings[]`: the line and the result files are written before the hook runs, and the failure is reported to the user in one line. A `[MANUAL]` block that no longer matches what was approved is now caught before the new version is published, with `error.phase` `merge:verify-manual-integrity` (where before it stopped later with `write:verify-manual-integrity`), and a headless run that meets a `[MANUAL]` conflict (`merge:conflict-resolution`) now stops before it writes anything. ([#633](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/633); issues [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593), [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585))

  **Migration:** Read `skf_update.status`, and `skf_update.error.phase` and `skf_update.error.reason`, from every line, a stop's included, never a top-level `status`. Treat `taken_action` `deferred-headless` as a path left for a person, and run Update Skill once interactively to promote or skip each one. An `on_complete` hook now also runs after a run that found no change: read `summary.update_status` in the result file it is given to tell the two apart. To learn that the hook failed, check the hook's own exit status (or have the hook report its failure itself) instead of `warnings[]`. Treat `merge:verify-manual-integrity` as the same `[MANUAL]` mismatch as `write:verify-manual-integrity`.

- **skf-update-skill:** A headless Update Skill run given no skill name or folder path now stops before it reads anything, with status `blocked` (`error.phase` `init:skill-name`, `error.reason` starting `input-missing`), where before it had no rule for the missing answer and could wait for one or guess. A skill name passed with the invocation now answers the first question in an interactive run too, which no longer asks it again, and an interactive run given no name offers the skills SKF generated to pick from. ([#633](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/633); issue [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594))

  **Migration:** Pass the skill's name, or the path to its folder, with every headless run, for example `@Ferris US <skill> --headless`.

- **skf-update-skill:** `--detect-only` and `--dry-run` now stop when the skill still uses the old flat layout (`<skills folder>/<name>/SKILL.md` with no version folder). Before, they first moved the skill into the versioned layout, a write in a mode that promises to write nothing. The run ends `blocked` with `error.phase` `init:read-only-flat-layout`, and nothing is moved. ([#499](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/499); issue [#497](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/497))

  **Migration:** Move the skill into the versioned layout once, then re-run with `--detect-only` or `--dry-run`. To move it without updating it, run `@Ferris AS <name>` (Audit Skill) or `@Ferris TS <name>` (Test Skill); `@Ferris US <name>` without the flag also moves it, but runs a full update and writes a new version. Skills already in the versioned layout are not affected.

- **skf-update-skill:** Update Skill now needs `git`, and a commit it can read, to update a skill built at Forge tier or above from a remote repository. When `git` is missing, the tag or branch the skill tracks no longer exists upstream, or the repository cannot be reached and the pinned commit is not on this machine, it stops `blocked` before comparing anything (headless `error.phase` `init:source-tree`, with the reason at the start of `error.reason`). Before, it went on without them and read SKF's clone of the repository at whatever commit it was on. ([#517](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/517); issue [#511](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/511))

  **Migration:** Install `git`, check that you can reach the repository, and re-run. If the tag or branch was deleted or renamed upstream (`ref-not-found`), pass the one to use with `--target-ref <ref>`; the skill then records it. The troubleshooting guide lists every reason and its fix.

- **skf-update-skill:** Update Skill's lock against two updates of one skill at once now works, so `halted-for-concurrent-run` fires while another update of the same skill holds a fresh lock. The lock file, `.skf-update.lock` in the skill's forge data folder, now names the run that holds it and when that run took it. Before, it held the process id of a shell that was gone by the next step, so every later run read the lock as left behind, cleared it and went on, even while the first update was still running. A lock goes stale 60 minutes after it was taken or last renewed, and the next update replaces a stale lock with a `run-lock-replaced` warning. An update renews its lock before it writes the skill and again before it writes the skill's metadata. When its lock went stale while it waited, for example at a confirmation prompt, it stops there with `halted-for-concurrent-run` (`error.phase` `merge:run-lock` or `write:run-lock`, `error.reason` starting `run-lock-lost`), since another update may have changed the skill in the meantime. Every stop after the lock was taken releases it, and a release never removes another update's lock. When another update holds the lock, a headless halt's `error.reason` names that run, since when, the lock file and when the lock goes stale, never a PID. `--detect-only` and `--dry-run` still take no lock. ([#624](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/624); issue [#588](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/588))

  **Migration:** When Update Skill stops with `halted-for-concurrent-run` and no other update of that skill is running, delete the lock file the message names (`<forge data folder>/<skill>/.skf-update.lock`), or wait until the time the message gives, when the lock goes stale; then run the update again. When it stops because its own lock went stale while it waited (`run-lock-lost`), run the update again, first deleting the new version folders the message names, if any. A pipeline that read a PID from `error.reason` reads the run that holds the lock there instead.

- **skf-update-skill:** Update Skill with `--from-test-report` now stops before anything is written, with status `halted-for-remediation-path` (headless `error.phase` `re-extract:targeted-reextraction`), on a gap rated Critical or High, or with no severity it recognizes, for an export the provenance map does not have when the gap has no `file:line` Source that points at the line defining the export and its Remediation names no file to read. The message lists each such gap with `files_scanned: 0`. With `--dry-run` the run stops the same way instead of exiting `dry-run`. If such a gap reaches the provenance write anyway, because that check was skipped, the run stops there with status `blocked` (headless `error.phase` `write:provenance-map`) and writes neither `metadata.json` nor `provenance-map.json`. Before, a real run rewrote `SKILL.md` and only then stopped, with no documented status, which left the skill half repaired; a dry run reported success. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#558](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/558))

  **Migration:** For each gap the message lists, give it a `file:line` Source that points at the line defining the export, or name the source file that defines the export in the gap's Remediation, or lower the gap to Medium, Low or Info if an entry without a source line is acceptable; then re-run Update Skill.

- **skf-verify-stack:** Verify Stack's verdicts and report now keep the shared feasibility-report contract. A run that finds no integration pair between two or more covered technologies reports `CONDITIONALLY_FEASIBLE` instead of `FEASIBLE` (report `overallVerdict`, envelope `overall_verdict`), with a recommendation to describe the integrations in prose, because they were never checked. A pair is capped at `Plausible` only when neither skill cites the other literally: protocol tokens inferred from prose no longer cap a pair at `Plausible`, and a pair whose skills name no protocol is no longer flagged `Risky` (conflicting protocols with no adapter still are), so a pair with a literal citation can reach `Verified`, and a re-run can change per-pair and overall verdicts. `--previous-report` naming the `-latest` report copy (or a link to it) now stops a headless run with exit `5` `previous-report-collision`, and an interactive run asks for another path, because the finished run replaces that copy with its own report, which would then name itself as its previous report; before, the run overwrote that file before it compared, so it was compared with itself and every finding came out unchanged. `skf-report-delta.py` compares each skill's evidence tier (`T1`, `T1-low`, `T2`, `T3`), which the report now records in a table under `## Evidence Sources`, and exits `2` with code `UNKNOWN_TIER` on any other tier token, a forge tier such as `Deep` included, instead of skipping it. The delta now reads both reports' coverage and verdict tables itself, so a previous report it cannot read is named in the delta section instead of compared. `skf-verdict-rollup.py` takes a required `coveredCount` in place of `continuedPastZeroState`. The project slug in the report file name comes from `skf-validate-feasibility-report.py`, the rule Refine Architecture and Stack Skill use to find the report, so a name with accents or punctuation can get a new slug. ([#623](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/623), [#630](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/630); issue [#590](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/590))

  **Migration:** Re-run `@Ferris VS` to refresh your feasibility reports. Pass a timestamped report from an earlier run as `--previous-report`, not the `-latest` copy, or leave the flag out to compare against the newest earlier report. A pipeline that proceeds only on `FEASIBLE` now needs the architecture document to describe in prose how its technologies interact. A caller of `skf-verdict-rollup.py` passes `coveredCount` (the coverage tally's `covered_count`) instead of `continuedPastZeroState`, and a caller of `skf-report-delta.py` passes evidence tiers, not forge tiers.

- **skf-verify-stack:** Verify Stack no longer stops at a vacuous analysis: exit code `8` and halt reason `analysis-halted` are gone. A run with 0% coverage, with every referenced technology marked for removal, or with every integration pair `Blocked` prints one warning and continues, so it ends `NOT_FEASIBLE` with its recommendations in the report, a replacement library or a bridge for each Blocked pair included. The report step no longer ends with the `[R]` review and `[X]` exit menu either: it names the report file, runs `on_complete` and finishes, and you can ask for a walkthrough of any section. A helper the run needs that is missing now halts with exit `3` `resolution-failure` instead of being worked out by hand. ([#623](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/623); issue [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599))

  **Migration:** Stop branching on exit `8` or `analysis-halted`: such a run now finishes with `overall_verdict` `NOT_FEASIBLE`. To stop one early, cancel at the step 1 prompt (exit `6`) or interrupt it. If a run now stops with exit `3` `resolution-failure`, install `uv` or reinstall SKF so the shared helpers resolve.

- **skf-verify-stack:** A circular-dependency row of the report's `| lib_a | lib_b | verdict | rationale |` table now has `cycle` in `lib_a` and the arrow chain (`a → b → c → a`) in `lib_b`, so the next run's delta never merges it into the row of a pair of its skills. Two skills that cite each other, or a loop of skills that all cite each other, no longer add a `Risky` cycle row: a mutual citation is evidence for the pair's own verdict. A stack whose only cycles were mutual citations now counts fewer `Risky` rows, so its `pairsRisky`, `recommendationCount` and `overallVerdict` can change, and it can now reach `FEASIBLE`. ([#640](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/640); issue [#598](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/598))

  **Migration:** Read cycle rows by `lib_a == "cycle"` instead of by the first skill of the chain, and take the chain from `lib_b`. Expect a one-time delta on the first run after upgrading: the earlier cycle rows show as gone and the new ones as new.

- **skf-verify-stack:** When Verify Stack cannot write a result file, `verify-stack-result-<YYYYMMDD-HHmmss>.json` or `verify-stack-result-latest.json` in the forge data folder, the run no longer stops with exit `4` (`write-failed`, status `error`). It finishes with exit `0` and status `success`, and its envelope's `warnings` list `result_file_write_failed` (`result_path` is null when the per-run file is the one that failed), so `verify-stack-result-latest.json` can still hold an earlier run's record. Exit `4` with `write-failed` at the result contract now means only that the shared emitter refused the result payload twice. ([#630](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/630); issue [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))

  **Migration:** A pipeline that treated exit `4` as a missing result file now checks that the envelope's `warnings` hold no `result_file_write_failed` entry and that the `run_id` in `verify-stack-result-latest.json` matches the envelope's, before it reads that file.

- **skf-verify-stack:** Verify Stack drops the `integration_rules_path`, `coverage_patterns_path` and `output_folder_path` settings, and a `_bmad/custom/skf-verify-stack.toml` that sets them is now ignored. The integration rules hold the verdict obligations the shared feasibility report schema puts on the report's producer, so a house-style copy could break what Create Stack Skill and Refine Architecture read. Coverage now takes the skills a document names from the shared mentions helper, and the other names a skill goes by come from `persistent_facts`, so the bundled coverage patterns are no longer an override surface. The report always lands in `forge_data_folder`, where Create Stack Skill, Refine Architecture and the next Verify Stack run look for it. The `report_template_path` comment now names what a copy of the report template must keep. ([#640](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/640); issues [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596), [#590](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/590))

  **Migration:** Remove `integration_rules_path`, `coverage_patterns_path` and `output_folder_path` from `_bmad/custom/skf-verify-stack.toml` and `_bmad/custom/skf-verify-stack.user.toml`. Give the other names your documents use for a skill as a `persistent_facts` entry instead, for example "Our documents call the postgresql skill Postgres or PG.". To keep reports somewhere else, change `forge_data_folder` in `_bmad/skf/config.yaml`, which moves the report and the workflows that read it together.

### Added

- **skf-analyze-source:** A headless step-by-step Analyze Source run now records the default each step's menu took in the envelope's `headless_decisions`, with five new values of its `gate`: `scan-project.scan`, `identify-units.classifications`, `map-and-detect.findings` (with the composite merges it accepted), `recommend.recommendations` and `generate-briefs.write`. An `AN[auto]` run also records in `warnings` a version pin it could not resolve (`pin_unresolved`) and the `-wiki` rename of a split package whose name an existing skill already holds. ([#637](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/637); issue [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594))
- **skf-analyze-source:** The result envelope's `headless_decisions` can now carry the gate `auto-scope.shape`: an `AN[auto]` or `forge-auto` run records there whether it scoped a package whose only app signal is a framework dependency as an application (`reference-app`, the default) or as a library (`library-API`), with the sentence of the manifest description or README that decided. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-audit-skill:** A headless Audit Skill run that continues past the baseline confirmation now records that decision in its envelope's `headless_decisions`, as gate `init.baseline-confirm`, with the doubt that raised it: the tier the skill was compiled at, the tier of this run and the age of the provenance map in days. Step 1's other two gates already recorded theirs. ([#649](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/649); issue [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599))
- **skf-audit-skill:** The audit drift report lists the public API a package entry point exports from files outside the skill's scope, in an `Out-of-Scope New Public API` table under Remediation Suggestions, so update-skill's scope reconciliation now gets its candidates from the latest audit instead of always finding none. Audit Skill's re-index now extracts through the shared recipe runner, `skf-extract-public-api.py --mode full`, in one call over the files the provenance map covers, and a script builds `extraction-snapshot.json` from its result and the exports read by eye: each file gets a status (`extracted`, `read-by-eye`, `hash-tracked`, `missing`, `parse-failed` or `unread`), a script, asset or doc the map tracks by content hash is compared by hash and never read for exports, and the audit never diffs an incomplete snapshot. The relocation check of the Forge+ and Deep tiers runs the same runner over the files that may hold a removed export and adds what it finds to the snapshot through the same script. ([#633](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/633); issue [#589](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/589))
- **skf-brief-skill:** The `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` envelope may now carry a `warnings` list, an optional property added to `skf-brief-result-envelope.v1.json`. It holds the run's non-fatal warnings, one string each, such as `customization_resolver_unavailable` when the overrides under `_bmad/custom/` could not be applied. The list appears only when the run recorded at least one warning, so an envelope without warnings is unchanged. A pipeline that checks envelopes against its own copy of the v1 schema, which refuses unknown properties, should take the updated copy. ([#616](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/616); issues [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593), [#595](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/595))
- **skf-brief-skill:** The Brief Skill target prompt now takes a package name or a registry page (`zod`, `requests==2.31.0`, an npmjs.com, pypi.org or crates.io URL) and resolves it to its GitHub repository, offering every candidate when the name is ambiguous. A GitHub `/tree/<ref>/<folder>` URL sets the brief's `target_ref` and a scope hint for the folder, a repository on another git host gets a pointer to clone it first, and a relative path that exists, such as `briefs/skill-brief.yaml` or `libs/mylib`, is read as a local path whatever its shape. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issue [#582](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/582))
- **skf-brief-skill:** Monorepo detection recognizes Nx: a repository with an `nx.json` at its root and `project.json` project folders is reported as a monorepo of `manifest_kind` `nx`, each project folder a workspace. The detector also reads a Rush repository's `rush.json` (`manifest_kind` `rush`) once its content is passed with the other root manifests. Brief Skill does not pass it yet, so on a Rush repository the detector warns that `rush.json` was not supplied and detection goes on as before. ([#622](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/622); issue [#584](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/584))
- **skf-campaign:** Campaign now records what its Export stage did for each skill. After each `skf-export-skill` call, Export writes the outcome to `_campaign-state.yaml` through the new `campaign-state.py set-skill --export exported|failed` (with `--export-halt-reason` and `--export-exit-code` for a failure), as a skill's new optional `export` field. The headless `SKF_CAMPAIGN_RESULT_JSON` line gains two optional fields, `skills_exported` (the skills Export exported) and `export_failures` (each skill whose export failed, with its `halt_reason` and `exit_code`), both null when the Export stage did not run, and the Export Gate table of `campaign-report.md` gains an Export column. A failed export still ends the campaign with `status` `success`, so a CI job that needs every skill exported checks `export_failures`; before, the only trace of a failed export was a line in the decision log, and the report and the envelope showed only the gate verdicts. ([#655](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/655))
- **skf-campaign:** `src/shared/scripts/schemas/skf-campaign-result-envelope.v1.json` is the new schema of the `SKF_CAMPAIGN_RESULT_JSON` envelope and its one definition: the shared emitter (`skf-emit-result-envelope.py --workflow skf-campaign`) builds and checks every campaign envelope against it, the success line at the end and the error line at every HARD HALT, so the model no longer types it. The success line gains `exit_code` and `halt_reason` (`0` and null; `10` and `report-failure` when the report could not be generated), and the error line now carries every field the success line does, with `halt_reason` beside `phase` and `error`. In headless mode the success line is held until the end-of-run health check, which displays it as the run's last line, so a `claude -p` caller reads the envelope as the final message. ([#641](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/641); issues [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593), [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585))
- **skf-create-skill:** Create Skill now checks the final `description` for `<` and `>`, which the Claude platform does not accept in a skill description, replaces them with `{` and `}`, and checks again. If any remain, it stops with `description-angle-brackets` before anything is written to the skills folder. To avoid this, write placeholders in a brief as `{name}` or `NAME`, not `<name>`. ([#517](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/517); issue [#513](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/513))
- **skf-create-skill:** Create Skill's `SKF_CREATE_SKILL_RESULT_JSON` line now has a schema, `skf-create-skill-result-envelope.v1.json`, and the shared emitter builds and checks every line. Each brief that finishes prints one on stdout, and every HARD HALT of steps 1 to 7 prints one on stderr, in every mode, where only some halts of a headless run printed one before. Each halt exits with a code: `2` for a missing, malformed or invalid brief, `3` for a missing forge configuration, source, helper or documentation, `4` for a failed write, `5` for a refused or broken package, and `6` when the user stops to refine the brief's scope. The line carries `exit_code`, `halt_reason`, `error`, `timestamp`, `run_id`, `result_path`, `headless_decisions` and `warnings`, and keeps `summary.halt_reason` and `summary.evidence_report` at a halt. The emitter writes the result files and stamps their name and timestamp from the clock. The run records each decision it takes without asking as the gate decides, in a run folder under `_bmad-output/.skf-run/` that is deleted when a brief finishes and kept after a halt: every gate a headless run passes by its default, the extraction review (`review-gate`) included, and the tier override in any mode. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issue [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))
- **skf-create-skill:** The extraction review gate, when it shows the zero-export or head-cap warning, and the zero-export gate after a failed documentation fetch offer `[R] Refine the brief`, which stops the run with `halted-for-brief-refinement` (exit code `6`) and, under `--batch`, ends only that brief. A headless run still continues past both gates. ([#656](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/656))
- **skf-create-skill:** Running Create Skill again for a version it already built now keeps what you wrote by hand in that build: each `[MANUAL]` section of its SKILL.md goes into the new SKILL.md before validation, and the files in its `scripts/[MANUAL]/` and `assets/[MANUAL]/` folders go into the new package. Before, the re-run replaced the package whole and deleted them without notice. The run records a `manual-carry-forward` decision, and its final report lists the sections and files it kept under Warnings. If a section does not reach the new SKILL.md, the earlier SKILL.md is kept in `manual-backup/` in the version's forge data folder, as `SKILL-<UTC time>.md` so a later run never replaces it, with a warning that names the section. The promotion helper, `skf-promote-staged.py`, gains a `--carry-manual` option for this. ([#661](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/661))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-audit-skill, skf-update-skill:** Create Skill has three new ast-grep recipes, so these TypeScript exports are found by ast-grep (`T1`) instead of being read by eye (`T1-low`) or missed: `js-local-exports` finds each name of a local export list such as the `export { Button, buttonVariants }` that ends a shadcn/ui component file, `react-wrapped-components` finds PascalCase components wrapped in `memo`, `forwardRef` or `lazy`, and `ts-exported-types` finds exported interfaces, type aliases and enums, generic, `extends` and `export declare` forms included. Component-library extraction runs all three, and takes its shared types from `ts-exported-types` instead of patterns that missed those forms and listed the members of an `export namespace` block as exports. Audit Skill and Update Skill run the same recipes, so the first audit or update of a TypeScript skill created before this release can report these exports as new. ([#622](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/622); issue [#559](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/559))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-brief-skill:** When you confirm or correct the demo files Create Skill leaves out of a component library, or confirm or name its registry file, the run now records your answer in the brief's `scope.demo_patterns` or `scope.registry_path` through the new `skf-write-skill-brief.py amend`, which keeps the previous brief as `skill-brief.yaml.bak` and adds a `scope.amendments` entry with the new `demo-and-registry` category, so the next run of the same brief asks neither question and a later headless run uses the registry you named instead of scoring candidates again. A registry path in the brief that can no longer be read is replaced the same way once you confirm or name another. A headless run's automatic choices are never written back. The new `skf-detect-registry.py` lists the files in the brief's scope, counts the demo files, scores the registry candidates and lists the chosen registry's entries with their lines, and a headless run accepts a candidate only when it scores 7 or more out of 9. When the brief sets `scope.demo_patterns`, the run also names the demo files of the usual kinds those patterns leave in. `skill-brief.v1.json` now lists `scope.registry_path`, `scope.demo_patterns`, `scope.ui_variants` and `scope.amendments`, and `skf-write-skill-brief.py` accepts and writes the three component-library fields. A missing field inside an object of the brief, such as a `scope.rationale` with no `reason`, is now reported by its full path (`scope.rationale.reason`). ([#628](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/628); issue [#605](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/605))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-quick-skill, skf-create-stack-skill:** Create Skill, Quick Skill and Stack Skill now refuse, with `not-skf-output`, to write into a skill folder SKF did not generate (another module's skill with the same name, for example), and write nothing into it; before, they wrote their new version into it. For pipelines, Quick Skill now exits `9` and Stack Skill exits `5` on this refusal, both named `state-conflict`. A `--batch` run of Create Skill stops at a brief it refuses and records it under `refused` in `batch-state.yaml`; the next `--batch` run resumes with the brief after it. ([#509](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/509); issue [#501](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/501))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-test-skill:** Tessl Review is now opt-in. Tessl retired `tessl skill review`, so the review Create Skill and Test Skill ran had stopped working. To turn it on, set `tessl_review_workspace` in the forger preferences (`_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/preferences.yaml`) to your Tessl workspace name; that setting is your consent to upload the skill's `SKILL.md`, `references/`, `scripts/` and `assets/`, and it is off by default, headless runs included. Create Skill records the review as advice (its content-quality gate is gone) and Test Skill uses the score in External Validation. It needs the `tessl` CLI and a Tessl login (`tessl login` or `TESSL_TOKEN`), and each fresh review spends Tessl credits. ([#517](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/517); issue [#513](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/513))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill now declares and binds the headless inputs `skills` (the libraries to rank in code mode, or the constituent skills in compose mode, each a skill name or a package path, which `skf-enumerate-stack-skills.py candidates --explicit` now accepts), `stack_name` (the stack's skill name and folder, with `-stack` appended when it lacks it; `{project_name}-stack` when absent), `scope_overrides` and `architecture_doc_path`, beside `project_path` and `mode` (an explicit `mode` wins over `architecture_doc_path`), so a campaign capstone can name its constituents and its stack. A headless run given `skills` in a project with no manifests composes those of them SKF generated, as before, and ranks them as libraries when none is an SKF skill instead of stopping with `resolution-failure`. Compose mode picks and gates its constituent skills with `skf-enumerate-stack-skills.py candidates`, and the first step decides the mode from that helper and `skf-scan-manifests.py`. The `SKF_STACK_RESULT_JSON` envelope and the result contract's `summary` gain `quality_score`, the skill-check score of the committed stack (`null` when skill-check did not run and on every error; it is not a test-skill score). An input the run cannot use (a `project_path` that is no folder, an `architecture_doc_path` that is not a readable file, a `stack_name` that `skf-validate-frontmatter.py --check-name` refuses once `-stack` is appended, such as one over 64 characters or with two hyphens in a row, an unknown `mode` or `scope_overrides` value) stops it before any question with exit `2` and the new halt reason `input-invalid`. ([#635](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/635); issues [#586](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/586), [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594), [#606](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/606))
- **skf-export-skill:** Export lists `--context-file` in its invocation contract, which now lives in `references/invocation-contract.md`, and resolves a snippet-root mismatch in a headless run by checking the disk: it keeps the root an earlier export chose, for that run, when only that folder holds the skills, and uses the IDE skill folder otherwise. Option (d), the earlier root for this run only, now reaches the snippets and the managed section. A draft snippet's `skills/<name>/` root no longer counts as a layout choice, so exporting a new or updated skill no longer raises the mismatch warning; when no earlier export chose a root, an interactive run asks one layout question and a headless run takes the IDE skill folder, recorded under the new `load-skill.snippet-root-layout` gate of `headless_decisions`. A repo whose skills live in `skills/` sets `snippet_skill_root_override: skills/` in `config.yaml`, since a `skills/<name>/` root an earlier export wrote now reads as a draft root. Export finds every helper it cannot run without, the skill inventory included, before its first prompt, so a missing one halts with exit code 4 (`context-rebuild-failed`) before you answer any question. ([#639](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/639); issues [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594), [#600](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/600))
- **skf-export-skill:** The `SKF_EXPORT_RESULT_JSON` envelope now has a schema, `skf-export-result-envelope.v1.json`, and the shared emitter builds and checks every line, a halt's included. The envelope gains `result_path` (the per-run result file, or `null` when none was written), `warnings` (present only when the run recorded one) and, on a halt, `error` with the `phase` and `reason` of the stop. Each headless gate records its auto-decision the moment it decides, so `headless_decisions` lists every gate that auto-resolved, also in a halt's envelope, and the result file now carries `headless_decisions` too. The result file's name and `timestamp` come from the clock, and a run that ends in the same second as another gets a `-2` suffix instead of overwriting its record. A headless run keeps its decisions in a scratch folder under `_bmad-output/.skf-run/`, deleted when the run ends and kept after a halt. ([#629](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/629); issue [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))
- **skf-forger:** Typing `CA` to Ferris now starts Campaign, matching the `[CA] Campaign` entry bmad-help already showed. Campaign still cannot be chained with other codes in a pipeline. ([#523](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/523))
- **skf-forger:** A chain of codes whose first workflow lacks its input, such as `QS TS EX` or `QS TS EX --pin 1.2.0`, no longer starts a pipeline that stops at that workflow for it. Ferris now asks for the input first and runs the chain with it in brackets, as `QS[cocoindex] TS EX`: a project path for `AN`, a target for `QS`, an architecture document for `VS` and `RA`, and a skill name for `CS`, `TS`, `EX`, `US` and `AS`, looking past a leading `SF`. A bare `BS CS TS EX` asks for a target and a skill name and runs as `forge <repo-url-or-path> <skill-name>`. A headless run asks nothing and stops before any workflow runs, with a halt reason such as `QS needs a target before the pipeline can start: give it as QS[<target>]` in a failed pipeline result, and leaves no stopped chain to resume. A chain no bracket can complete (one led by `RS` or `DS`, any other chain led by `BS`, or a first code whose bracket already holds `min:N` or `auto`) stops there the same way, with a reason that names what no bracket can give, and asks for another invocation when it is not headless. A headless chain led by `CS` still leaves the brief to create-skill, which compiles the only brief and halts naming them when there are several. ([#652](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/652); issue [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594))
- **skf-forger:** `skf-forger` now ships a `customize.toml` with an `[agent]` block (`code`, `name`, `title`, `icon`, `description` and `agent_type`), and SKF's `module.yaml` lists Ferris under `agents`, so a BMAD Method install counts Ferris as an agent: GitHub Copilot's Custom Agents picker now lists him, and `_bmad/config.toml` gets an `[agents.skf-forger]` entry that `_bmad/custom/config.toml` can override like any other agent's. The values copy `bmad-skill-manifest.yaml` and are read-only at runtime: Ferris's persona and menu stay in his `SKILL.md`. ([#631](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/631); issue [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596))
- **skf-forger:** Ferris now keeps a pipeline's state on disk as it runs: a journal, `pipeline-journal.json`, in the run's own folder under `_bmad-output/.skf-run/`, written when the chain starts and after every workflow, with the plan and its bracket values, the alias, the arguments and what each workflow hands the next. When a pipeline stops, its summary names the next action, and the next time you start Ferris he offers it: after an interruption or an error, to pick the chain up at the step it stopped on with that plan, so a resumed `forge-auto` still tests at 90 and skips no step; after a quality halt, its repair route instead, such as `US <name> --from-test-report` and then `TS` and `EX` at the recorded threshold after a `FAIL`. The offer goes away once you test or export that skill yourself, another pipeline records its result, or you ask Ferris to drop it, and `WS` shows it as the one next action for that skill. `pipeline-result-latest.json` keeps its fields and its `summary.status` values, and gains `run_id`, `summary.alias` and `summary.steps`, one entry per step of the plan in a fixed shape; a chain whose journal step fails still writes it. Ferris also runs a code, alias or chain given with the invocation at once, such as `@Ferris TS cocoindex`, instead of first showing the menu, and a start with `--headless` that names nothing to run stops with the form to use instead of ending at the menu. ([#631](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/631); issues [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587), [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593), [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594))
- **skf-quick-skill:** Quick Skill now takes `--language-hint <lang>` and `--scope-hint <path>` on a single-target run, the hints a batch line gives with `language=` and `scope=`: the gates that told a user to re-run with `--language-hint` now point at a flag the run parses, and a target passed as an argument skips the target prompt in an interactive run too. The multi-language gate now offers every detected language to pick from, where it offered only the first one or an abort, and a language hint given at the zero-exports retry or at the review's rescope is reported as `language_resolution` `hint`. Step 1 lists the repository once through the GitHub probe, which falls back to GitHub's unauthenticated API when gh is missing or logged out, and step 3 fetches the files it reads from that listing into the run folder (from `raw.githubusercontent.com`, or through gh for a private repository) and hands them to the extractor by path; a repository no probe can list stops in step 1 with exit code `3` (`resolution-failure`) and the probe's message. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issues [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592), [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594))
- **skf-quick-skill:** Quick Skill recognizes a skills module, a repository that ships agent skills rather than code, such as a BMAD module or a plain Agent Skills package. It takes the skills from the folder `scope_hint` names, else from the repository root or a top-level folder that holds `module.yaml` or `module-help.csv` (with the skills at any depth below it), or from a folder of skill folders when the repository's own manifest publishes no code, so a library that ships its agent skill beside its code stays a library. It records `repo_shape: skills-module`, reads each skill's frontmatter and the module's `module-help.csv` when there is one, and lists the skill names and menu codes as the skill's Key Exports and in the `exports` of `metadata.json`, with Usage Patterns taken from the `module-help.csv` rows. Such a repository used to pass as a library and give a README-only skill with no exports. When a run that stays a library finds no exports but the repository holds skill folders, the extraction note names the folder to re-run with as `scope=`. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#527](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/527))
- **skf-quick-skill:** Quick Skill now ends a finished run with a `SKF_QUICK_SKILL_RESULT_JSON` line on stdout, where only its HARD HALTs printed one before, and the shared emitter builds every such line, a halt's included, and checks it against the new `skf-quick-skill-result-envelope.v1.json` schema. The success line gives `skill_package`, the files written in `outputs`, and a `summary` with `quality_score` (the skill-check score of the written package, null when skill-check did not run; not a test-skill score), `confidence`, `repo_shape`, `language_resolution`, `detected_languages`, `zero_exports_rescue`, `active_pointer`, `exports_documented` and `validation_issues`. Every line now also carries `halt_reason`, `timestamp`, `run_id`, `result_path`, `headless_decisions` (each gate a headless run resolved by its default, such as overwriting an existing package, recorded as it decides) and `warnings`, among them `also_found_in` when a headless run kept the first registry's pick of a package name another registry also holds; `quick-skill-result-latest.json` carries the same decisions and warnings. With `--batch`, each `results[]` entry of the batch summary carries its target's `quality_score` and `batch` number, and the summary and its `batch_summary` event carry the batch's `exit_code`. A run keeps its staged payloads in a run folder under `_bmad-output/.skf-run/`, deleted when it finishes; a run that cannot create that folder stops at once with exit code `4` (`write-failure`). A headless run started with no target now stops with exit code `2` (`input-invalid`) and its envelope, where it stopped with neither. ([#627](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/627); issues [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585), [#586](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/586), [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))
- **skf-refine-architecture:** `SKF_REFINE_ARCHITECTURE_RESULT_JSON` now has a schema, `skf-refine-architecture-result-envelope.v1.json`, and the shared emitter builds and checks every line, each halt's included, so a halt in any step prints the same shape even when the skill's own instructions are no longer in context. The envelope gains `previous_refined_path` (the name an earlier refined document was renamed to, or `null`), `previous_pass` (the input held an earlier Refine Architecture pass, which this run replaced), `unverified_count` (the technologies the architecture names that no skill covers), `run_id`, `result_path` (the per-run `refine-architecture-result-<YYYYMMDD-HHmmss>.json`, `null` on a halt), on a halt `error` with the step and gate it stopped at, `headless_decisions` (the [VS] report a headless run used or went without, the document scope it kept and the review it approved), and `warnings` when the run recorded one (`malformed_ra_markers`, `legacy_blocks_set_aside`, `unknown_scope_skills`, `scope_fallback_all_skills`, `result_file_write_failed`); the result file carries both lists too. Two halt reasons are new: `preservation-failed` (exit `4`: the draft would lose or change a line of the architecture document, so it was not promoted) and `resolution-failure` (exit `3`: the shared emitter or the preservation script cannot run). The gap, issue and improvement counts come from the plan the preservation script applied, never typed. The result files take their name and timestamp from the clock, and a run that ends in the same second as another gets a `-2` suffix. A run keeps its working files and its decisions in a scratch folder under `_bmad-output/.skf-run/`, deleted when the run finishes and kept after a halt. ([#633](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/633), [#645](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/645); issue [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))
- **skf-rename-skill:** The new `--acknowledge-official` flag lets one headless run rename a skill whose `source_authority` is `official`, and the run records that decision in its envelope's `headless_decisions`. Without the flag, or the standing `force_source_authority_in_headless` setting (which now also accepts the TOML boolean `true`), the run still stops with `source-authority-blocked`, and the message names the flag. An interactive run now takes the current and new names given with the invocation, such as "rename cognee to cognee-ai", instead of asking for them again: it asks only for a name that is missing or that fails its check. A missing manifest or inventory helper now stops the run before its first question with exit code `4` (`write-failed`), where before the run read the manifest by hand or refused the skill with `not-skf-output`. The new-name check and the version listing, which the run used to do by hand when their helper could not run, now also stop with exit code `4` when their helper prints nothing. A `persistent_facts` entry that starts with `!` now drops the entry it names, so `persistent_facts = ["!file:{project-root}/**/project-context.md"]` in `{project-root}/_bmad/custom/skf-rename-skill.toml` stops Rename Skill from loading every `project-context.md`, which it loads by default. ([#639](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/639); issues [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594), [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596))
- **skf-rename-skill:** `SKF_RENAME_SKILL_RESULT_JSON` now has a schema, `skf-rename-skill-result-envelope.v1.json`, and the shared emitter builds and checks every line from it: the finished rename and the `--dry-run` exit on stdout, and each halt on stderr, a halt before the run folder exists included. Every envelope gains `run_id` and `result_path` (the per-run result file, null on a dry run and on a halt), a halt envelope gains `error` with the phase, the message and the path the halt names, and an envelope carries `warnings` when the run recorded one, such as `run-lock-replaced`, or `run-lock-not-released` when a halt could not release the run lock. A pipeline that checks envelopes against its own copy of the schema should take the installed one. ([#629](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/629); issues [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593), [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585))
- **skf-setup:** Setup's `blocked` `SKF_SETUP_RESULT_JSON` envelope has two new `error.phase` values: `step <N>:helper-missing` (a helper script is missing from the install) and `step 1:detect-tools` (tool detection failed). On Windows machines with no `python3`, the halts that come before the `uv` check now print their envelope too: Setup tries `uv run`, `python3`, `python` and `py -3` in turn. When none of them can run the envelope helper, or SKF's scripts are not installed, a halt prints only its reason, so treat a missing envelope as a failure. ([#499](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/499), [#509](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/509); issues [#494](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/494), [#506](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/506))
- **skf-test-skill:** `SKF_TEST_RESULT_JSON` now has a schema, `skf-test-result-envelope.v1.json`, and the shared emitter builds and checks every line, a halt's included, from a payload `build-result-context.py` derives from the report, the gap ledger and the scoring output, so no step types the envelope or the result contract.
