Every envelope gains `run_id` and `result_path` (the per-run result file, null on a halt), a halt's envelope gains `error` (`phase`, `reason`, and `path` when the halt names one), and `warnings` appears when the run recorded one, such as `customization_resolver_unavailable` when the customization resolver could not run; the result file gains `skill`, `timestamp`, `run_id`, `headless_decisions` and `warnings`. The envelope is a headless run's last line: the shared health check displays it after everything else. A skill run cannot set the exit status of the agent that runs it, so automators read `exit_code` from the envelope or from `skf-test-skill-result-latest.json`. The headless contract moved to the skill's `references/invocation-contract.md`, which also lists the `--headless`, `-H` and `--discovery-catalog=all` flags the workflow already took. ([#637](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/637); issues [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593), [#600](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/600))
- **skf-update-skill:** A headless `--from-test-report` run now records how it answered each rescope question (document the export, or take it out of the skill's scope when the test report says to remove it) in the result line's `headless_decisions`, under the new `gate` value `gap-driven.rescope`, with the gap's id, the export and the remediation as `evidence`. The invocation contract's Gates row lists the gate. Before, the run took this decision without recording it, although the contract said every headless gate decision is recorded. ([#663](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/663))
- **skf-update-skill:** A `persistent_facts` entry that starts with `!` now drops the entry it names, so `persistent_facts = ["!file:{project-root}/**/project-context.md"]` in `{project-root}/_bmad/custom/skf-update-skill.toml` stops Update Skill from loading every `project-context.md`, which it loads by default. ([#638](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/638); issue [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596))
- **skf-update-skill:** New `--target-ref <ref>` flag for a skill built at Forge tier or above from a remote repository: update it against a tag, branch, `HEAD` or full commit instead of the ref it recorded, for example the newer tag Audit Skill checked out. When the update writes, the skill records that ref and the commit it read, and the Audit Skill report now suggests the flag after it checked out a newer ref. On any other skill (built from a local folder, from documentation only, or at Quick tier) the flag stops the run with `target-ref-needs-remote-source`. ([#517](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/517); issue [#511](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/511))
- **skf-verify-stack:** `SKF_VERIFY_STACK_RESULT_JSON` now has a schema, `skf-verify-stack-result-envelope.v1.json`, and the shared emitter builds and checks every line, each halt's included, so a halt in any stage prints the same shape even when the skill's own instructions are no longer in context. Every envelope gains `run_id`, the run's timestamp (the one in the report's file name), and `result_path`, the per-run `verify-stack-result-<YYYYMMDD-HHmmss>.json` file (null on a halt). A halt's envelope gains `error`, with the stage and gate it stopped at (`phase`) and its message, and keeps `report_latest_path` null unless the finished report was already published. `headless_decisions` lists the earlier report a headless run chose to compare against, and `warnings` appears when the run recorded one (`no_technology_referenced`, `no_live_technology` or `zero_coverage` when no referenced technology has a skill, `skill_modified_mid_run` when a skill changed while the run read it, `all_pairs_blocked` when every integration pair is Blocked) or the emitter raised one; the result file carries both lists too. The result files take their name and timestamp from the clock, and a run that ends in the same second as another gets a `-2` suffix. A pipeline that checks envelopes against its own copy of the schema should take the installed one. ([#630](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/630); issue [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))
- **skf-verify-stack:** Verify Stack now writes the report's table rows from the rows it counted instead of typing them a second time. `skf-coverage-tally.py --render` prints the Coverage Analysis rows from the coverage rows file, with `none` in the Source Section or Skill Match cell of a technology that has none, and the canonical `| lib_a | lib_b | verdict | rationale |` rows from `verdict-rows.json`, which now holds each pair's rationale, with one `cycle` row per circular dependency; each cell stays on one line, a `|` in it escaped. The coverage tally now refuses a Covered row that names no skill, or a Missing or Replaced row that names one, so a rows file it counts also renders. The overall verdict now comes from `skf-verdict-rollup.py --report`, which reads the counts from the report's frontmatter itself. A count a step left out, or left at the template's `null` or empty value, now stops the run with exit `5` (`schema-violation`, phase `synthesize:rollup`) instead of counting as `0`. So does a report whose `stepsCompleted` does not list `coverage`, `integrations` and `requirements`, since the template starts `coveragePercentage` and the pair counts at `0`. A missing shared report reader stops it with exit `3` (`resolution-failure`, the same phase). When subagents are unavailable, the integration step reads the skills' `SKILL.md` files one at a time instead. ([#644](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/644); issues [#598](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/598), [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592))

### Changed

- **all workflows:** Every workflow that loads the bundled `persistent_facts` default (every `project-context.md` under the project) now honours an entry that starts with `!`: it loads nothing and drops each earlier entry it names. So `persistent_facts = ["!file:{project-root}/**/project-context.md"]` in `{project-root}/_bmad/custom/<skill>.toml` turns that default off for the workflow. Each `customize.toml` shows that override above its `persistent_facts`, where some said an override could not remove the default. ([#650](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/650); issue [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596))
- **all workflows:** SKF now moves, updates, renames or deletes only the skills it generated, in both `skills_output_folder` and `forge_data_folder`, so a skills folder shared with other skills is supported. It recognizes its skills by the SKF marker in their `metadata.json` (such as `generated_by`), which every SKF release has written. On a skill SKF did not make, Update Skill, Export Skill, Audit Skill and Test Skill stop with `not-skf-output` instead of moving it into the versioned layout (Update Skill's headless envelope names `init:ownership-gate`), and Drop Skill and Rename Skill refuse it with exit `5`. Export Skill's skill list (with no skill named, or `--all` before a first export) and Analyze Source's `[M]erge` choice now offer only SKF skills. Rename Skill also stops with `flat-layout` on an SKF skill still in the old flat layout, which it could not rename before either, and says to run `@Ferris TS <name>` (Test Skill) on it once. ([#499](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/499), [#509](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/509); issues [#497](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/497), [#500](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/500))
- **docs site:** The documentation site now shows the latest stable release. It is built from the release tag and deployed at the end of each stable release, and a merge to `main` no longer redeploys it, so the site no longer describes changes that are not released yet. A prerelease leaves the site as it is. ([#578](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/578); issue [#556](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/556))
- **packaging:** `package.json` no longer declares `main`, so `require('bmad-module-skill-forge')` now fails with `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` instead of running the installer CLI against the calling program's arguments, and `import('bmad-module-skill-forge')` fails with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. `require.resolve('bmad-module-skill-forge')` fails the same way; to find the installed package, resolve `bmad-module-skill-forge/package.json`, which still works. Requiring the package was never a supported use, and `npx bmad-module-skill-forge` works as before. The npm package also stops shipping `.gitattributes` and `.nvmrc`, which only the SKF repository uses. ([#620](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/620); issue [#567](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/567))
- **releases:** Release notes and the `CHANGELOG.md` entry now describe each user-visible change once, in plain words, grouped under Breaking changes, Added, Changed, Fixed and Documentation, and every breaking change says what to do. They no longer list commit subjects, so merge and maintenance commits stay out. ([#578](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/578); issue [#556](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/556))
- **requirements:** Every SKF helper script now declares `requires-python = ">=3.11"` in its uv header, the Python minimum the docs already state. 78 scripts still declared 3.9 or 3.10, so in a project pinned to Python 3.10 (a `.python-version` file) `uv run` ran them on 3.10; it now runs every helper on Python 3.11 or newer, fetching one when uv is allowed to download Python. Where uv may not download Python and only an older one is installed, `uv run` now stops with an error naming the requirement (`No interpreter found for Python >=3.11`): install Python 3.11 or newer. ([#642](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/642); issue [#576](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/576))
- **requirements:** The prerequisites table in Getting Started now lists every tool SKF uses, with `git`, `tessl` and `skill-check` added, and gains a Minimum and a Tested on column: the oldest version SKF supports and the versions SKF's own runs use, such as Node.js 22 (tested on 22 and 24), Python 3.11 (tested on 3.11 and 3.12), git 2.15 and ast-grep 0.45.3. A tool with no minimum yet reads `none`. The table is generated from `src/shared/tool-requirements.yaml`, the one list of tool versions, which the installer copies to `_bmad/skf/shared/` with the rest of the shared files. ([#615](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/615); issue [#576](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/576))
- **skf-analyze-source:** After an `AN[auto]` run that merged into or skipped an existing skill, or one that was interrupted, a plain `AN` of the same target now archives that analysis in `forge_data_folder` as `analyze-source-report-<project>-<YYYYMMDD-HHmmss>.md` and starts the step-by-step analysis. Before, it went back into automatic scoping: it asked the merge, alongside or skip question again, a headless run wrote a `-wiki` brief that the skip had declined, and an interrupted `--pin` run lost its pin. An `[auto]` run that switched to the step-by-step analysis still resumes. ([#665](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/665); issue [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587))
- **skf-analyze-source:** Analyze Source now proposes composite units once it knows how the units import each other, and carries each approved composite into its brief. The import graph comes from the shared helpers instead of hand counts: which unit imports which, from how many files and through which files, which units are imported together in 3 or more files, which import each other in a cycle, and which external dependencies each unit's files import. A Maven, Gradle, Composer or Swift unit is matched by the packages, namespaces or targets it declares, so imports between such units count too. Each unit's exports come from the ast-grep recipe runner for the languages it has recipes for (Python, JavaScript and TypeScript, Rust, Go), and are read by eye where ast-grep is missing or the language has no recipe, such as Java, Kotlin or Swift. Composite merges are proposed after that graph is built, in the same pass that flags stack skill candidates, so a group of units is never both. A monorepo whose facade package re-exports its members, whose members share one runtime contract, or whose members are private building blocks now gets a merge proposal on the interactive path too, and a headless run accepts it, so it writes one brief instead of one per package. The analysis report records the approved composites in `confirmed_composites`, and each composite's brief lists every constituent's path in `scope.include`. ([#627](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/627); issues [#602](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/602), [#598](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/598), [#584](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/584))
- **skf-analyze-source:** Running Analyze Source again after an analysis finished now starts a fresh analysis, in interactive and headless mode: the finished report (one whose steps include writing the briefs, or the auto scope) is archived beside it as `analyze-source-report-<project>-<YYYYMMDD-HHmmss>.md`, where before the run resumed it and stopped at the health check. Only an unfinished report is resumed, and an `[auto]` run archives any earlier report instead of overwriting it. A unit that only has a brief from an earlier analysis is analyzed again (its status is `briefed`, and its brief is rewritten if you confirm the unit): only a compiled skill SKF generated makes a unit already skilled. An `[auto]` run that falls back to the step-by-step analysis now marks its report as interactive, so a resumed session continues that analysis instead of restarting the auto scope, and its envelopes from then on carry `mode: interactive`. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issue [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587))
- **skf-analyze-source:** Analyze Source no longer drops a whole unit because one of its files looks generated. A committed lockfile such as `Cargo.lock` (which opens with `@generated`), a source folder named `src/build/`, a Java package folder named `build/` or a Go `vendor/` folder used to disqualify the crate, package or module around it. A `build/`, `dist/` or `target/` folder now counts as build output only directly below a unit or beside a manifest, and a unit drops as `generated-code` or `auto-generated-tag` only when every one of its source files is generated; a unit with some generated files stays, with those files listed as evidence the workflow weighs, in headless runs too, and a unit dropped over files that are its own code comes back when it is large enough with them. The size rules (fewer than 3 source files or 100 lines of code) now count only the source files that are not generated, so a lockfile, a README, a manifest such as `setup.py` or `build.gradle`, or a tool configuration file such as `vite.config.ts` no longer lifts a small unit over them. Units and briefs are also named by one rule in auto and interactive runs, and the check for an existing skill looks up that same name: a unit takes the name its own manifest gives its package (`@trpc/server` gives `trpc-server`, `github.com/spf13/cobra` gives `cobra`) unless the package is private, a merged monorepo or composite takes its facade's name or else the name its packages share (`aws-sdk` for the `@aws-sdk/*` packages), and any other unit takes its repository or folder name (`.` names the current folder) and a documentation URL its host (`docs-example-com`). Two units whose names would clash are told apart by their parent folders (`server-api` and `client-api`). A monorepo the auto path splits into several briefs now names each after its package instead of prefixing the package with the project name, and a package whose name an existing skill from another source already holds gets the `-wiki` suffix. ([#627](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/627); issue [#582](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/582))
- **skf-analyze-source:** The step-by-step Analyze Source asks one opening question instead of three: it first reads what the invocation already says (`--project-path`, `--intent-hint`, `--scope-hint`, or a path, a goal and folders to focus on or skip named in the message), then asks only for what is missing, and nothing when the path and the goal are known. With a stated goal, an interactive run now marks the units outside it `deferred` before mapping their exports, lists them in the report and lets you restore any of them; a headless run defers nothing, and `--intent-hint` still only ranks its recommendations. Each step now has one menu that also takes your feedback, where it used to ask an open question and then show the menu (the export mapping menu now offers `[X]` Cancel and exit too), and Recommend leads its menu with the confirmed, rejected and flagged units instead of asking for a final confirmation first; Advanced Elicitation, Party Mode and Discover Additional Source now feed what they change back into the decisions before the brief step, and a run cancelled at that menu resumes at the recommendations instead of skipping to the briefs. The `[D] Discover Additional Source` option of the export mapping and recommendation menus now runs one shared procedure that scans, names, maps and checks the new repository for composites with the same helpers as the rest of the analysis. Each unit's files are listed by the helper (what git lists, so ignored build output and installed dependencies never count) under the project path it was found in, and its language is read from that list; a folder path typed as `./packages/auth` or `packages/auth/` lists the same files as `packages/auth`. ([#637](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/637); issues [#602](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/602), [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599), [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594))
- **skf-analyze-source:** Analyze Source no longer checks each unit for scripts and assets. The export summary it shows, and the Export Map in `analyze-source-report-<project>.md`, drop their Scripts/Assets column, and each unit's analysis does less work. Briefs are unchanged: Create Skill still detects and packages a skill's scripts and assets when it extracts the source. ([#665](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/665); issue [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599))
- **skf-analyze-source:** Running Analyze Source again on the same target with a different `--target-ref`, `--target-refs`, `--scope-hint` or `--intent-hint` now archives the unfinished report in `forge_data_folder` and starts a fresh analysis with the new values, and a headless run names the archived report in its envelope's `warnings`. Before, the run resumed the unfinished report with its old values, so a mistyped ref halted every re-run until the report was renamed by hand. An unfinished step-by-step analysis that the re-run gives none of these flags, or the same values, still resumes. ([#659](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/659))
- **skf-analyze-source:** The interactive project scan now takes its candidate boundaries from the manifest scan instead of walking the tree by hand: `skf-scan-manifests.py` proposes each folder that holds a manifest or a Docker, compose or serverless file, leaves out a workspace root with no code of its own, and marks the workspace members `skf-detect-workspaces.py` resolves. Runs on the same tree always propose the same boundaries, found the same way as by the Discover Additional Source option. Workspace membership is now a reported signal, and the scan's catalogs list the manifests and service configurations; the entry points are reported per unit at unit identification. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-analyze-source, skf-brief-skill, skf-quick-skill:** The shared language helper, `skf-detect-language.py`, now lets the manifests nearest the repository root decide a repository's language, never one in a docs, example, test, tool or hidden folder while another manifest exists: a Python library whose docs site has a `package.json` is `python`, not `javascript`, and a Rust-and-Python repository with a root `pyproject.toml` is `python`. When no manifest sits at the root (CPython's), the nearest one decides at `medium` confidence instead of `high`. `detected_languages` follows the same ranking, folder depth first and then the rule order, so its first entry is still the `language` answer, and the output gains `source_language`, the language most of the source files are written in, which also ends `detected_languages` when no manifest sits at the root. Analyze Source, Brief Skill and Quick Skill take their language from this helper. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issue [#582](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/582))
- **skf-audit-skill:** An interactive Audit Skill run shows its baseline summary and analysis plan and goes straight on to the analysis, instead of always asking "Ready to begin drift analysis?". It stops to ask only when the baseline looks wrong: this run reads the source with fewer tools than the compile did (its forge tier is below the tier the skill was compiled at), so the diff can report as drift what only the tools changed, or the provenance map is more than 90 days old. There it offers to continue or to stop (`user-cancelled`, exit 6) and refresh the baseline first, keeping the baseline-only drift report. A headless run continues past that confirmation as before. The drift report's Structural Drift and Severity Classification tables are now printed from the saved JSON by a script: an empty table reads `None.`, and 10 or more rows of one table that share a file (removed rows also a directory) always roll up into one row, with `n/a` for its signature. Stopping at the upstream-moved question now says how to update the skill to the newer ref. ([#644](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/644); issues [#589](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/589), [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599))
- **skf-brief-skill:** An interactive Brief Skill run on a repository now shows its target analysis once, in the "Analysis Complete" summary, with one progress line for each part while it works, instead of a block per part before the summary. The summary gains the package name with its dependency count, the extraction warnings and the target and detected versions side by side, and the scope question that follows in the same message no longer repeats the target, language and modules. No item is removed, and headless output is unchanged. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-brief-skill:** Brief Skill now runs your `on_complete` command when an `[auto]` brief is approved (the path the `forge-auto` pipeline takes), right after its `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` line; before, only a brief written through the interactive, headless or ratify path called it. The envelope's `warnings` list now carries the run's warnings (a failed doc detection, a doc URL that did not answer, a partial repository listing, the warnings a helper returned). In a headless or `[auto]` run, every halt that has a `halt_reason` prints its error envelope before it stops, the halts of the first two steps included; in `[auto]` mode this holds even when the run is not headless. A failing `on_complete` command still never fails the run: the run shows one `on_complete hook failed` line after the envelope. ([#628](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/628); issues [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585), [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))
- **skf-brief-skill:** Brief Skill's target analysis now finds a package's entry files with the rules the extractor follows (the `package.json` `exports` map, with a build output read from its source, the package a Python manifest names, a crate's `[lib] path`) and follows `export *` and star-import re-exports to the modules that define the names, so the analysis lists more of a package's exports. The scope-type recommendation counts those exports from the extractor's result instead of a number typed into its request. A language the analysis reads without the extractor (Ruby, C# or Swift, say), or a run whose extraction failed, no longer gets a `public-api` recommendation from a count of the entry points read by hand: pick `[P]`, or pass `scope_type=public-api` in a headless run. ([#659](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/659))
- **skf-brief-skill:** A headless or `[auto]` Brief Skill run now holds its `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` success line until the end-of-run health check, which displays it as the run's last line, so a `claude -p` caller reads the envelope as the final message. A headless run also skips the interactive success summary. A halt still displays its error line where it stops. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-campaign:** Campaign Setup now writes `campaign-brief.yaml` with `campaign-state.py init --brief-out` (and the new `--brief-template` and `--notes`), in the same call that writes `_campaign-state.yaml` and from the same checked targets, instead of having the model fill in the brief template by hand. The brief keeps the template's keys in their order and its leading comment block, which now reads `Written by campaign-state.py init from the parsed targets`, but no longer carries the template's comment on each key. Values are quoted the way YAML needs, so a pin such as `1.10` is always quoted and stays a string, and an empty value reads `''`. ([#655](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/655))
- **skf-campaign:** Campaign now renders each Tier A skill's kickoff message entirely in `campaign-render-kickoff.py`, so the model no longer rewrites parts of it for every skill. The brief summary lists the skill's target entry in `campaign-brief.yaml`, and Setup now writes every field a target's source gave it into that entry, so a language or scope hint in a seeded brief reaches the kickoff and the Tier B batch. The standing directive is copied byte for byte from the new `--directive-file`, whatever its encoding, or reads `No directive configured`. The persistent facts are piped from the customization resolver into the new `--facts-json -`, one bullet each, or read `None`: the script resolves `{project-root}` with the new `--project-root` and reads the files of each `file:` entry itself. A `file:` path with no glob character that names no file now stops the run with exit `2` `invalid-input` instead of dropping its facts without a word (a glob that matches nothing still adds none), and so does a template, state, directive or facts file the script cannot read; a missing or unreadable brief stops it with exit `8` `missing-brief`, as at the other stages. An interactive Setup now asks for its targets in one question: give a manifest or `campaign-brief.yaml` path, a pasted list or repository URLs, and Setup drafts the target table (tier `A`, the latest release and no dependencies unless you say otherwise, and the project name as the campaign name), shows it once for you to correct, then asks only about what is still missing or ambiguous. `--brief` and `--manifest` still imply `--headless`. ([#631](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/631); issue [#604](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/604))
- **skf-campaign:** A `persistent_facts` entry prefixed `!` now drops every earlier entry it names from every Tier A kickoff, and adds no fact of its own, so `!file:{project-root}/**/project-context.md` in a team or personal override (`_bmad/custom/skf-campaign.toml` or `_bmad/custom/skf-campaign.user.toml`) keeps the bundled default's `project-context.md` files out of the kickoffs. A dropped `file:` entry is never read. A `!` entry that names no earlier entry changes nothing. ([#642](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/642); issue [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596))
- **skf-create-skill:** With `--batch`, every brief now ends with its own result contract (`create-skill-result-<timestamp>.json` and `create-skill-result-latest.json` in that brief's version folder) and its own `on_complete` call, each with that brief's `-latest.json`, where only the last brief of a batch got either before. The health check still runs once, after the last brief. A brief that halts, or whose result contract could not be written, gets no `on_complete` call; the run says why. `on_complete` is run as a shell command from the project root, so it cannot name an agent skill such as Test Skill. A failing `on_complete` command still never fails the brief: it shows one `on_complete failed` line. The extraction inventory a run builds is now kept on disk beside the staging folder, as `_bmad-output/.skf-stage/<skill>.inventory.json`, and the later steps read it there instead of from the conversation, so a long run that loses earlier context still compiles from what it extracted. The new `skf-extraction-inventory.py` writes it from the recipe runner's and the script detector's own output, so no export, citation or count is copied by hand, and it counts the enrichment annotations and writes `extraction-rules.yaml`. When a free-text `scripts_intent` or `assets_intent` leaves detected files out, the evidence report names them. A whole-language reference now keeps its fetched language documentation in `_bmad-output/<skill>-docs/`, as a docs-only skill does, and compiles its Language Guide from those pages. Step 7 now copies the skill as validation left it in the staging folder, byte for byte, and replaces the version's package whole, so compiling a version again no longer leaves reference files of the earlier compile in it; a half-written or hidden file in the staged `references/` is left out. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issues [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585), [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587))
- **skf-create-skill:** Create Skill no longer runs an ecosystem check. The stage looked for an existing official skill in an agentskills.io registry that has no API, so it never found a match and never asked anything; the run now goes from loading the brief straight to extraction. The other steps keep their numbers. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issue [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599))
- **skf-create-skill:** Create Skill now records the README of a GitHub source in `metadata.json` `doc_sources` as the raw file at the ref the skill was built from (`https://raw.githubusercontent.com/<owner>/<repo>/<ref>/README.md`, with the README's own file name), picking `README.md` over a translation such as `README-ja.md`, and hashes it with the same fetch Audit Skill's doc-drift check runs again (`skf-detect-docs.py readme-entry`). The entry used to be `<source_repo>/blob/main/README.md`, an HTML page, or a URL with no scheme for an `owner/repo` source, so every audit reported the README as changed or could not fetch it. A local source's README is recorded as a `file://` URL, and a source on another host gets no README entry. Pages of the repository's `docs/` folder are now recorded as raw GitHub URLs, not as `file://` paths into SKF's clone. The first audit of a skill after it is compiled again sees the new URLs. ([#622](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/622); issue [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592))
- **skf-create-skill:** Create Skill now stages a skill in `_bmad-output/.skf-stage/<name>/` before it writes the version, instead of in `_bmad-output/<name>/`. If you set `skills_output_folder` or `forge_data_folder` to `_bmad-output`, an earlier release may have left `SKILL.md`, `metadata.json`, `context-snippet.md`, `references/`, `evidence-report.md` and `provenance-map.json` at the root of a skill's folder there, which can make Create Skill stop with `flat-layout` and make Drop Skill and Rename Skill refuse the folder. Delete those leftovers and keep the `<version>/` folders, the `active` link and `skill-brief.yaml`; the troubleshooting guide has the details. ([#509](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/509))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-update-skill, skf-audit-skill:** The shared recipe runner, `skf-extract-public-api.py --mode full`, now stops its ast-grep runs after 540 seconds by default (`--timeout` sets another limit) and still writes its result, marked `incomplete`, with the files it left unread, so a long extraction no longer runs past an agent's shell time limit with nothing written. It also reads a `--files-from` list whose first path starts with `[` (a `[slug]/page.tsx` route folder) as one path per line, exits `2` with one line on stderr instead of crashing on an unforeseen error, and records each export's declaration on one line as `signature`, so a parameter list, a destructured parameter or a generic a formatter split over several lines is no longer cut at its first line. ([#633](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/633); issue [#584](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/584))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill in compose mode now treats the library pairs an architecture document names together as candidates, not integrations. A pair the document names only in separate list items or table rows (a Tech Stack table and a Components list that name every library, say) is dropped with a `comention-list-only` warning. A pair named together in one passage (a sentence, list item, table row or diagram line), or in a lead-in sentence and the list under it, becomes an integration only when that passage says how the two libraries work together (data flow, wrapping, configuration or events), and the integration's architecture reference quotes it; the others are dropped with a `comention-unconfirmed` warning. With no architecture document, a pair is a candidate only when one skill's own docs name the other or the two share a domain keyword, never because they share a language, and only the top 20 pairs are analyzed. A candidate is kept only when that docs line says how the two work together (a documented contract, which the integration cites) or when both skills' descriptions and exports show how the shared domain links them (marked `[inferred from shared domain]`), so two libraries that do the same job, such as two validators, are never paired; the others are dropped with an `infer-unconfirmed` warning. Expect fewer integrations with better evidence in compose-mode stacks; the evidence report lists each pair dropped. ([#630](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/630); issue [#582](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/582))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill's code mode now counts the files that import each dependency with the shared `skf-count-imports.py` helper instead of a hand-run grep, so the count matches on module boundaries and reads a distribution name as its import name: `react-dom` no longer counts as `react`, and `PyYAML` counts the files that import `yaml`. A ranking, the recommended scope and the stack version (taken from the most imported library) can differ from an earlier run where the old count was wrong. A dependency the helper cannot count with confidence (a guessed import name that matches no file, no import name, or an ecosystem it has no import rules for) is listed below the threshold with its reason instead of counting 0, and a headless run keeps the same recommended scope an interactive `C` accepts (the runtime libraries imported by 2 or more files). The detection summary now splits runtime and dev-only dependencies from the scanner's own data, the no-manifest message lists the manifest files the scanner looks for (no longer `*.csproj`), and code-mode integration pairs take their co-import files and lines from the same counts, through `skf-pair-intersect.py intersect --counts`, instead of a second grep. At Forge+ and Deep, CCC semantic search no longer adds co-import files to a pair, since the counts already hold every file that imports both libraries: it only picks the files that best show each integration, so no integration is labelled `ccc-augmented` any more. ([#635](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/635); issue [#598](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/598))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** In compose mode, Stack Skill now writes each integration's evidence into the stack it builds. Every Library Pair Integrations entry of SKILL.md and every `references/integrations/<a>-<b>.md` file cites both constituent skills on `[from skill: <name>] <signature>` lines, with the architecture excerpt, documented contract or shared domain that confirmed the pair, which is the evidence Test Skill's integration check reads. When a Verify Stack report rated the pair, the entry also shows `VS overall` and `VS pair` lines, and its Confidence line ends with `[VS: Risky]` or `[VS: Blocked]`. Before, Stack Skill found this evidence and the verdicts but left them out of the compiled stack, so a pair Verify Stack judged infeasible carried no flag and Test Skill scored compose integrations as incomplete. ([#661](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/661))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** In compose mode, a library pair that the architecture document names together in only one passage, in a lone diagram, or under an Overview or Introduction heading now becomes a candidate that Stack Skill judges from its excerpt, like every other candidate. Before, a pair needed two paragraphs outside those headings, so a terse architecture document (two short sections, an Integration Points list or a single Mermaid diagram) gave no integration at all. An excerpt from an introductory section that only names both libraries still confirms nothing. The pairs a document only lists together (a Tech Stack table, say) are still dropped, with one `comention-list-only` warning that gives their count. A document that names no two confirmed libraries in one passage now records a `comention-no-candidates` warning, which the evidence report lists. ([#657](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/657))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill in code mode now relabels its export records with one call, `skf-render-stack-metadata.py relabel`, instead of copying each label the check expects into the records and running the check again by hand. The helper runs the label check of `skf-render-metadata-stats.py`, sets each mislabeled field to the label the record's `extraction_method` implies, and rewrites the records file only when a label changed; the `export-labels-relabeled` warning lists what it changed. Where the check names no value, the helper picks one: an unknown or missing `extraction_method` becomes `source_reading` (nothing shows an ast-grep rule matched the export), a known one written in another case takes its usual spelling, and a record with no valid `signature_source` takes `T1` when an ast-grep rule matched it and `T1-low` otherwise. The code-mode `provenance-map.json` now gets its `entries[]` from the same records file and its `integrations[]` from the extraction bundle's pairs (with their co-import files) through `skf-render-stack-metadata.py provenance --records ... --bundle ...`, which writes the map in one atomic write, so the run no longer types every export record or integration pair a second time. ([#644](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/644); issues [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592), [#606](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/606))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Every ownership refusal of Stack Skill now reads "`<stack>`: nothing was written." followed by its reason: the stack still uses the flat layout, the folder is not SKF output, SKF could not check who generated it, or `skf-skill-inventory.py` is missing. The remedy each one gives is unchanged, and so are the halt reasons (`flat-layout` and `not-skf-output`) and exit code `5`. When `skf-skill-inventory.py` is missing, the halt's `path` now names the stack's folder in `skills_output_folder`, since no write check ran to name another. ([#644](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/644); issue [#600](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/600))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill now takes each integration's confidence tier, and the counted fields of `metadata.json` (`library_count`, `integration_count`, `libraries`, `integration_pairs`, `confidence_distribution`, `confidence_tier` and `source_authority`), from one helper, `skf-render-stack-metadata.py`, instead of working them out by hand from rules several steps restated; an SKF install without the helper stops the run with exit `3` (`resolution-failure`). The values follow the rules the steps gave before: a pair takes the weaker of its two libraries' tiers in both modes, the distribution counts each library once, `confidence_tier` is its largest bin (a tie goes to the weaker tier), and `source_authority` is the lowest of the constituents' (`internal` below `community`). A constituent that records no authority now counts as `community`, so it never lifts a stack to `official`, and a code-mode stack, whose libraries record none, is `community`. The validation step reads a new stack structure check of `skf-validate-output.py --skill-type stack` (`validation.stack_structure`, and `--forge-tier` to compare the run's tier) instead of reading every file itself, and runs it again after `skill-check` fixes or splits the package: it warns on a missing `SKILL.md` section, a catalog link that does not resolve, a `metadata.json` key or value off the stack template (the dominant tier recomputed), a library or pair `metadata.json` lists with no file of its own, a reference or pair file without its sections, and a per-library summary, pair entry or reference file with no `T1`, `T1-low`, `T2` or `T3` label. The stack version now comes from `skf-skill-inventory.py version`: a code-mode stack takes the version of its most imported library reduced to one folder name (`^18.2.0` gives `18.2.0`), a re-composed compose-mode stack bumps the prior version as before, and only a run without the helper, or a prior stack that records no usable version, starts a new line at `1.0.0` with a `stack-version-default` warning. Compose mode finds the Verify Stack report with `skf-validate-feasibility-report.py --locate`, as Refine Architecture does: a report whose `schemaVersion` is not `1.0` still stops the run with exit `2` (`schema-version-mismatch`), a verdict token outside the schema's set is still a hard error, and a report that breaks the contract otherwise (a section missing or out of order, no verdict table or a second one, or a file that cannot be read) is no longer read, with a `vs-report-unusable` warning. The context snippet is now measured with `skf-count-tokens.py`, the count the validator takes (characters divided by 4, rounded down), with a `snippet-unmeasured` warning when that helper is missing. ([#630](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/630); issues [#606](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/606), [#597](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/597), [#590](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/590), [#591](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/591))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill's code mode now scans only the folder you choose. Pass `project_path` to build the stack for one package of a monorepo, or, in an interactive run whose dependency manifests sit in more than one folder, answer the one question it asks (the whole project, or one package folder). The manifest scan, the import counts and the co-import pairs all stay inside that folder, while every path the run records (each `source_file`, the co-import files of an integration) stays relative to the project root. Without `project_path`, a headless run still scans the whole project and, when the manifests sit in several folders, records that choice as its `init.scan-root` decision, whose reason says to pass `project_path`. `project_path` was declared before but never read. ([#635](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/635), [#640](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/640); issue [#606](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/606))
- **skf-drop-skill:** A Drop Skill run that ends with a degraded outcome now says so in the `SKF_DROP_SKILL_RESULT_JSON` envelope and in `drop-skill-result-latest.json`, whose `warnings` list names each context file the drop could not rebuild (`context_rebuild_failed`), an `active` link a version purge left pointing at no version (`active_link_dangling`), each folder a partial purge could not delete (`delete_failed`) and each final-state check that failed (`verification_failed`), each with its error or manual fix. Before, such a run read as a plain `success`, and its report pointed at error-handling guidance that did not exist; the report now names each manual fix and lists the folders a partial purge left on disk. A run given `skill_name` now reads that skill alone and shows no list of every skill. An interactive run that stops or is cancelled at one of its questions, checks or the confirmation, or stops while it drops, now deletes its run folder under `_bmad-output/.skf-run/` instead of leaving it behind. ([#646](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/646); issues [#591](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/591), [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594), [#600](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/600))
- **skf-export-skill:** A headless Export Skill run, a `--dry-run` included, now ends on its `SKF_EXPORT_RESULT_JSON` line: the run hands the line to the shared health check, which displays it after everything else, so `claude -p` prints the envelope as the final message instead of the health check's closing `Workflow complete.` line. Interactive runs are unchanged, and a halt still ends on its own line. ([#662](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/662))
- **skf-forger, skf-setup:** When `_bmad/skf/config.yaml` is missing, Ferris no longer sends you to Setup, which stops on the same missing file: Ferris, Setup and `skf-preflight.py` now name the installer (`npx bmad-module-skill-forge install`, or `npx bmad-method install` and add SKF), and Ferris's `KI` does the same for a missing knowledge index. Setup's `on-activation:config-missing` reason keeps its opening sentence. Ferris checks for the config before reading it, loads the config and its sidecar folder with one `skf-preflight.py` call (a first run with no sidecar folder goes on with defaults), and reads the last pipeline result before it greets you, so the offer to resume a stopped pipeline comes in the greeting. Ferris now needs `uv` to start, as every workflow already does; without it he stops and gives the uv install link. The first-run greeting and the menu show `forge-auto` and the other pipeline aliases, every menu ends with a reminder to run each workflow in a fresh context window, `WS` names its data sources, reads them again each time, and ends with one recommended code per skill in progress, and picking a second workflow in one session first offers a fresh session or a pipeline. `skf-preflight.py` gains `--allow-missing-sidecar`, a `derived.headless_mode` field, absolute folder paths (`config.output_folder_resolved`, `config.skills_output_folder_resolved` and `config.forge_data_folder_resolved`) that expand the `{project-root}` placeholder the BMAD Method installer writes, as `config.sidecar_path_resolved` now does too, and a `CONFIG_MALFORMED` code for a config that exists but does not load. A `sidecar_path` that still holds a placeholder, such as `{project-root}/{value}`, halts with `SIDECAR_UNDEFINED`, and a sidecar file Setup has not written yet reads as empty instead of setting `preferences_error` or `forge_tier_error`. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#608](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/608))
- **skf-quick-skill:** A headless single-target Quick Skill run now ends on its `SKF_QUICK_SKILL_RESULT_JSON` line: the run hands the line to the shared health check, which displays it after everything else, so `claude -p` prints the envelope as the final message instead of the health check's closing line. Interactive runs and `--batch` targets still show the line where the result contract is written. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-refine-architecture:** Refine Architecture no longer writes the refined document before you review it. Compile now builds a draft beside the RA state file in the forge data folder (`.skf-ra-draft-<project_name>.md`): a script copies your architecture document line for line, places each gap, issue and improvement where its anchor puts it (after the whole paragraph, list, table or code block that holds it, so none is split), appends the Refinement Summary with counts it takes from what it placed, and checks that every original line is still there, unchanged. Only `[C]` at the review replaces `refined-architecture-<project>.md`, after the same check passes again, and an earlier file of that name is first renamed to `refined-architecture-<project>-<YYYYMMDD-HHmmss>.md` instead of being overwritten; `[X]` deletes the draft and leaves the earlier file as it was. A draft that would lose or change a line of your document is never promoted. Each refinement now sits between `<!-- RA:BEGIN ... -->` and `<!-- RA:END -->` marker lines, and running Refine Architecture again on a refined document (or on one an older version refined, with its `RA:` subsections and its Refinement Summary) sets the earlier annotations aside, analyzes only your own text and writes one current set in their place: to keep a refinement, move it into your own prose. An older version's `RA:` subsection carries no end marker, so it is set aside up to the next heading: the run names those lines when it starts, at the review and in the report, and says they were not checked, since a paragraph of yours below such a subsection is not in the refined document. The improvement subsection heading now reads `RA: Enhancement: <title>`. The final report takes its counts and the technologies no skill covers from that build instead of re-reading the document, writes the result contract, passes the per-run result file to `on_complete`, and shows an `on_complete` failure as a warning line in the report. ([#633](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/633), [#641](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/641); issues [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585), [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587))
- **skf-refine-architecture:** When Refine Architecture works out the document's scope itself (no `--scope-skills`) and leaves some skills out, it now shows which skills are in and out of scope before it reads their APIs, and you can bring a skill in by typing its name or take one out with `-<name>`; type `C` to keep the scope. A headless run keeps the derived scope and logs that decision. The Refinement Summary gains a `Not verified (no skill)` row that names the libraries, databases and services the architecture mentions but no skill covers, since nothing checked what the architecture says about them, and a `VS Coverage` row when a Verify Stack report was used. A skill covers a technology when its name, or the repository or folder it was built from, equals the technology's name once case and separators are ignored. A name found only inside a longer name (`react` in React Native, `ai` in Azure AI Search, `core` in ASP.NET Core) does not count by itself: the technology stays in the row unless the architecture shows the skill is that technology, as a `next` skill is Next.js. When the `Not verified (no skill)` row names any technology, the next steps say to create their skills with Create Skill or Quick Skill (`@Ferris CS` or `@Ferris QS`) and run Refine Architecture again before Stack Skill; the final report shows the same next step. The final report no longer repeats the count table the review just showed: a headless run reads the counts in its result line. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618), [#645](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/645), [#657](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/657), [#661](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/661); issue [#610](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/610))
- **skf-rename-skill:** Rename Skill now names the copy of a skill that `npx skills add` installed in the IDE skill folder a context file's managed section points at, such as `.claude/skills/<old-name>/`, which a rename leaves under the old name while the rebuilt context-file rows point at the new one. The confirmation plan says such copies are not renamed, the final report gives each copy it finds a next step (remove it, run `npx skills add` on the renamed package, then reload the IDE), and the run's warnings, in the envelope and the result record, carry an `installed-copy-not-renamed: <path>` warning for each. No check runs when `snippet_skill_root_override` is set. ([#658](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/658); issue [#591](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/591))
- **skf-rename-skill:** A rename that stops with `verify-failed` now says how to recover: edit a note an earlier run wrote, or a `context-snippet.md` line such as `|gotchas:`, in the old skill and re-run the rename; or, when the match names the library or repository the skill is named after, or the old name is a word of the snippet template itself such as `api`, create the skill again under the new name with Brief Skill and Create Skill, as the troubleshooting guide explains. ([#658](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/658); issue [#510](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/510))
- **skf-setup:** Setup now shows its first-run notice, or its re-run notice, after tool detection has told it whether the project was set up before, and still before it writes any configuration. The FORGE STATUS report comes from the new `render-report` subcommand of `skf-emit-result-envelope.py`, which picks every line from the run's own results instead of leaving that choice to the agent. Setup keeps the output of its tool detector, its QMD collection classifier and its registry cleanup in a scratch run folder under `_bmad-output/.skf-run/`, where the report and the `SKF_SETUP_RESULT_JSON` envelope read them, and deletes the folder when the run ends; a halt that names a phase leaves it in place. When `--orphan-action=remove` deletes QMD collections without a prompt, the envelope's `warnings` now name each one (`orphan_removed: <name>`, and `orphan_remove_failed: <name>` for one it could not delete). ([#625](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/625); issues [#612](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/612), [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))
- **skf-test-skill:** Test Skill now decides whether a skill is docs-only by counting the citations it writes (only `[EXT:...]` links, with no `[AST:...]`, `[SRC:...]` or `[from skill: ...]` citation), and computes a docs-only skill's Export Coverage in `reconcile-coverage.py`, the script every other skill's coverage already comes from: complete documented items over documented items, where an item is complete when it has a description and, for a function or method, its parameters and return type. A missing description counts as an incomplete item, never as a broken inventory. Before, the docs-only verdict and the completeness ratio were worked out by hand, so the score of a docs-only skill, most of which is Export Coverage once Signature Accuracy and Type Coverage are skipped, can shift on its next test. ([#635](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/635); issue [#540](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/540))
- **skf-test-skill:** `skf-test-skill` writes its test report as `.skf-test-report-<skill>-<run_id>.md` in the version folder while it runs, and gives it its public name `test-report-<skill>-<run_id>.md` only once the report step's checks pass, a run the hard gate blocked included. A run that halts leaves no partial report where export-skill and update-skill look, so they keep reading the last finished one, and the hidden copy never makes drop-skill or rename-skill refuse the version folder. The run resolves its pass threshold once, records it with its source in the report frontmatter (`threshold`, `thresholdSource`), and the later steps read the threshold, the tooling status (`toolingStatus`) and the drift override (`workspaceDrift`) from there, so `--allow-workspace-drift` on a tree that has not drifted no longer turns a PASS into `pass-with-drift`. The report step no longer waits at a one-option `[C] Finish` menu before the health check. ([#637](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/637); issues [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587), [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599))
- **skf-test-skill:** At Quick tier, Test Skill counts the exports of a skill that Quick Skill built from a local source with the parser that built the skill's export list, `skf-extract-public-api.py --mode quick`, instead of scanning the entry points for `export` keywords by eye, so both sides count the same surface and such a skill's Export Coverage score can shift. Export forms the parser does not read are still read by eye, and the report names them as read by eye. ([#624](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/624), [#628](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/628); issue [#584](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/584))
- **skf-test-skill:** `skf-test-skill`'s coherence check takes its facts from the shared SKILL.md scanner, `skf-scan-skill-md-structure.py`, instead of greps and one subagent per reference. The zero-usage check counts each function and method export as a whole, fixed name, so an export named `$state` or `a.b` is no longer an empty or wildcard pattern; the scanner's `reference-check` checks that each file a markdown link names, and each `references/`, `scripts/` or `assets/` path the skill mentions, exists inside the skill, its source or (for a stack) the skills folder, and any other path written outside a link is no longer checked; the Scripts & Assets section test reads the scanner's new `scripts_assets` field; and `aggregate-coherence.py` counts the valid references itself from the per-reference results (`--references`, `--judged`, `--integration`). The documented-inventory spot-check runs in `validate-inventory.py` (`--skill-package`). skill-check now runs fresh on every test instead of reusing a score from create-skill's evidence report. Coherence and external validation scores can shift where the old counts or the reused score were wrong. ([#637](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/637); issues [#598](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/598), [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599))
- **skf-test-skill:** The gaps Test Skill's scripts find are now written to the gap ledger by `gap-ledger.py` from the scripts' own result files, instead of being typed in by hand: missing exports and types, stale and fabricated signatures, numerator inflation, the denominator guards, export-count drift, provenance lines, broken, inaccurate and escaping references, and the structural findings of an individual skill. Each reaches the hard gate and the Gap Report with a fixed title, Source and remediation, so a rerun records it once. The Gap Report wording of these gaps changes: references are titled `coherence: broken reference: {target}` and `coherence: inaccurate reference: {target}`, and each gap gets a remediation that names the file to change. ([#656](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/656))
- **skf-test-skill:** Test Skill now asks SKF's shared workspace detector whether a skill's local source is a monorepo, instead of judging it by eye from folder and manifest names, and the Coverage Analysis section records the layout it found. The answer picks the coverage denominator rule for a monorepo, so Export Coverage can shift for a repository the two judge differently: two or more packages under `apps/`, `libs/` or `code/` now make a monorepo, and a `packages/` folder that holds fewer than two packages no longer does. ([#624](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/624); issue [#584](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/584))
- **skf-test-skill, skf-create-stack-skill:** Test Skill now computes its remaining scoring inputs in scripts instead of by hand, so scores can shift slightly. At Forge, Forge+ and Deep tier it reads the source surface from the shared recipe runner, `skf-extract-public-api.py --mode full` with no match cap, so an internal match no entry point exports never counts as a missing export. `score-signatures.py` computes Signature Accuracy and Type Coverage (the source's interfaces, type aliases, enums and classes) from schema-checked subagent results and writes each wrong signature to the gap ledger. `load-coverage-inputs.py` makes every denominator count, guard, union and fold the coverage step used to count by hand, and `compute-score.py` applies the State 2 undercount deduction (10 points off Export Coverage when the provenance map and `metadata.json` disagree by more than 5%). At Quick tier the brief's scope now picks the denominator set and the deflation guard checks it, as at the higher tiers. Type Coverage counts only the types in the set the denominator counts, so a stratified skill's deferred types no longer lower it. A Forge, Forge+ or Deep run with no local source (only the skill's `metadata.json`, or a remote read) now skips Signature Accuracy and Type Coverage and moves their weight to the other categories, as a Quick-tier run does; before, its score could not be computed. A documented name now counts only as a whole name, so `get` written inside `getAll` no longer counts, and Stack Skill's pre-commit check (`skf-names-present.py`) matches the same way. The scripts hand each other their results through files in a run folder under `_bmad-output/.skf-run/`, removed when the run ends. A documented inventory that fails validation is now requested once more, and a second failure halts with the new `halt_reason` `inventory-invalid` (exit 1, with its result envelope on stderr). ([#635](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/635); issue [#613](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/613))
- **skf-update-skill:** In headless mode the `SKF_UPDATE_RESULT_JSON` line is now the run's last line, shown after the health check, so `claude -p` prints it as its final message, for `--detect-only` and `--dry-run` too. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-update-skill:** Update Skill with `--from-test-report` now routes each gap by its category, not its severity, so a missing export that Test Skill rates Medium and that cites no source line still gets a targeted re-extraction from the files its remediation names; when none of them holds the export, it is recorded without a source line, as a Medium gap may be, and the run does not stop. Each gap Update Skill has no repair for adds `test-report: not routed: <id> (<category>)` to `warnings[]`. A run without `--from-test-report` now looks for the skill's newest test report first: when that report failed, or passed only under workspace drift, is newer than the skill and was not applied by an earlier repair, an interactive run offers to repair its gaps (`[G]`, the default for a failed report) or to check the source for changes (`[S]`, the default for a drift pass), and a headless run checks the source and adds `unconsumed-test-report: <report path>` to `warnings[]`. When no source file changed, the report then names `--from-test-report`, or for a drift pass a new test against the pinned commit, instead of saying no action is required. So that a repaired skill reads newer than the report it applied, `metadata.json` `generation_date` is now written to the second, and the update block of `provenance-map.json` records that time as `last_update` and the applied report's run id as `test_report_run_id`. In a normal run, scripts now work out which files and exports changed, one category after another; an authoritative document such as `AGENTS.md` that the skill brief's scope already includes is now tracked without a prompt, as Create Skill tracks it, and one an earlier headless run skipped is asked about again in an interactive run. ([#628](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/628); issues [#583](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/583), [#589](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/589))
- **skf-update-skill:** Update Skill no longer has a separate validate stage: the merge step goes straight to the write step, which runs `skill-check` once per update, in one call that validates, fixes and runs the security scan against the written package. Before, the validate stage showed checks that had not run yet and could run `skill-check` a second time. After that call the write step checks each `[MANUAL]` section against the ones you approved and stops with `halted-for-manual-mismatch` (`error.phase` `write:verify-manual-integrity`) if `skill-check` changed one, and a body split never moves a section that holds a `[MANUAL]` block. The `active` link now moves to the new version only after those checks, so a `[MANUAL]` stop in the write step removes the new version and leaves the previous one active, where it used to leave the new version live with the changed section. A failed write of `metadata.json`, the provenance map, the evidence report or the context snippet stops with `halted-for-write-failure` (`error.phase` `write:artifact-write`). Without `skill-check`, the package structure is checked with `skf-validate-output.py`. The report's validation table now shows what the write step recorded in the evidence report, with `Diff` and `Security` rows, and the detect, re-extract and merge stages each print one progress line instead of a table the report repeats. The stages are renumbered: write is step 5, the report step 6 and the health check step 7. ([#638](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/638); issues [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599), [#600](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/600))
- **skf-verify-stack:** Verify Stack now finds the skills an architecture document names, and the pairs of them it names together, with the shared mentions helper Refine Architecture uses, so both workflows rate the same pairs in the same document. A coverage row of a skill the document names is labelled with the skill's name (`postgres`, where the document writes "PostgreSQL"), so the first run after upgrading can show a one-time coverage delta. A skill the document names only inside fenced code (any code block, not only a Mermaid diagram) now counts as Extra. When the helper cannot be found, the run stops with `resolution-failure` at the new phase `coverage:mentions`. ([#640](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/640); issue [#598](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/598))
- **skf-verify-stack:** Verify Stack now writes only its timestamped report, `feasibility-report-<slug>-<timestamp>.md`, while it runs, and copies it to `feasibility-report-<slug>-latest.md` only after the finished report passes the feasibility-report check, so a run that halts leaves the last finished `-latest` copy in place for Stack Skill, Refine Architecture and campaign instead of a partial report. Without `--previous-report` it compares against the newest earlier report whose run finished (its `stepsCompleted` lists `synthesize` and it passes the feasibility-report check), never a partial one. Integration evidence now comes from scripts instead of reading each SKILL.md in context: Check 4 quotes the literal citation, substring and line, that `skf-scan-skill-md-structure.py cross-reference` finds, and a citation of the repository or folder a skill was built from also counts (a skill named `oms-cognee` is cited where another skill's SKILL.md says `cognee`) unless another skill shares that name, so some pairs move from `Plausible` to `Verified`. Two technologies that match one skill no longer form a pair. The circular-dependency check reads the same citations, and a skill changed during the run is found by its `metadata_hash` (`skf-enumerate-stack-skills.py --expect-hashes`) instead of a modification time; its pairs are reported `Risky`, with a re-run recommendation. Coverage matches technologies against the repository and folder names the enumerate helper reports, and the requirements pass reads the summaries step 3 writes for every covered skill instead of each SKILL.md. The pair counts, the requirement counts and `recommendationCount` now come from `skf-coverage-tally.py` and `skf-verdict-rollup.py`: each circular-dependency row counts as `Risky`, so a stack where two skills cite each other, or more skills cite each other in a circle, reports `CONDITIONALLY_FEASIBLE`, and `recommendationCount` counts the recommendations the report lists: one per Missing or Replaced technology, per Blocked, Risky or Plausible row, for no integration pair, and per unmet requirement. A failed write of the report in any stage now stops the run with exit `4` `write-failed`. The run keeps its inventory and working files in `_bmad-output/.skf-run/skf-verify-stack-<timestamp>/`, deleted when the run finishes and kept after a halt. ([#630](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/630); issues [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587), [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593), [#598](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/598))
- **skf-verify-stack:** Verify Stack takes its integration pairs from the architecture document's prose alone, as every run already did: the rule that preferred a stack skill's `integration_patterns` is gone, because no stack skill SKF builds declares that key (a stack's `metadata.json` carries `integration_pairs`). The feasibility report's integration display table loses its Source column, which only ever read prose co-mention, and the Evidence Sources section no longer lists a stack manifest. The workflows reference and the BMAD synergy guide no longer say VS reads a stack skill's integration patterns. ([#658](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/658); issue [#590](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/590))
- **stability contract:** The SemVer contract in `docs/_internal/STABILITY.md`, which ships in the npm package, now also covers the parts of SKF's workflows that pipelines and saved Ferris commands depend on by name: the enum values and properties of the JSON schemas for the headless result envelopes, the skill brief and workspace detection, the Ferris menu codes, the pipeline aliases and the workflow flags. Removing or renaming one needs a major release, and adding one needs at least a minor release. Exit codes, `halt_reason` and `error.phase` values and preference keys are checked at each release but are not yet part of the contract. ([#578](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/578); issue [#556](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/556))

### Fixed

- **all workflows:** The review gate of the end-of-run health check now has a headless default, `[Q] Queue locally` (save the findings to the local improvement queue, submit nothing), so a headless run of any workflow no longer waits at it. Interactive runs can pick `[Q]` there too, and a headless Campaign queues every finding locally whatever `health_findings_queue` says. Finding fingerprints are now computed on stock macOS and Windows, which have no `sha1sum`. ([#499](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/499); issue [#494](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/494))
- **all workflows:** Every workflow now says where its shared and cross-skill files live: a path that starts with `knowledge/` or `shared/`, or that names another SKF skill's folder (such as `skf-create-skill/references/`), resolves from the installed SKF module under `_bmad/skf/`, not from the workflow's own folder, so a run reads the health check, the shared references and a sibling skill's extraction tables where the installer put them. A workflow that loads files from another skill names it, since that skill must be installed too. Installed paths named in the workflows now carry the project root, Audit Skill names its `scripts/` folder, Export Skill says which step loads each of its optional reference files, and Setup Forge names the tier table its report reads. Setup Forge's missing-install message now reads "Setup cannot proceed: the SKF config file was not found" and leaves the file's path to the blocked envelope's `path`, and the troubleshooting page lists it under that heading. ([#649](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/649); issue [#601](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/601))
- **all workflows:** `headless_mode: true` in the forge's `preferences.yaml`, in the `sidecar_path` folder that `_bmad/skf/config.yaml` sets, now makes every workflow run headless, as the docs say. Audit Skill, Update Skill and the other workflows that named the file without its folder now read it from that folder, so they no longer miss the setting and stop at their prompts. ([#649](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/649); issue [#603](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/603))
- **packaging:** The npm package no longer ships files only SKF's maintainers use: an old release audit, the release runbook, and the docs build and validation scripts. From `tools/`, only the installer CLI and the `npx` wrapper ship. ([#489](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/489), [#578](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/578); issue [#487](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/487))
- **skf-analyze-source:** Analyze Source now writes the ref you pin into every brief it generates. `--target-ref <tag|branch>` was never read: it now pins every project path, and `--target-refs <path:ref,...>` pins each one; each project path is scanned at its ref (a package folder inside a local repository too, read from a copy of that repository at the ref), and each brief records its unit's ref as `target_ref`, so Brief Skill and Create Skill build at the ref that was analyzed instead of the default branch. An `AN[auto]` run that falls back to the step-by-step analysis keeps the tag or branch it pinned the same way. A remote project path given as `owner/repo` or `github.com/owner/repo` is fetched over https. Each brief's `source_repo` is now the project path its unit was found in, where every brief of a multi-repository analysis named the first path, and each unit is listed and named under that path. The `[auto]` path now applies `--scope-hint`, which narrows its include patterns and the packages a split briefs, and `--intent-hint`, which picks the facets a merged repository keeps and words each description, where it ignored both. ([#637](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/637); issue [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594))
- **skf-analyze-source:** An `AN[auto]` run that cannot classify the repository and goes on as a step-by-step analysis now flags the units that already have a compiled skill as already skilled (and recommends Update Skill for them) and marks the units with an earlier brief as briefed, as an interactive analysis does. Before, the auto report started with no inventory of skills and briefs, so the fallback briefed those units again and, in a headless run, replaced the brief behind an existing skill. ([#659](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/659))
- **skf-analyze-source:** An `AN[auto]` run on a repository URL now roots each brief's include patterns at the source folder the repository really has, such as `lib/` for a JavaScript package or the import package folder of a Python project (`sklearn/` for scikit-learn). Before, the run looked for that folder in a checkout that holds only the manifests and could fall back to `src/`, which wrote a brief that matched no file, so Create Skill extracted nothing. On every `AN[auto]` run, a local path included, a package the run detects as JavaScript now gets `.js` patterns instead of `.ts` ones, and a Python project's import package is found from its file list. A repository with no conventional source folder, or a monorepo merged into one brief whose packages sit outside its root `src/` or `lib/`, now gets patterns over the whole repository that leave out top-level folders such as `docs/`, `examples/`, `scripts/` and `tests/`, so the brief drops no package.
