A monorepo the run splits into several briefs gets the same patterns for each package, rooted at the package's folder. ([#665](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/665); issue [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592))
- **skf-analyze-source:** `AN[auto]` and `forge-auto` no longer scope a library that extends a web framework (FastAPI on Starlette, sqladmin, a Flask or Django extension, axum-extra) as `reference-app`. Shape detection now adds the signal `app_or_library:framework_dep` when a framework dependency is the only app signal of a package that names itself a library, and the auto-scope judges from the manifest description or the README whether it is an application built on the framework, which stays `reference-app`, or a library, which gets a `full-library` or `public-api` brief; the choice is recorded in the result envelope's `headless_decisions` under the new gate `auto-scope.shape`. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-analyze-source:** An `AN[auto]` run (forge-auto included) given an https clone URL on a host other than GitHub, GitLab or Bitbucket, such as a self-hosted `https://git.example.com/team/repo.git` or an Azure DevOps `/_git/` URL, now analyzes it as a repository, named after the repository. Before, any such URL was treated as a documentation site: the run wrote a docs-only brief named after the host and reported success, and an interactive run stopped saying the URL was a documentation URL. Any other `http` or `https` address on such a host is now checked with git first, by an `[auto]` run and an interactive one alike. An address git reads as a repository, which on most hosts is the repository's main page (a self-hosted GitLab, Gitea, Codeberg or GitHub Enterprise page), stops the run with a request for its clone URL, its address without `https://` or its SSH address. The run also stops when the server asks for credentials, such as a private repository's page, saying SKF reads no documentation behind a login, and when git gets no answer from the address in 20 seconds, asking for the repository's clone URL or SSH address, or a documentation URL that answers. A repository browser page git cannot read at that address, such as a Bitbucket Data Center `/projects/<p>/repos/<r>` page, a SourceForge or Launchpad code page or a link to a file or folder inside a repository, is still read as documentation: give its clone URL. ([#665](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/665); issue [#582](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/582))
- **skf-analyze-source:** `AN[auto]` and `forge-auto` now accept a repository given as a host path without a scheme (`github.com/owner/repo`) or as the `owner/repo` shorthand, as the step-by-step analysis does, instead of halting with `resolution-failure` when git read it as a local folder. `skf-skill-inventory.py derive-name --target` now returns the target's `kind` (`local`, `docs` or `remote`) and the `clone_url` git clones it from, and both analysis paths route and clone through it. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-analyze-source:** An `AN[auto]` run no longer classifies a repository as a `language-reference` just because it depends on a parsing library such as `nom`, `winnow`, `pyparsing` or `tree-sitter`, or lists one as a development dependency. A command-line tool or app built with one is now classified as it would be without it (a `reference-app` brief when it declares a `bin` or `[[bin]]` or runs on an app framework such as `express` or `fastapi`), and a library as `library-API`. When the repository may implement a language on that parser, the run decides from its description or README and records the decision in its result's `headless_decisions` under the gate `auto-scope.shape`; a repository that also depends on a concrete parser such as `yaml` or `acorn`, as a linter or formatter does, is not asked. Compilers, interpreters, grammars and parser libraries are classified as before. ([#665](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/665); issue [#582](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/582))
- **skf-analyze-source:** The shape detection behind Analyze Source's auto-scope (`skf-shape-detect.py`) now reads `pyproject.toml` and `Cargo.toml` with Python's standard `tomllib` parser. On Python 3.9 and 3.10 it used a hand-written fallback that misread common manifests: a PEP 508 extra such as `requests[socks]` or a `]` in a trailing comment cut the dependency list short, and a dotted key such as `pest.workspace = true` lost its dependency, so a parser or framework dependency could go unseen and the repository get another shape. The fallback is gone, and the script needs Python 3.11 like every other helper. A grammar file below a library's `parsing/` or `parsers/` folder (sympy's `sympy/parsing/latex/LaTeX.g4`) no longer makes the repository a `language-reference`: it parses a format the library reads. A library such as sympy is classified by its other signals instead: `library-API` when its `pyproject.toml` has a `[project]` table, and with sympy's own manifest, which has none, auto-scope cannot classify it and switches to the interactive analysis instead of picking a wrong shape. ([#642](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/642); issue [#562](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/562))
- **skf-analyze-source, skf-brief-skill:** A detected source version that is not full `X.Y.Z` semver (`2.0.0rc1`, `0.1`) now falls back to `1.0.0` with a warning, as the brief schema documents. Before, the brief writer exited 1 on it, which `AN[auto]` turned into a `write-failed` halt. Analyze Source also reads the version from the manifest scan, which now gives each manifest's own `version` (a Cargo member's `version.workspace = true` resolved from its workspace) and flags a version computed at build time as `version_dynamic`, so only such a version is still looked up by hand. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-audit-skill:** A Deep-tier audit's semantic drift step now checks what the skill documents about each export, retrieved from the skill's QMD collection, against the current source, and each Semantic Drift row cites the source file and line that no longer supports the claim. Before, the step compared the skill's QMD collection, which indexes the skill's own files, with itself, so it had no current state to find drift in, although the audit plan and the docs promise a semantic comparison with the current source. A claim with no source line is not recorded, and the Dependency Shifts table gains an Evidence column. ([#655](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/655))
- **skf-audit-skill:** Audit Skill's re-index step now reads exports at Forge, Forge+ and Deep with Create Skill's ast-grep recipes and extraction protocol: each recipe runs once over the files the skill covers, each export records the line of its name, and the plain `find_code` pattern search is only a fallback. Before, the step named no recipes, so exports could be matched with plain patterns that find nothing for some forms on ast-grep 0.45.3 (such as `export function` in `.tsx` files or a Rust `pub fn`) and then be read by eye or reported as removed. At Deep, the re-index no longer asks QMD about each export's history, which never reached `extraction-snapshot.json`; Deep audits still compare QMD context in the semantic drift step. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#561](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/561))
- **skf-audit-skill:** When the recipe runner cannot run (no ast-grep it can use, or no result after the call), Audit Skill no longer reports exports dropped by a match cap as removed CRITICAL drift. The recipes it then runs one at a time keep every match over the files the snapshot left to read, as the runner itself does with `--head-cap 0`, so an audit of a large skill reports the `drift_score` and `next_workflow` its source supports. ([#660](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/660))
- **skf-audit-skill:** Audit Skill now scores drift from the structural diff it saves, not from the tables it writes into the drift report. Each run keeps the diff, the findings built from it and their classification in `forge-data/<skill>/<version>/.skf-audit/<timestamp>/`, and every export the diff lists is one finding: 40 line-only changes give a `MINOR` drift score, and 15 new exports are each graded `HIGH`, so the drift score is `SIGNIFICANT` and `next_workflow` is `update-skill`, even when the report shows them as one row. An export moved to a file outside the skill's scope is reported as moved, not removed, once ccc finds it and ast-grep confirms it. A name found in several files (a `GET` handler in two route files, for example) gets a table of its own for review instead of being paired by guess. A compose-mode stack is now audited by its constituents' freshness instead of stopping with `source-dir-missing` (exit `3`), since it has no source folder: a constituent skill that changed since the stack was composed shows under Constituent Freshness as a `HIGH` finding, so the drift score is `SIGNIFICANT` instead of `CLEAN` and `next_workflow` is `update-skill`. When the export manifest lags the `active` link (after an update, before the next export), the audit reads the provenance map of the version the link names and writes its report and results into that version's folder; before, it compared that version against the older version's provenance. The drift report's frontmatter now records the audited version and why it was chosen, the refs compared and the outcome of the upstream fetch, and its Provenance table shows the audited version and the cosmetic differences the diff collapsed. ([#630](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/630); issues [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587), [#589](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/589), [#597](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/597))
- **skf-audit-skill, skf-test-skill, skf-update-skill, skf-create-stack-skill, skf-drop-skill, skf-rename-skill:** These workflows resolve the version folder and compare versions through one helper, `skf-skill-inventory.py`: its `resolve` command chooses the version a workflow reads (the `active` link's when the export manifest lags it) and returns that version's folder and the metadata, provenance map and evidence report paths, and its `version` command orders two versions, gives the next patch version and a compose-mode stack's next version, and names a code-mode stack's primary library. The knowledge file `version-paths.md` now describes both commands and lists every file a version folder holds, test-skill's gap ledger and audit-skill's per-run stage data included, so no step orders, bumps or resolves a version by hand. ([#633](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/633); issue [#597](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/597))
- **skf-brief-skill:** At the Deep tier with QMD available, an `[auto]` Brief Skill run (`BS[auto]`, as the `forge-auto` pipeline starts it) now indexes the brief it writes into a `<name>-brief` QMD collection and records that collection in `forge-tier.yaml`, as an interactive, headless or ratify run does, so the similarity check of a later interactive Brief Skill run at the Deep tier sees the brief. A QMD problem adds a warning to the run's `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` envelope and never fails the run. Before, a brief written by an `[auto]` run was never indexed. ([#663](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/663))
- **skf-brief-skill:** An `[auto]` brief (`BS[auto]` after `skf-analyze-source`) now keeps every field of the upstream brief, the scope rationale, tier-A list, amendments, registry path, UI variants and demo patterns included, instead of writing some of them empty, and its edit option rewrites only the fields the user changes. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issue [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592))
- **skf-brief-skill:** A docs-only brief now reaches the written brief, with the language `documentation` (or the `language_hint` given), instead of stopping at the repository analysis, which has no repository to read. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issue [#603](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/603))
- **skf-brief-skill:** The interactive draft checkpoint is written once the description is accepted and again after scope definition, so a resumed run keeps the accepted description and, past scope definition, resumes at the brief review instead of redoing the analysis and the scope. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issue [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587))
- **skf-brief-skill:** Brief Skill now reads a public GitHub repository without the GitHub CLI (`gh`), as the Quick, Forge and Forge+ tiers promise: every file it reads is fetched raw through `skf-github-fetch.py`, which falls back to `gh` only for a private repository. Before, a run without `gh`, or with `gh` logged out, halted with `gh-auth-failed` at the target analysis (the `forge <url> <name>` pipeline included), and the first prompt warned that it would. A private repository still needs a logged-in `gh`. ([#659](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/659))
- **skf-brief-skill:** A partial version pin given at the first Brief Skill prompt, such as `react@18`, `django==4.2` or `owner/repo@1.2`, is now named and asked for again before the version is kept, as the version question already said, instead of halting the brief writer after you approved the brief. Every version, typed or taken from the target, now goes through the check `skf-validate-brief-inputs.py` applies to a headless `target_version`, and its error says what a partial pin lacks, such as `'18' is a major version only`. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-brief-skill:** When you ratify an existing brief, `[R] Revise Scope` at the review step now analyzes the brief's repository at the brief's ref before scope definition, so the scope-type recommendation reads the real file list, module count and export count instead of an empty analysis; the brief's name, version and language stay as they were. Brief Skill now honors `headless_mode: true` in the forger preferences (`_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/preferences.yaml`), which it looked for in a file that does not exist, so a project that sets it now runs Brief Skill headless. It also writes the brief's description in `document_output_language`, which it never loaded. The GitHub URL check at the first prompt now accepts a private repository that `gh` can read instead of asking for the URL again; when the repository cannot be read, it names the cause (no such repository, no access, `gh` missing or not logged in) and offers `[K] Keep anyway`. ([#624](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/624); issues [#603](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/603), [#588](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/588))
- **skf-brief-skill:** When you ratify an existing brief (a brief path at the first prompt, or a headless `from_brief`), Brief Skill now writes it from the brief file plus the changes approved at the review step, so the fields it holds are no longer dropped on the rewrite: its `scope.amendments` log, its `scope.tier_a_include` list, its `target_ref` and `source_ref` git refs, and the component-library `scope.registry_path`, `scope.ui_variants` and `scope.demo_patterns` stay as they are unless you change them. Before, the run retyped every field into a new payload for the writer, and a field it left out was lost with no warning. ([#663](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/663))
- **skf-brief-skill:** Brief Skill no longer recommends a scope type by matching words in your intent. "Kickstarter-style" no longer reads as asking for a starter app (`reference-app`), "the whole library, not just the parser" no longer reads as naming one module (`specific-modules`), and "not a demo app or starter template" no longer reads as asking for one. The workflow now judges what the intent asks for and hands the recommendation three signals (a wiring pattern wanted, modules named, only the public API wanted), in interactive runs and in headless runs that pass no `scope_type`. The recommendation also reads the repository's file list and its component registry files itself instead of copies typed out by hand, so on a large repository its component-registry check no longer runs on a shortened list, and a headless run now judges a registry file by its contents, as an interactive run does, instead of by its presence alone. A brief whose scope type came from a wiring-pattern intent now records `reference-app-intent` in `scope.rationale.heuristic` instead of `reference-app-keywords`. ([#624](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/624); issues [#582](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/582), [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592))
- **skf-brief-skill:** Brief Skill passes repository file lists, manifests, entry files and JSON payloads to its helpers through files in a run folder under `_bmad-output/.skf-run/`, never typed into a shell command, so a large repository no longer reaches language, workspace and scope detection cut short, and a source file, description or note with an apostrophe no longer breaks a run. At Forge tier and above, the tier-A list of a monorepo subset comes from the files the recipe runner traces the entry barrel's re-exports to. The run folder is deleted once the brief is written or the run is cancelled. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issues [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592), [#584](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/584))
- **skf-brief-skill:** A `target_version` now also finds its git tag among tags named after the repository, such as `astro@4.16.0` in withastro/astro, so the repository is analyzed at the pinned version; when no tag matches or the tags cannot be listed, the run warns and analyzes the default branch. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issue [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592))
- **skf-brief-skill, skf-create-skill:** On Windows, Brief Skill's check of its headless inputs (`skf-validate-brief-inputs.py`) and the `forge-tier.yaml` registry writes of Brief Skill and Create Skill (`skf-forge-tier-rw.py` `register-qmd-collection` and `register-ccc-index`, and `write-tools` when it takes its payload on stdin) now read the JSON they are piped as UTF-8, so an accented letter or another non-ASCII character in a target path, an intent or an indexed folder's path is no longer garbled. ([#642](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/642); issue [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592))
- **skf-campaign:** A `campaign-brief.yaml` that is not a mapping, or that holds a target with no `name` or no `repo_url`, now stops the pin and provenance stages with exit code `2` (`invalid-input`) and the error code `INVALID_BRIEF`, naming the target by its number (and its name when it has one) and the key it lacks. Before, `campaign-validate-pins.py` and `campaign-provenance.py` crashed with a traceback and exit code `1`, which the pin stage reads as invalid pins (exit `5`) and the provenance stage as inaccessible repositories (exit `6`). ([#655](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/655))
- **skf-campaign:** A directive `## Quality Overrides` item that holds more than the documented `soft_target` and `soft_fallback` pairs, such as `- react: soft_target 80 (was soft_target 90)`, or that names a key twice, is now reported in `unparsed` and logged to the decision log, and none of its numbers apply. Before, `campaign-quality-gate.py` took every number it found in the item and applied the last one, so the gate a skill was tested and exported against could be a value the operator never meant, with nothing logged, although the directive spec says an item it cannot read is never applied. ([#662](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/662))
- **skf-campaign:** Campaign's Export stage now finds a Tier B skill whose campaign target name differs from the library name Quick Skill gave its package, as in a scoped monorepo target: it runs `skf-export-skill` under the skill folder the build wrote, the new `export_name` that `campaign-quality-gate.py classify` returns for each skill (the last part of its recorded `skill_path`, else its name), and the Export table shows it. Before, Export ran `skf-export-skill` under the campaign target name, so that skill passed the gate and the confirmation, then failed with `resolution-failure`. The capstone names its constituent skills by the same `export_name`. ([#655](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/655))
- **skf-campaign:** An interrupted campaign now resumes at the right stage, including one interrupted during the Tier B batch: `campaign resume` takes its stage from the new `campaign-state.py resume`, which sends a skill left `active` back to its own stage (the skill loop for Tier A, the batch for Tier B, whose script batches the interrupted skills again) and otherwise continues after the last completed stage, never past the report stage. Every write to `_campaign-state.yaml` now goes through `campaign-state.py`, which validates the state before and after the write, copies only a valid state to `_campaign-state.yaml.bak`, stamps `last_updated`, `started_at` and `completed_at` from the clock in UTC, and writes atomically, so a bad write can no longer overwrite the last good backup. Strategy records its stage only after the plan is confirmed, so a campaign cancelled at the plan shows the plan again when it resumes, and the cancel message now says to re-run `campaign` and choose overwrite to change targets. A Tier B skill pinned to a branch is batched as `<repo>/tree/<branch>`, which quick-skill reads as that branch, instead of `<repo>@<branch>`, which it could not read. The provenance stage reports a repository that does not exist as `not-found` instead of a network failure, an organization's SSO or SAML refusal as `forbidden` instead of a rate limit, and an offline `gh` as `network`, each result with its `error_class`, and shows a shared root cause above the per-repo errors. The Tier A kickoff lists each workaround pre-apply applied as a readable line. Decision log entries are now typed and timestamped (`decision`, `auto` or `event`). Overwriting a campaign now moves its state, its `.bak` and its brief into a new folder under `archive/` through `campaign-state.py archive`, and Setup refuses to write over an existing state or backup. A headless `campaign resume` of a campaign that already finished ends with its `SKF_CAMPAIGN_RESULT_JSON` success line (exit code `0`) instead of an error envelope, and a resume of a Setup that stopped before it wrote the brief halts at once with exit code `8` (`missing-brief`) and says to start the campaign again. `campaign resume` no longer asks whether to recover from a backup that looks newer than the primary state (a headless resume already kept the primary): every write now leaves the backup one write behind the primary, so only a hand edit could make it newer. ([#641](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/641); issues [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587), [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585), [#582](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/582))
- **skf-create-skill:** Create Skill's ast-grep recipes now find the exports they target (checked on ast-grep 0.45.3). Before, the JavaScript and React function recipes and the Rust recipe matched nothing, and `python-public-functions` and the re-export pattern were rejected, so extraction fell back to reading files by eye (`T1-low`); the Python recipes also returned methods and nested definitions, which were then documented as public functions. New recipes cover `export { a, b as c } from`, `export * as ns from` and Vue `defineProps` in `<script setup lang="tsx">`. ([#553](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/553))
- **skf-create-skill:** At Forge, Forge+ and Deep tiers, Create Skill now extracts a source in a language the ast-grep recipes read (Python, JavaScript or TypeScript with `.vue` files, Rust or Go) through one script, `skf-extract-public-api.py --mode full`, which runs every recipe over the files in scope and records each export's file, line, recipe and node kind, instead of the model running each recipe, applying the brief's globs, merging the matches and copying node kinds by hand, so every run records the same exports and lines. A brief in another language, such as Java, Kotlin, C#, Ruby, Swift or PHP, is extracted by source reading (T1-low), as at Quick tier. Before, extraction followed an outdated copy of the extraction protocol that sent a scope of 100 files or fewer to `find_code` patterns, which also return methods, nested definitions and private names, and cited a decorated class at its decorator line. `**` in `scope.include` and `scope.exclude` now also matches no folder at all, as it already did in the authoritative-files scan (`src/**/*.ts` matches `src/index.ts`, and `**/test_*` leaves out a top-level `test_x.py`), so a new run can put a different set of files, and of exports, in scope than an earlier one. `exports_public_api`, `exports_internal` and `effective_denominator` in `metadata.json` come from the same script's reading of the package's entry points, and a recipe that reaches its head cap is reported in the extraction summary and the evidence report instead of dropping exports silently. At validation, the provenance verifier's `fix` rewrites mismatched citation prefixes and moves the source lines that have one answer, and a node kind to repair comes from the verifier's `kind-at` lookup. ([#628](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/628); issue [#584](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/584))
- **skf-create-skill:** Create Skill now looks for authoritative AI documentation files that a brief's scope leaves out (`llms.txt`, `AGENTS.md` and the like) after it has cloned a remote source, in the workspace or temporary clone, instead of handing the scanner the repository URL, which failed with `source-root not a directory`. When a remote source is not cloned, at Quick tier or after a failed clone, the scan is skipped with a notice that the evidence report also lists. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#525](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/525))
- **skf-create-skill:** Under `--batch`, a HARD HALT in steps 1 to 7 now ends only its brief even after a context compaction, and from step 3 on it also closes the private source tree, so the batch summary is still written and the remaining briefs still compile. ([#656](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/656))
- **skf-create-skill:** A component-library skill's `extraction-rules.yaml` now records the `component-library` recipe set and the recipes the runner ran, and its extraction inventory carries the runner's exports, counts and warnings as the runner wrote them. ([#656](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/656))
- **skf-create-skill:** On a Deep-tier run, Create Skill's doc-rot scan now reads the changelog, release, issue and pull request text it fetched from GitHub, also when it reuses a fetch from the last 7 days, and a `## CORRECTION` block it finds there cites the fetched file as `[QMD:<skill>-temporal:<file>]`. The fetched files used to be deleted once they were indexed, and a reused fetch had none, so a deprecation or removal found only in upstream history never became a `## CORRECTION` block, and nothing said so. The files now stay in a `.skf-temporal/` folder inside the skill's folder under `forge_data_folder`, which keeps itself out of git and is replaced only by a later fetch that gets at least one file, so a failed refresh keeps the last good copy. When that folder holds no fetched file, the evidence report says so under Remaining Warnings. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#524](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/524))
- **skf-create-skill:** An authoritative file, such as `llms.txt` or `AGENTS.md`, that an earlier headless run skipped no longer counts as a person's decline. A headless run records that skip in the brief's `scope.amendments` with `action: "skipped"` and `reason: "headless: no user to prompt"`, and the next interactive `skf-create-skill` run used to leave the file out without asking. It now asks about the file, as it does for one no run has decided. A skip a person chose still holds, and briefs need no change. ([#616](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/616); issue [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))
- **skf-create-skill:** Create Skill's documentation fetch no longer tells a whole-language reference to skip the step that keeps the registry pages and writes the Language Guide index, so the compiled skill keeps its foregrounded Language Guide instead of being assembled as a standard library skill. ([#656](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/656))
- **skf-create-skill:** At Forge tier and above, Create Skill now reads a remote source from a private tree at the resolved commit, made by `skf-source-tree.py resolve` in one call that also matches `target_version` or `version` against the repository's tags and moves SKF's workspace clone to that commit. Another run can no longer move the files while this one extracts them (the lock the steps asked to hold across tool calls could never be held), and a clone that cannot be moved no longer sends the run to a temporary clone. The workspace clone's ccc settings are merged by `skf-merge-ccc-exclusions.py --clone-root`, and `forge-tier.yaml` registry entries (`qmd_collections` and `ccc_index_registry`) are written only by `skf-forge-tier-rw.py`, which holds `forge-tier.yaml.lock` for each write, so concurrent `--batch` runs keep each other's entries and a failed `qmd collection add` removes its entry again. When several tags match the brief's version, a headless run now takes the first in priority order and logs the choice instead of waiting for an answer. ([#622](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/622); issues [#588](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/588), [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592))
- **skf-create-skill:** On native Windows, Create Skill no longer fails before extracting anything when it runs ast-grep from the command line, which it does without the ast-grep MCP tools or for a source of more than 500 files. ([#554](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/554))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-brief-skill:** Create Skill's documentation fetch now measures each fetched page with `skf-detect-docs.py page-metrics` and keeps a discovered subpage only when `skf-detect-docs.py filter-urls` finds it on the root page's own site, instead of counting words and link lines by eye, so a miscount no longer skips subpage discovery or fetches subpages for nothing. `filter-urls` also reads a gzipped sitemap, and leaves a page out only when a whole path segment is `blog`, `changelog`, `pricing`, `about` or `careers`, so a section such as `about-the-rest-api` keeps its pages. A subpage that fails to fetch no longer leaves a stub page in the docs corpus. A Deep run fetches its temporal context through the new `skf-fetch-temporal.py`, which writes the same issue, pull request, release and changelog files from the same upstream data on every run, reads the notes of each release, which the release loop as written never handed to its formatter, and keeps the last good files when a refresh fetches nothing but failure notes. Enrichment now searches only the skill's own temporal and docs collections, so another skill's issues or docs no longer annotate it, and a skill with only a docs collection is enriched as well. Component-library extraction runs its recipes through `skf-extract-public-api.py` in one call, and a docs-only skill now keeps its fetched pages in `_bmad-output/<skill>-docs/` at every tier, as it already did at Deep tier. On Windows, `skf-write-skill-brief.py` now reads the brief context it is piped as UTF-8, so an accented letter in a description or an amendment's reason is no longer garbled. ([#628](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/628); issue [#605](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/605))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-campaign:** Create Skill's doc-rot step no longer reports lines such as "No breaking changes in this release" as breaking changes. It turned every changelog, release, issue, pull request or skill line holding a keyword such as "breaking" or "deprecated" into a `## CORRECTION` block, so negations, "non-breaking" notes, bug fix notes, pull request template checkboxes and old release history shipped as corrections, and the skill's own lines were kept ahead of upstream ones when the list was cut to 10. The keyword scan now only proposes candidates: the step keeps a candidate only when the project itself announces a change (an issue report alone is not enough) that is in effect at the skill's version and concerns one of the skill's exports, keeps one correction per change, and turns at most 10 of those into `## CORRECTION` blocks, each citing its source by annotation or file name instead of a local file path. A campaign records only those kept corrections in a skill's `workarounds_applied`, and its report no longer says doc-rot corrections stay out of campaign state. ([#622](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/622); issue [#582](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/582))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-create-stack-skill:** Create Skill applies new ccc exclusions with a plain `ccc index` instead of `ccc reset --force`, which threw the whole index away and could leave none. Its standard exclusions (such as `node_modules`) are now written in a form ccc matches, and they are applied when an existing index is reused too. Discovery searches with `ccc search --refresh`, which also makes Stack Skill's ccc step run after the first Setup run; before, it never did. When Create Skill builds an index on demand, it no longer runs `ccc init` or `ccc index` in the project root or the enclosing git checkout, which built an index there without SKF's exclusions. ([#493](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/493), [#499](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/499), [#509](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/509); issue [#496](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/496))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-update-skill:** The node kind recorded for an ast-grep provenance entry (`ast_node_type`) now comes from the recipe that matched, and Create Skill and Update Skill check it with ast-grep. A kind ast-grep does not know, such as `async_function_definition`, is replaced with the recipe's kind, or kept with a warning when no recipe matches the entry's line. Before, the kind was guessed and nothing checked it. ([#553](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/553); issue [#530](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/530))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-update-skill:** In an installed project, Create Skill and Update Skill now find the description guard's instructions, which keep `skill-check` from replacing or truncating a skill's `description` when it rewrites `SKILL.md`. They pointed at a path that exists only in SKF's own repository. ([#517](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/517); issue [#512](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/512))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-update-skill:** The metadata stats check now flags a provenance entry read by eye (`source-read` or `source_reading`) that carries `signature_source: T1`, whatever its `confidence`: `T2`, `T3` or none as well as `T1` and `T1-low`. Before, only `T1` and `T1-low` entries were checked, so such an entry passed and counted in the `t1` bin of `confidence_distribution`. Create Skill and Update Skill relabel it to `T1-low`, as they already do for the entries the check covered. No published provenance map holds such an entry. ([#623](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/623); issue [#555](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/555))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-update-skill:** The provenance check no longer compares a reference app's documented exports with its provenance entries. A reference app documents no exports by design while its entries follow each citation, so every entry came back as orphaned: Create Skill listed each one under Remaining Warnings, and Update Skill recorded `Provenance: WARN` on every run with one warning per entry. For a skill whose `metadata.json` has `scope_type: reference-app`, the check now reports no missing or orphaned exports, marks the comparison `set_diff: not-applicable`, and still checks source lines and citation prefixes. ([#617](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/617); issue [#549](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/549))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-update-skill, skf-audit-skill:** ccc index folders and SKF's workspace lock no longer show up as changes in git. In the source clones SKF keeps, their ignore entries go to `.git/info/exclude` instead of `.gitignore`, so switching a clone to another ref works again instead of cloning afresh on every run, and Audit Skill's `[C]` option no longer stops on the lock file or stashes it. After `[T] Transient stash` in that option, the stash stays in the clone until you run `git -C <source> stash pop`, as the menu now says. An index Create Skill builds for a local source inside another git checkout now ignores itself. ([#509](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/509); issues [#503](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/503), [#505](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/505))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-update-skill, skf-audit-skill:** Provenance entries are labeled by the tool that read the export, at every tier: an ast-grep match is `T1` with its node kind and an `[AST:]` citation, an export read by eye is `T1-low` with `[SRC:]`. Before, the label followed the forge tier, so skills built at Forge or Deep claimed `T1` for exports nothing parsed. Expect lower `T1` counts in new and updated skills. Audit Skill labels its re-index the same way, lists label changes in their own table and does not count them as drift. ([#531](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/531), [#533](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/533); issue [#530](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/530))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-update-skill, skf-test-skill:** Create Skill and Update Skill now check that each provenance entry points at the line that defines its export and that each `[AST:]` or `[SRC:]` citation matches how its entry was read, fix what has a single answer and warn about the rest. Test Skill reports a wrong line as a Low gap and a line it cannot verify as Info. A repair from a test report (`--from-test-report`) now also adds the entry for a cited export the map did not have. ([#532](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/532), [#551](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/551); issue [#530](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/530))
- **skf-create-skill, skf-update-skill, skf-test-skill:** The provenance check now accepts the definition lines the ast-grep recipes cite for four shapes it used to reject: a later name in one `export const` list, on the same line or on a line of its own as Prettier writes it (`export const a = 1, B = 2;`, or `export const a = 1,` with `B = 2;` on the next line), a quoted name in a re-export (`"str-name" as alias`, `export * as "ns" from`), a name on the line after its keyword (`export function` or `export interface` with the name below, or a Python `def \` continued on the next line) and a decorator on the `export` line (`@dec export class H {}`). Before, Create Skill and Update Skill listed these exports as unverified and Test Skill added an Info gap for each. ([#617](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/617); issue [#560](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/560))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill moves the library catalog out of `SKILL.md` into `references/stack-catalog.md` for a large stack (more than 6 libraries or more than 6 integration patterns), or when the `SKILL.md` body runs over its 500-line limit. Each library link in that file now names the reference file beside it (`{name}.md`), so the links open instead of pointing into a `references/references/` folder that does not exist. A stack forged before this fix keeps its broken catalog links until you forge it again. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#535](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/535))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill in compose mode takes each constituent's tier from the evidence the constituent recorded, the largest bin of its `confidence_distribution` (`T3` included, a tie going to the weaker tier), instead of from where its export list came from, which read `T1` for every skill whose `metadata.json` lists exports. Each provenance entry cited from a constituent carries that tier. Every integration then takes the weaker of its two constituents' tiers, a pair with a `T2` member included: a `T1` and `T2` pair is `T2 [composed]`, not `T1 [composed, +T2 annotations]`. Compose-mode integration tiers and the stack's `confidence_tier` can drop, for example for a docs-only constituent whose evidence is all `T3`. ([#623](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/623); issue [#534](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/534))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill in compose mode now checks, before it commits the stack, that each provenance entry's `export_name` appears verbatim (case-sensitive) in the staged `SKILL.md` or in a file directly in `references/`, the files Test Skill searches to credit a name toward Export Coverage. A name that fails is set to the literal identifier the stack documents, or its entry is dropped, and a headless run drops it; each change is listed as a warning in the evidence report. Before, a descriptive label, or a name documented only in `references/integrations/`, was committed and lowered the stack's Export Coverage in Test Skill. ([#623](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/623); issue [#538](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/538))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** A compose-mode `provenance-map.json` is now written by SKF's provenance helper instead of being assembled by hand. Each constituent's `metadata_hash` is copied exactly from the inventory the run read, each `skill_path` is the constituent's folder relative to the project root, so it resolves when `skills_output_folder` is not the default `skills`, and each entry takes its constituent's tier as its `confidence` and `signature_source`. An Audit Skill run on the stack no longer reports false constituent drift or missing constituents. ([#657](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/657))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill in compose mode no longer runs the import file-list intersection when it detects integrations. Compose mode counts no imports, so that pass had no input and reported a pruned pair count for an intersection that never ran. Compose mode now goes straight to its own pairs, taken from the architecture document, or from shared domains when there is no architecture document. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#537](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/537))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** A stack skill's `confidence_distribution` in `metadata.json` now counts each library once, by the library's tier, so its four bins sum to `library_count`, the sum validation checks. Before, nothing said what one count stood for, and a run could bin each provenance entry instead (22 entries against 3 libraries, say) and publish the stack with a validation issue it could no longer fix. The compilation review and the final summary show these library counts, `T3` included, and the evidence report's confidence tier distribution counts the provenance entries, one per entry by its `signature_source`. ([#623](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/623); issue [#528](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/528))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill in code mode labels each export by the tool that read it, at every forge tier: an export an ast-grep rule matched is `T1` (`ast_bridge`), an export read by eye is `T1-low` (`source_reading`) even at Forge or Deep, and a library is `T1` only when an ast-grep rule matched every export it recorded. Before, every export at Forge or Deep read `T1` unless ast-grep failed, so a stack could claim `T1` for code nothing parsed. The labels are now checked before integrations are detected, and in Python and TypeScript or JavaScript sources each provenance entry's `source_line` is checked against the line that defines its export: a decorator line moves to the `def` below it, and any other line the check cannot confirm is listed as a warning in the evidence report. Expect lower integration tiers and a lower `confidence_tier` on some code-mode stacks. ([#623](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/623); issue [#555](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/555))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill's manifest reference now lists the manifest files it actually scans: `setup.cfg`, `build.gradle.kts` and `Package.swift` are added, and `*.csproj`, which the scanner never read, is removed. The table also names each ecosystem's runtime and development dependency keys, and a test now fails whenever it drifts from `skf-scan-manifests.py`. ([#617](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/617); issues [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596), [#598](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/598))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill's report step no longer forbids every file write while it writes the result contract. Its rules now allow the two result files, the per-run record in `<forge data folder>/<project>-stack/<version>/` and `create-stack-skill-result-latest.json` in `<forge data folder>/<project>-stack/`, and keep the rest of the report on screen, so a run no longer skips the result file that pipelines read. When `skf-atomic-write.py` cannot be found, the report skips the two result files with a warning and finishes instead of halting, and a configured `on_complete` command still runs. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#539](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/539))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** When skill-check is not installed, Stack Skill now records a `skill-check-unavailable` warning, and its final summary lists `skill-check unavailable: manual fallback checks used, security scan skipped`, so a run checked only by the fallback no longer reads as validated by skill-check. ([#519](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/519), [#522](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/522))
- **skf-create-stack-skill:** Stack Skill's validation step no longer calls itself read-only. Its rules now say that only `skill-check --fix` and the split of an oversized `SKILL.md` body into `references/` change the published skill, and that every other check leaves it as written, so a run no longer has to choose between the rule and those fixes. The frontmatter and body-size check that runs before the skill is published now records its warnings before `evidence-report.md` is written, so the report lists them, and the warning list in the final summary shows them too. A failure to point the `active` link at the new version now also shows in that list. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issues [#536](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/536), [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585))
- **skf-drop-skill:** Drop Skill's refusal to drop the active version while other non-deprecated versions remain now names a recovery that works: point the skill's `active` link at the version to keep (`ln -sfn <version to keep> <skills_output_folder>/<name>/active`, with the newest version it can keep filled in), run `@Ferris EX` on the skill, which exports that version and archives the one you meant to drop, then drop it. Before, the refusal said to re-run Export Skill with a different version selected, but Export Skill has no version choice: following it exported the same version again, and the drop was refused again. The guard refuses the same drops as before, with exit `5` (`active-version-guard-refused`). ([#663](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/663))
- **skf-drop-skill:** Deprecating a whole skill (`--mode deprecate` with `version=all`, or the deprecate option after choosing all versions) now marks every version of the skill `deprecated` in `.export-manifest.json` and keeps its entry, as the deprecate option and the drop report promise. The drop stays reversible: set a version's `status` back to `active` or `archived` and re-run Export Skill. Before, it deleted the skill's manifest entry, so there was no status to restore, the next drop listed the skill as a draft that can only be purged, and when it was the last exported skill, the next Export Skill run exported it again. A purge still deletes the entry. ([#639](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/639))
- **skf-drop-skill, skf-rename-skill:** Drop Skill and Rename Skill no longer write a second pair of SKF markers into the managed section of `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` or `.cursorrules` when they rebuild it. ([#517](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/517))
- **skf-export-skill:** Export Skill with `--dry-run` no longer moves an SKF skill that still uses the old flat layout into the versioned layout. It reads the flat skill where it is, says it would migrate it, and writes nothing. ([#499](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/499); issue [#497](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/497))
- **skf-export-skill:** Export Skill now reports a missing recommended metadata field through its export gate, so the package status it shows (`READY` or `WARNINGS`) is always the gate's own verdict. The gate lists each missing or empty field under `validation.metadata.recommended_missing` as a low warning: `description`, `source_repo`, `language` and `tool_versions` for a single skill, plus `ast_node_count` when the skill counts AST-verified (T1) signatures, and `language` and `tool_versions` for a stack. A package missing one of them shows `WARNINGS` and still exports, and a stack is no longer warned about fields its template does not write. ([#617](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/617); issue [#607](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/607))
- **skf-export-skill:** The export gate finds Section 7b by its `## Scripts & Assets` heading, with or without a section number, outside fenced code. A heading that only mentions scripts or assets, such as `### Loading assets from JavaScript`, or a shell comment in a code block no longer halts a valid skill with `NOT_READY`. `validation.crossref_7b` gains `heading` and `heading_line`, the heading the gate matched and its line in `SKILL.md`, and the halt message names both, with the fix to make in the package. ([#639](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/639); issue [#582](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/582))
- **skf-export-skill:** Export now writes each context file's managed section with that file's own skill root. With `ides` set to `claude-code` and `codex`, the `AGENTS.md` section listed every skill under `.claude/skills/`, the root of the first configured IDE; it now lists them under `.agents/skills/`, Codex's folder, while `CLAUDE.md` keeps `.claude/skills/`. `snippet_skill_root_override` still sets one root for every file. A headless halt at context-file resolution now reports the resolved skills in its envelope's `skills`, as every later halt does. ([#646](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/646); issues [#591](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/591), [#600](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/600))
- **skf-export-skill:** Export Skill no longer halts on a stack skill whose `confidence_tier` holds the dominant confidence tier of its libraries (`T1`, `T1-low`, `T2` or `T3`), the value Stack Skill writes there. The export gate accepted only the forge tiers (`Quick`, `Forge`, `Forge+`, `Deep`), so these stacks failed the gate and `--all` stopped before exporting the skills beside them. The gate now checks `confidence_tier` against the scale of the skill's `skill_type`, so these stacks export without being forged again. An older stack that still holds its forge tier in `confidence_tier` keeps exporting as before, now with a warning that re-running Stack Skill records the dominant tier. The gate also reads a `scripts/` or `assets/` path in the Scripts & Assets section only from the start of a path, so an upstream file such as `bmad-module-builder/scripts/validate-module.py` is no longer reported as a bundled file missing from the package. ([#617](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/617); issue [#552](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/552))
- **skf-export-skill, skf-drop-skill, skf-rename-skill:** Export Skill, Drop Skill and Rename Skill no longer change a context file (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` or `.cursorrules`) where a `<!-- SKF:BEGIN` marker has no `<!-- SKF:END -->` of its own, for example after the end marker was deleted. Export Skill stops there, and Drop Skill and Rename Skill report that file as failed and rebuild the others. Before, Export Skill added a second managed section below the unclosed marker, and the next rebuild deleted the lines between the unclosed marker and that new section, your own lines included. Restore the end marker, or delete the stray begin marker, and run the workflow again. A managed section that still holds the second marker pair Drop Skill and Rename Skill used to write inside it now rebuilds into one section with one marker pair, and the extra `<!-- SKF:END -->` lines those rebuilds left below it are removed. ([#617](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/617); issue [#591](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/591))
- **skf-forger:** A headless `maintain` chain whose audit found that upstream moved now always hands the new ref to Update Skill. The forger's gate, `pipeline-gate.py`, prints Audit Skill's `upstream_ref` as `target_ref` in a new `handoff` field of its decision, and `pipeline-journal.py step --gate` records it in the chain's journal, so the update no longer runs at the skill's old pin while the chain goes on to test and export it as a success. The ref is kept after a `CRITICAL` audit too, so the update that resumes the chain after you review the drift report reads it. The gate's JSON output gains the `handoff` key, null after every other decision. ([#658](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/658); issue [#588](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/588))
- **skf-quick-skill:** Quick Skill keeps the description you approved when `skill-check --fix` rewrites it. The fix now runs inside the description guard that Create Skill and Update Skill use: the approved description goes back into `SKILL.md`, so it matches `metadata.json` again, and the validation results note the restore. When `skf-description-guard.py` is missing, `skill-check` runs without `--fix` and the validation results say so. The review screen also shows `metadata.json` as one line (version, confidence tier and exports documented) instead of the whole file, which is written in the next step. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#609](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/609))
- **skf-quick-skill:** Quick Skill now finds a package's entry files in the repository listing with the rules Create Skill's extractor follows, instead of guessing them from the package name: a Python package whose import name differs from its distribution name (Pillow's `PIL`, scikit-learn's `sklearn`) or a single-module package (`six.py`) gets its exports, its name read from whichever of `pyproject.toml`, `setup.py` or `setup.cfg` gives one, a `package.json` whose `main` or `types` names an uncommitted build output (`dist/index.js`) is read from its source (`src/index.ts`), a crate's `[lib] path` is honoured and a Maven package's folder comes from its `pom.xml` group. Before, such a package compiled a README-only skill with low confidence and an empty Key Exports section. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-quick-skill:** Quick Skill now follows `export * from` and `module.exports = require(...)` chains, NodeNext `./core.js` specifiers that name `core.ts` included, and Python star imports such as `from .core import *` to the module that defines the names, reading each module in turn until the chain ends (less its default export, which `export *` does not pass on), instead of guessing the names from the README. Only a statement whose module is not in the repository (a package import, a spread, a Rust glob) is still read by eye. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-quick-skill:** A repository of skills written in Markdown alone is no longer refused for having no language: it is documented as a skills module, with `language` `markdown`. A Go module path such as `go.uber.org/zap`, or a Maven coordinate, now goes through the web search for its GitHub repository before the redirect. A large source tree, or a source file holding an apostrophe, now reaches the extractor whole, and `metadata.json` is rendered from the extraction files, so a description holding quotes or an apostrophe reaches it as written and the SKF version is read from the installed module. ([#634](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/634); issues [#527](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/527), [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592), [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594))
- **skf-quick-skill, skf-brief-skill, skf-test-skill:** The Quick-tier export scan (`skf-extract-public-api.py --mode quick`) reads more export forms, and names in `warnings` the statements whose names the entry file alone cannot give. It now lists Python names re-exported with `from ... import` (a nested package's absolute imports of its own modules included), `async def` functions and every public name the module's `__all__` lists, a tuple or one built with `+=` or `.extend()` included; Rust `pub use` re-exports, `#[macro_export]` macros and `pub async fn`, `pub unsafe fn` and `pub extern "C" fn` items, with `pub const fn f` listed as `f` instead of an export named `fn`; and JavaScript and TypeScript `export async function`, `export let` and `var`, every name of `export const a = 1, b = 2`, `export abstract class`, `export declare`, `export function*`, `export * as ns`, `export default <name>` and TypeScript's `export = <name>`, and CommonJS `module.exports` (Express's `exports = module.exports = <name>` included) and `exports.<name>` assignments. A statement whose names the entry file alone cannot give (`export * from`, a star import from the package, an `__all__` built from another module's `__all__`, `pub use x::*`, `module.exports = require(...)`, a `module.exports` set inside an `if` or a block) is now named in the result's `warnings`, as are an anonymous `export default` and a destructuring `export const`; an indented Rust `pub fn` (a method) is no longer listed as a top-level export, and a regular expression such as `/\/*$/` no longer hides the exports after it. Quick Skill's Key Exports, the export list Brief Skill shows when it analyzes a target, and Test Skill's Quick-tier coverage of a Quick Skill all come from this scan. ([#626](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/626); issues [#584](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/584), [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592))
- **skf-refine-architecture:** Refine Architecture now checks the architecture's API claims against each skill's full export list, not only the exports its `SKILL.md` shows. The readers it sends to each skill get the export names from the skill inventory and read the skill's `references/` files for the signatures `SKILL.md` leaves out, and a claim fails on existence only when the name is missing from that list. Skills whose full API lives in `references/` no longer get false API Mismatch warnings in the refined document. ([#657](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/657))
- **skf-refine-architecture:** Refine Architecture's improvement step now suggests unused capabilities and cross-library synergies only for the skills in the document's scope, as its gap and issue steps already did. Before, it looked at every skill in the skills folder, so a skill that `--scope-skills` or the derived scope left out could still add an enhancement subsection to the refined document. An improvement or synergy that involves a skill outside the scope is now listed for awareness only and stays out of the refined document. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#529](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/529))
- **skf-refine-architecture:** A team's refinement rules copy (`refinement_rules_path`) whose VS Report Integration Raises cell names a severity tier and also says No issue or None, such as `No issue, even when the rationale notes minor caveats`, now stops Refine Architecture before any analysis with exit `3` (`resolution-failure`, phase `init:rules`) and a `vs-raises` violation that asks you to bold the one it raises, instead of silently raising that tier for every matching [VS] verdict. A bolded No issue or None now raises no issue whatever tier the rest of the cell mentions, as a bolded tier already decides for its row, so `**No issue**, even when the rationale notes minor caveats` maps its verdict to no issue. ([#663](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/663))
- **skf-rename-skill:** Rename Skill rolls back only a failure up to the manifest re-key: a copy, a file rewrite, the leftover check or the manifest update that fails deletes the new folders and leaves the old skill as it was. A context file that fails to rebuild after the re-key is listed in `context_files_failed` and never undoes the rename, as the exit codes and the docs already said. The step file used to count that rebuild inside the rollback, which could delete the renamed skill after the manifest had moved to the new name. ([#625](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/625); issue [#611](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/611))
- **skf-rename-skill:** Rename Skill works again. A skill with an `active` link always rolled back with `write-failed`, and a skill SKF made then failed the final check for leftovers of the old name with `verify-failed`, because the old name stayed in each context snippet and fields that default to the skill name (`source_library`, `source_package`) counted as leftovers. A skill named after its library or repository can still stop with `verify-failed`, for example on the README link Create Skill records in its metadata: such a skill cannot be renamed, so create it again under the new name, as the troubleshooting guide explains. ([#517](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/517); issue [#510](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/510))
- **skf-setup:** SKF's ccc exclusions now cover only what SKF generated. Folder patterns are anchored to the project root (`skills`, not `**/skills`), so a folder with the same name deeper in the project stays searchable. A skills folder shared with other skills is excluded entry by entry, a folder with none of SKF's output (your own source, for example) stays indexed with a warning, and patterns left from an earlier folder setting are removed. Setup also warns on every run when a `!` entry you added to `exclude_patterns` brings SKF's output back into the index. ([#493](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/493), [#499](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/499), [#509](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/509); issues [#491](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/491), [#492](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/492), [#495](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/495), [#506](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/506))
- **skf-setup:** Setup now runs `ccc init` before it adds SKF's exclusions, so ccc keeps its own defaults (hidden folders, `node_modules`, the index database) and adds `/.cocoindex_code/` to `.gitignore`. Before, SKF wrote `settings.yml` first, ccc skipped both, and the index database was indexed and showed up in `git status`. Setup rebuilds a `settings.yml` an earlier run damaged, keeping your entries, warns when `/.cocoindex_code/` is not gitignored, and with `--ccc-skip-index` still prepares `settings.yml` and skips only the index build. ([#493](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/493); issue [#490](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/490))
- **skf-setup:** On Deep tier, setup no longer offers another project's QMD collections for removal. QMD keeps one index for every project on the machine, and setup used to call any collection with an SKF suffix that its own registry did not list an orphan, so the orphan question, or `--orphan-action=remove`, could delete collections that another SKF project still uses. A collection now counts as an orphan only when the folder QMD indexes it from lies inside this project; the question shows that folder for each one, and the removal checks it again just before it deletes a collection and leaves alone one that no longer points into this project. ([#641](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/641))
- **skf-setup:** Under `--quiet` or `--headless`, the `SKF_SETUP_RESULT_JSON` envelope is now the only line Setup prints, halts included, as the docs promised: no status banner, progress lines or health-check output. `--quiet` no longer stops to ask whether to remove orphaned QMD collections: it keeps them, as `--headless` does, and records `quiet-default` in the envelope warnings. Pass `--orphan-action=remove` to remove them without a prompt. ([#499](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/499); issue [#494](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/494))
- **skf-setup:** A Setup re-run no longer says the ccc index is current when it did not check it (`--ccc-skip-index`) or when there is no index. The closing line now states only what the run checked. ([#517](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/517); issue [#514](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/514))
- **skf-setup:** Setup now reads and writes its sidecar files under the `sidecar_path` that `_bmad/skf/config.yaml` sets, as every other workflow does, instead of always under `_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/`, so a project with a custom sidecar folder gets its `forge-tier.yaml` where the other workflows look for it. Setup's helpers now pass their results to each other by file: the helper that prepares the ccc settings also builds the index and stamps its time and file count from `ccc status` with its own clock, and `forge-tier.yaml`, the FORGE STATUS report and the `SKF_SETUP_RESULT_JSON` envelope read the detector's and that helper's results from the run folder, so no step types a timestamp, a count or the SKF exclusion record back by hand. When that call is stopped before the index finishes, the envelope's `ccc_index.status` is `failed`, and the exclusion patterns it already merged into `settings.yml` are still recorded. The first-run and re-run notices no longer tell you to press Esc before anything is written, a pause the run never gave, and the "Proceeding to ..." lines between steps are gone. ([#641](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/641); issues [#592](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/592), [#596](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/596), [#599](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/599), [#600](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/600))
- **skf-setup, skf-create-stack-skill, skf-test-skill, skf-verify-stack:** Five helper scripts no longer stop with `UnicodeEncodeError` when their `--help` runs on a Windows console, whose cp1252 output cannot print every character of the help text: `skf-detect-tools.py`, `skf-enumerate-stack-skills.py`, `skf-qmd-classify-collections.py`, `verify-declared-numerator.py` and `skf-report-delta.py` now write UTF-8. Verify Stack's synthesis step points the model at `skf-report-delta.py --help`, so on Windows that step could not read the script's contract. ([#623](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/623))
- **skf-test-skill:** The naive coherence check no longer raises a High async finding because an export's description holds the word `async`. A script now finds where the skill describes each exported function and every call to it in the examples, and whether each call awaits it, and the check reads what the descriptions say about that export. So a sync function documented beside its async variant (`readFileSync`, "the sync counterpart of the async `readFile`") passes, and a call awaited through `Promise.all`, `asyncio.run`, `async for` or Rust's `.await` counts as awaited. A skill whose example leaves a documented async call unawaited still gets the High finding, as does one whose example awaits a function the skill documents as sync. ([#624](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/624); issue [#582](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/582))
- **skf-test-skill:** Test Skill's discovery test now counts only the folders in the skills folder that hold a skill: it skips SKF's own `_batch/` folder and finds a versioned skill's `SKILL.md` through its `active` link. Before, it counted every folder, so the routing test could run with fewer than two real skills and inflate the Discovery score. ([#509](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/509))
- **skf-test-skill:** Test Skill now counts its discovery test in the Gap Report. A skipped discovery test (`--no-discovery`, a skills folder with fewer than two skills, or no subagents to route the prompts) is a numbered Info gap, and a discovery test that misroutes one of its three prompts is a Medium gap, two or three a High one. Each is counted in the Gap Report's totals and in the gap counts the final summary shows. Discovery still runs after the hard gate, so a discovery gap never blocks a run: the Gap Report counts a High one on a line of its own, outside the blocking total. ([#627](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/627); issue [#543](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/543))
- **skf-test-skill:** Test Skill now reads a skill's `provenance-map.json` and `evidence-report.md` from the skill's version folder in the forge data, `forge-data/<skill>/<version>/`, where Create Skill and Stack Skill write them, and falls back to the flat `forge-data/<skill>/` folder only when the version folder has no copy. Before, it looked only in the flat folder, which versioned skills no longer use, so a skill with no local source was checked from its metadata alone with no signature check, a stack lost the cited contracts behind its Export Coverage denominator, the export-count drift check left the provenance map out, the Deep-tier check of the Migration & Deprecation section never ran, and skill-check results in the evidence report were never reused. Deep-tier test reports can now show a Medium gap for a missing or unexpected Migration & Deprecation section. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#526](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/526))
- **skf-test-skill:** The naive coherence check no longer blocks a component-library skill with a High missing-section gap: its `## Component Catalog` heading now satisfies the API section check. A `# Usage` or `# API` comment inside a code block no longer counts as that section. When no known heading matches, the check reads the outline of the skill's headings, so a usage or API section under another heading, such as `## Usage Examples`, is judged as that section instead of blocking the run, and the Coherence Analysis names the heading it accepted. ([#656](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/656))
- **skf-test-skill:** When Test Skill checks that a stack skill's integration patterns are complete, it now looks for the wiring evidence each entry records instead of a code example. For a stack built from code, that is `file:line` citations and key files. For a stack composed from skills, it is a `[from skill: ...]` line from each skill the pattern joins, citing a function signature of that skill or, for a skill that exports no functions such as a reference app or a docs-only skill, the export or documented contract the line quotes. Each cross-cutting pattern and each library pair counts as one pattern; the hub library summaries do not. Stack Skill never writes a code example, so before, the same stack could score 0% or full integration completeness depending on how the check was read. Integration completeness weighs 40% of a stack's coherence score, so a stack's test score can change on its next test. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#544](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/544))
- **skf-test-skill:** Test Skill now checks its report before it publishes a verdict. The check that every step ran and the check that each report section was written once now run first, so a run that fails them halts (`step-completeness-violation` or `report-anchor-missing`) before `skf-test-skill-result-<run_id>.json` and `skf-test-skill-result-latest.json` are written and before `on_complete` runs. Before, the result files and the hook came first, and the step check expected the report step's own entry, which is recorded only after it, so it halted a finished run after its verdict was out. A step that skipped its report section is now caught, because each step writes its section in place of the template's placeholder and a placeholder left behind stops the run. ([#627](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/627); issue [#585](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/585))
- **skf-update-skill:** A headless run with `--allow-degraded` on a skill that has no provenance map now does what an interactive degraded rebuild does: it captures the [MANUAL] inventory, reads the source from its own tree and records `source_commit`. It used to skip straight to change detection. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-update-skill:** An update of a skill built without a skill brief, such as a Quick Skill, no longer halts `blocked` at `detect-changes:scope-reconciliation`: it skips scope reconciliation, with the line "Scope reconciliation: skipped (no skill brief).", as it already skips the authoritative-files mirror. ([#660](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/660))
- **skf-update-skill:** Update Skill with `--dry-run` now writes nothing in every mode. Before, a repair from a test report (`--from-test-report --dry-run`) went on to rewrite the skill in place, and a skill built from documentation only skipped the dry-run exit; now both stop after re-extraction with status `dry-run`. `--detect-only` and `--dry-run` no longer amend `skill-brief.yaml` either: each decision they would record there (a promoted or skipped document, a scope change, a rescoped export) is listed in the report and in `warnings[]` as `proposed-amendment:`. A skill built from documentation only is now checked for changes: its tracked documents are fetched and compared with their recorded hashes, as Audit Skill does, only the documents that changed are fetched again, and the update records their new hashes in `metadata.json`, where before every such update reported no change. An update now records, before its first write, what it is about to change, and a stop after that point puts the skill back as it was: a repair restores the skill, its provenance map, evidence report and skill brief from the copy it took first, and any other update removes the new version folders it created, so a re-run no longer stops at "version already exists". One stop keeps the new version: a `[MANUAL]` block that changed after the update made the new version the active one, which the stop leaves active for you to repair, as its message says.
