When an update was interrupted (its run lock went stale), the next update of the skill undoes what it left and says so in `warnings[]` (`interrupted-run-cleaned`). A `[MANUAL]` section you chose to remove or edit at a merge conflict no longer stops the update when it writes: the merged `SKILL.md` is checked against the sections you approved, before the new version is published. A rescoped export's scope amendment is written to the skill brief only when the repair writes. ([#633](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/633); issue [#587](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/587))
- **skf-update-skill:** Each Update Skill run that writes puts the new version into a folder of its own, beside the previous version, which stays unchanged. Before, it rewrote the previous version's files under the new number and then failed to switch the `active` link. An update never overwrites an existing version folder: it stops with `halted-for-write-failure` (`error.phase` `merge:new-version-folder`), and the message says which folders to delete or move before you re-run. Repairs from a test report still update the current version in place. ([#517](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/517))
- **skf-update-skill:** After an update, `stats.exports_public_api` and `stats.exports_internal` in metadata.json come from the recipe runner create-skill uses, run over the skill's whole scope with no head cap, where before the update counted them by hand. They match a fresh create of the same source whenever create's runner did not stop at its head cap. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-update-skill:** Update Skill with `--from-test-report` now reads every gap of a test report whose run Test Skill's hard gate stopped (`hard-gate-blocked`), with each gap's severity, `Source:` and remediation, from the gap ledger Test Skill writes beside the report (`test-findings-<run id>.json`). Before, it read only the report's Gap Report section, which such a run left as an empty placeholder, so the Critical and High gaps that stopped the test had nothing to repair from. A report older than the ledger is still read from its Gap Report. A script now reads the gaps, which were read by eye before: it expands the folders and globs a remediation names into their source files, and refuses a path that leaves the source tree, through a link too, which the run never scans; the `halted-for-remediation-path` message lists each refused path with its reason. ([#628](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/628); issues [#546](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/546), [#583](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/583))
- **skf-update-skill:** For a skill built at Forge tier or above from a remote repository, Update Skill now reads the source at the commit its ref points to, in a private folder for that run, and never moves SKF's shared clone during `--detect-only` or `--dry-run`. Before, it read the shared clone at whatever commit another run had left it on, so it could drop exports that still existed and cite one commit's lines under another commit. Changed files come from git instead of file timestamps and sizes. When the repository cannot be reached but the pinned commit is on this machine, it compares that commit and adds `source-not-fetched` to `warnings[]` to say upstream was not checked. If the run's private folder disappears while it runs, it stops `blocked` (`error.phase` `<step>:source-tree-missing`) instead of reading every file as deleted. ([#517](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/517); issue [#511](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/511))
- **skf-update-skill:** An update now writes a re-extracted function's parameters to `provenance-map.json` in the form create-skill writes them, read from the recipe runner: `name: type`, `name?: type` for an optional JavaScript or TypeScript parameter, and ` = default` after a default. It no longer counts a function of a changed file as modified when its parameters did not change. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-update-skill:** Update Skill now recomputes the `stats` of a reference app (`scope_type` `reference-app` in `metadata.json`) the way Create Skill computes them: from the number of Pattern Surface rows in `SKILL.md`, keeping `stats.pattern_surfaces_documented`. Before, each update counted provenance citations as documented exports and dropped `stats.pattern_surfaces_documented`, the count Test Skill measures a reference app's coverage with. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#550](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/550))
- **skf-update-skill:** `--from-test-report` on a skill with no `provenance-map.json`, such as Quick Skill output, now stops `blocked` before any gate and tells you to run a normal update first. It used to fail with `halted-for-write-failure` after every gate was answered. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-update-skill:** Update Skill with `--from-test-report` now repairs a split-body consistency gap, where the `SKILL.md` body and a file under `references/` document one export differently, by editing that reference file to match the body. It knows such a gap by a `Source:` that points inside the skill. Before, it could take the gap for a changed signature and leave both files as they were, so the next Test Skill run reported the same High gap. ([#618](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/618); issue [#547](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/547))
- **skf-update-skill:** Every warning an update raises (`source-not-fetched`, `proposed-amendment:`, `target-ref-not-recorded`, `workspace-clone-not-updated` and the rest) is now recorded when it is raised, so the `SKF_UPDATE_RESULT_JSON` line and the report carry it even after a long run's context is compacted. Before, a step could keep a warning only in its working context, so a run that never reached upstream could end `no-changes` without `source-not-fetched`, and a `--dry-run` report could list no proposed skill brief amendment. ([#664](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/664))
- **skf-update-skill:** An update whose only change is to an authoritative document the skill tracks no longer reports `no-changes`: it records the document's new hash, and the report names the change. ([#654](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/654))
- **skf-update-skill:** Update Skill's workspace-drift check, which runs under `--from-test-report` and before an update moves SKF's clone to the commit it read, now reads the right repository when SKF runs from a git hook or `git rebase --exec`. Before, the git variables a hook exports could make it read another repository's `HEAD`. ([#499](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/499); issue [#498](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/498))
- **skf-verify-stack:** An interactive Verify Stack run no longer asks again for a path given with `--architecture-doc`, `--prd` or `--previous-report`: it asks only for what no flag answered. ([#640](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/640); issue [#594](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/594))
- **skf-verify-stack:** The per-run result file, `verify-stack-result-<YYYYMMDD-HHmmss>.json`, is now named after the run's own timestamp, the one in its `run_id` and in the feasibility report's file name, instead of the moment the envelope was written, so a pipeline can pair the result file with its report by name. ([#641](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/641); issue [#593](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/593))
- **skf-verify-stack, skf-refine-architecture, skf-create-stack-skill:** Verify Stack, Refine Architecture and Stack Skill in compose mode build their skill lists only from the skills SKF generated. Other skills in the folder are listed once as not SKF output and no longer add warnings or trip the exit `7` `inventory-unreliable` halt. Compose mode also accepts skills of type `single`, the type Create Skill and Quick Skill write. ([#509](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/509); issues [#504](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/504), [#506](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/506))

### Documentation

- The published docs were checked claim by claim against the code, corrected, and now describe behavior they had left out. Corrections include: `gh` is needed for Campaign and `forge-auto --pin`, not only for the Deep tier; Ferris starts with `/skf-forger` (`$skf-forger` in Codex, `/skill:skf-forger` in Pi); bmad-help and `_bmad/custom/` overrides need the BMAD Method in the project; and Quick Skill makes a best-effort draft without a source citation on each instruction. The new Python minimum is under Breaking changes. ([#522](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/522))
- The docs now explain that each provenance label follows the tool that read the export, not the forge tier: an ast-grep match is `T1` with an `[AST:]` citation, and an export read by eye is `T1-low` with an `[SRC:]` citation, at every tier, so a skill built or updated with 3.0.0 can show lower `T1` counts with nothing less extracted. The tier descriptions no longer promise `T1` and `[AST:]` for every signature at Forge and above, the docs describe Audit Skill's "Provenance label differences (not drift)" table, which lists such label changes without counting them as drift, and Verifying a Skill shows the entry 3.0.0 writes for an ast-grep match next to the published oms-cognee entry, compiled before this rule, that its line and citation checks flag. ([#643](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/643); issue [#556](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/556))
- Drop Skill's bundled `customize.toml`, its headless contract and the Customizing a Workflow docs now say that an override file must put its settings under a `[workflow]` line, as the bundled file does: a `forbid_purge_in_headless` written above that line is ignored without a warning, so the headless purge guard stays off. The examples show the two-line form, `[workflow]` then `forbid_purge_in_headless = "true"`. The docs also say that Ferris's `skf-forger` override goes under an `[agent]` line instead, as his bundled file does. Nothing changes in how a run reads the setting. ([#662](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/662))
- `docs/_internal/STABILITY.md`, the SemVer contract that ships in the npm package, now states the contract for 3.0.0: it took effect at v1.0.0, was last amended at v3.0.0, and its pinning example is `^3.0.0`. Its requirement words follow BCP 14 (RFC 2119 and RFC 8174): MUST, SHOULD, MAY and the other keywords carry that meaning only when written in capitals. Its `@internal` list now matches the release check: it no longer says that workflow exit codes, `halt_reason` and `error.phase` values or preference keys can change in any release, because each release reviews them and a new exit code or preference key needs at least a minor release. The `SKILL.md` description rule now says what `validate:skills` checks (rule `SKILL-06`): at most 1024 characters, with a `Use when` or `Use if` clause. ([#620](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/620); issue [#573](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/573))
- The prerequisites table in Getting Started now fills in Tested on for `uv`, `git`, `gh`, `ccc`, `qmd`, `tessl` and `skill-check`, which read `not recorded` before. They now read uv 0.12.15 and 0.12.23, git 2.47.3 and 2.55.0, gh 2.102.0, ccc (cocoindex-code) 0.2.41, qmd 2.8.3, tessl 0.111.0 and skill-check 1.2.0. Each version comes from a named run, which `src/shared/tool-requirements.yaml` gives. qmd's is the release candidate's own qmd commands (collection add and remove, `qmd search` and `qmd ls`) run on qmd 2.8.3 with an isolated index and without the embedding step, and before that the Deep-tier create-skill runs of a development build shortly before 3.0.0. No minimum changed, so uv, gh, ccc, qmd, tessl and skill-check still have none and never warn. ([#667](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/667); issue [#556](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/556))

## [2.1.0](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/compare/v2.0.2...v2.1.0) (2026-08-07)

### Fixed

- **The docs link guard is now tested.** `validate-docs-links.js` is a required status check, so a bug in it either blocks a legitimate PR or waves a broken link through, and nothing would have said which. It was also untestable by construction: its paths came from `__dirname`, so it could only ever inspect this repo. `--project-root`, `--docs-dir`, and `--build-dir` now override that, `run()` returns its outcome instead of calling `process.exit`, and 23 tests drive it against throwaway fixture trees. They pin the behaviour that was hand-verified once and checked by nothing since: fence and inline-code blanking (including an unterminated fence, which must blank to end of file rather than revert to prose), base inference from the built artifact rather than the environment, `src` and `srcset` resolution, absolute hrefs missing the base, site-root escapes, `--require-build` failing independently of `--strict`, and a skipped built pass never reporting an unqualified pass.

- **Docs: in-body links now work on GitHub as well as the site.** `docs/` is read in two places, and the pages had drifted into site-route form (`[agents](../agents/)`), which the browser resolves correctly on the published site but which points at nothing when the same file is rendered on GitHub. Verified: `docs/getting-started.md`'s link to `../agents/` resolved to a 404, while the real page sits at `docs/agents.md`. All 103 are now in this repo's documented convention, `[agents](/docs/agents.md)`: GitHub resolves a root-absolute link against the repository root, and `rehype-markdown-links.js` strips the `/docs/` prefix and prepends the base to produce the route. One link is deliberately excluded: the styled CTA on `docs/index.md` is raw HTML, which the rewriter never sees, so it keeps its site-relative form and carries a comment saying why. It is the only in-body link that does not also resolve on GitHub, and the alternative was breaking it on the site instead. `npm run docs:fix-links` is now a no-op against `docs/`, where before it wanted to rewrite every one of them. Also corrected a link in `docs/_internal/RELEASING.md` that was one `../` short and resolved to `docs/.github/...`.
- **`validate-doc-links.js` computed heading anchors differently from the site.** It collapsed whitespace and hyphen runs, but `github-slugger`, which both GitHub and Starlight use, replaces each space individually and never collapses. A heading like `Scenario A: Greenfield + BMM Integration` really is `scenario-a-greenfield--bmm-integration`, with the doubled hyphen the removed `+` leaves behind, so a correct anchor was reported broken. Repeated headings were also missing their `-1`, `-2` disambiguation suffixes, so only the first of `docs/examples.md`'s five `— clear session —` headings was collectable. Both now match `github-slugger`, verified across all 249 headings in `docs/`. This was latent until now: the checker only ever looked at `](/...)` targets, and the route-form links it could not see were the ones carrying the anchors.
- **Docs site: the link rewriter emitted routes that could not resolve.** `rehype-markdown-links.js` only rewrote hrefs beginning `./`, `../`, or `/`, so a bare `[x](page.md)` shipped verbatim; and for relative targets it emitted a page-relative route. Since Astro builds `format: 'directory'`, a page lives at `/getting-started/`, so `./agents/` resolved to `/getting-started/agents/` rather than `/agents/`. It now accepts bare links and emits root-absolute routes, the form the sidebar already used, which is correct at any nesting depth. A separate bug in the same path: the index collapse matched a suffix, so a page named `myindex.md` would have been truncated to `/my`.
- **Docs links are now checked against the built site, not just the source.** `validate-doc-links.js` reads Markdown, so it cannot see a link that is correct in source and wrong as a route; that class ships silently, because an href resolving nowhere is still valid HTML and neither Astro nor markdownlint fails on it. `tools/validate-docs-links.js` resolves every `href`, `src`, and `srcset` in `build/site` against the file tree, and `test:rehype` pins the rewriter's contract. Both are wired into `npm run quality`, plus a `docs-links` CI job that builds the way the deploy workflow does. The two checkers now complement each other rather than overlap: `docs:validate-links` reads `](/...)` targets and their anchors in the source, which since the link conversion above means every in-body link, while the new tool is the only thing that sees what those links become once built.


### Features

* **docs-links:** detect stale renders and check llms.txt routes ([bbb4efc](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/commit/bbb4efc36dfefdc587cd5dd32c8816309af16ebf))

### Bug Fixes

* **brief:** constrain scope.tier_a_include and document what it exempts ([0dd356a](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/commit/0dd356aff0f9e0d30065c00f8779f697c6b2b056))
* **campaign:** record the doc-rot outcome instead of re-deriving it ([31f11e2](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/commit/31f11e2fe06ffc506f5c99573e4d728da070bb20))

## [1.0.0](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/compare/v1.0.0-rc.3...v1.0.0) (2026-04-23)

### First Major Release

First major release of Skill Forge (SKF). Introduces the `INCONCLUSIVE` test verdict, atomic stack-skill writes, a shared feasibility schema across VS/RA/SS, and formalizes Linux/macOS-only support. Sets the stable contract for subsequent 1.x releases.

### Initial Release

Skill Forge (SKF) — an agent skill compiler that transforms code repositories, documentation, and developer discourse into [agentskills.io](https://agentskills.io)-compliant agent skills with AST-backed provenance.

### Highlights

- **1 agent** — Ferris (Skill Architect & Integrity Guardian) with 5 workflow-driven modes
- **14 workflows** — full lifecycle from source analysis to ecosystem-ready export, with pre-code architecture verification
- **Progressive capability tiers** — Quick (baseline), Forge (+ast-grep), Forge+ (+ccc), Deep (+gh+qmd)
- **Source-traced instructions** — every documented symbol cites an upstream file and line
- **Dual-output strategy** — active skills (SKILL.md) + passive context (context-snippet.md) in ADR-L v2 format
- **CLI installer** — `npx bmad-module-skill-forge install` with skill directory installation for 23 IDEs
- **14 knowledge fragments** — curated cross-cutting principles loaded just-in-time by workflows

### Workflows

| Trigger | Name                | Purpose                                                            |
| ------- | ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| SF      | Setup Forge         | Initialize forge environment, detect tools, set tier               |
| AN      | Analyze Source      | Discover what to skill in a large repo                             |
| BS      | Brief Skill         | Design a skill scope through guided discovery                      |
| CS      | Create Skill        | Compile a skill from brief with AST extraction                     |
| QS      | Quick Skill         | Fast skill from package name or GitHub URL                         |
| SS      | Stack Skill         | Consolidated project stack skill with integration patterns         |
| US      | Update Skill        | Regenerate a skill while preserving manual sections                |
| AS      | Audit Skill         | Drift detection between skill and current source                   |
| TS      | Test Skill          | Verify whether a skill covers its target completely and accurately |
| VS      | Verify Stack        | Pre-code stack feasibility verification against architecture       |
| RA      | Refine Architecture | Improve architecture doc using verified skill data                 |
| EX      | Export Skill        | Package for distribution, inject into CLAUDE.md                    |
| RS      | Rename Skill        | Rename a skill and update all references                           |
| DS      | Drop Skill          | Remove a skill and clean up references                             |

### Confidence Tiers

- **T1** — AST-verified signatures (Forge/Forge+/Deep)
- **T1-low** — Source reading without structural verification (Quick)
- **T2** — QMD-enriched temporal context (Deep)
- **T3** — External documentation, quarantined as untrusted

### IDE Support

23 IDEs supported: Claude Code, Cursor, Windsurf, Cline, Roo Code, GitHub Copilot, Codex, Gemini CLI, Junie, Kiro, Trae, Google Antigravity, Auggie, CodeBuddy, Crush, iFlow, KiloCoder, Ona, OpenCode, Pi, Qoder, QwenCoder, Rovo Dev

### Links

- Documentation: <https://armelhbobdad.github.io/bmad-module-skill-forge>
- npm: <https://www.npmjs.com/package/bmad-module-skill-forge>
